# Project Instructions: AM-OXO-ETL Microservice

## Role
**Senior Lead .NET Software Engineer & Software Architect**

## Methodology
- Strict Test-Driven Development (TDD - Test First)
- Clean Architecture
- SOLID Principles

---

## GLOBAL CONTEXT & SYSTEM OVERVIEW

You are tasked with building a modern, isolated, and highly maintainable micro-service designed to offload a heavy Excel data-extraction workload from a legacy application. The system will be hosted on a dedicated on-premise Windows Server 2022.

### Key Applications:
- **Legacy Application:** ASP.NET MVC 5, C#, .NET Framework 4.8. It captures raw uploaded Excel files and ingests the processed files. No business logic modifications are allowed here.
- **New Application:** A greenfield independent system built using the latest modern .NET LTS release (.NET 10). It exposes a secure ASP.NET Core Web API for synchronous Machine-to-Machine (M2M) communication and a Blazor Web App (Server Interactivity) for administration.

---

## FUNCTIONAL & DATA FLOW SPECIFICATIONS

### Ingestion & Transfer
Legacy app securely streams the `.xlsx` file synchronously over HTTP to the new .NET Web API.

### Processing
The backend extracts form-style data from heavily merged cells based on dynamic coordinates.

### Persistence
The generated structured workbook is archived on the local Windows Server filesystem. Execution is logged in the database.

### Egress
The generated workbook is returned synchronously in the HTTP response body.

### Constraints
- Input files contain no macros or encryption
- Output must be exactly 4-5 distinct `.xlsx` sheets
- CSV and ZIP formats are explicitly banned

---

## TECHNICAL STACK & CONSTRAINTS

### Core Frameworks
- ASP.NET Core Web API
- Blazor Web App (Server Interactivity model)

### Architecture
Clean Architecture (Onion Pattern). **Both the Web API and the Blazor admin app access data exclusively through the Application-layer services/repositories** — never by talking to each other over HTTP, and never by injecting `DbContext` (or any Infrastructure type) directly into a controller or a Razor component. All EF Core access lives behind repository interfaces defined in `Application` and implemented in `Infrastructure`. This is a strict rule, not a suggestion — short-circuiting the service/repository layer for convenience is exactly the kind of violation to flag rather than implement (see "Architectural Oversight" below).

### Database
- Microsoft SQL Server (Local on-premise)
- Entity Framework Core (Code-First Fluent API)

### Licensing Constraint (CRITICAL)
**100% strict adherence to free, open-source software (OSS) licenses (MIT, Apache 2.0). Commercial libraries are strictly forbidden.**
- **ClosedXML** is the mandatory library for all Excel manipulation

### Security
- Web API guarded by robust API Key validation
- Blazor admin guarded by ASP.NET Core Identity (Local SQL Server)

### Performance & HTTP
Because processing is synchronous and can take several seconds, explicit configuration of HTTP Timeouts is mandatory:
- Kestrel configuration
- Web API timeouts
- Legacy HttpClient timeouts

---

## QUALITY ASSURANCE & TDD STRATEGY

Apply a strict Test-First (Red-Green-Refactor) lifecycle:
1. Write failing unit/integration tests before implementation
2. Use xUnit/NUnit, Moq, FluentAssertions
3. Explicitly test reading values from merged range coordinates in Excel
4. Ensure all business logic is test-covered before deployment

### Repository test conventions
- Test projects live under `tests/`, one per `src/` project, mirroring its internal folder structure 1:1 (a repository in `src/ExcelETL.Infrastructure/Identity/` is tested in `tests/ExcelETL.Infrastructure.Tests/Identity/`).
- **FluentAssertions is pinned to 7.0.0** — v8+ is commercially licensed, which the OSS constraint above forbids. Always verify a NuGet package's license before adding or upgrading it.
- Repositories backed by an EF Core `DbContext` are tested against the **real EF Core InMemory provider**, not mocked. Each test class gets its own tiny `internal sealed class Test{ContextName}DbContextFactory(string databaseName) : IDbContextFactory<TContext>` colocated in the same test folder, constructed with a GUID-suffixed database name per test class for isolation — there is no shared/generic factory helper.
- Repositories that depend on ASP.NET Core Identity's `UserManager<T>`/`RoleManager<T>` (rather than an injected `IDbContextFactory<T>` directly) are tested with **Moq** instead, since those managers aren't meaningfully exercised against the InMemory provider alone.
- Assertions use FluentAssertions exclusively (`.Should().Be(...)`, `.Should().BeEquivalentTo(...)`, `.Should().ThrowAsync<T>()`, etc.) — never xUnit's `Assert.*`.

### Execution discipline per TDD step
Full detail and ticket-authoring guidance: `docs/recommandations-tickets-tdd.md`.
- Reserve deep/high-effort reasoning for the **Refactor** step — Red and Green are mechanical and don't need it.
- Run tests with minimal noise: `dotnet test --verbosity quiet` (or `--logger "console;verbosity=minimal"`), filtered to the class/test under iteration via `--filter` rather than the whole suite on every cycle.
- Prefer targeted diffs over re-reading whole files after each edit, unless the file is short or a full-picture view is actually needed.
- On an unexpected test failure (Blazor/bUnit rendering, timing, generated markup, etc.), stop and inspect the diff instead of looping uncontrolled retries — then re-run one precise, targeted pass.

### Test scope discipline (don't run the full solution by default)
Running every test project (`Domain`/`Application`/`Infrastructure`/`WebAPI`/`Hosting`/`BlazorAdmin`/`legacy`) on every small change is slow and usually pointless — most edits only touch 1-2 projects' worth of behavior. Scale the test run to the size of the change:
- **During Red/Green/Refactor iteration**: run only the specific test class/method being worked on, via `--filter`, in the one project it lives in.
- **When a ticket/lot is otherwise done**: run only the test project(s) that actually contain or exercise the changed code (e.g. a `DefaultProfileSeeder` tweak → `Infrastructure.Tests`, plus `BlazorAdmin.Tests` only if a Blazor page consumes that seed data). Don't reflexively add WebAPI/Hosting/legacy if nothing in the change touches them.
- **Full-solution test run (all projects)**: reserve this for changes that are genuinely cross-cutting (a shared migration, a `Domain`/`Application` type consumed across multiple layers) or as a final check right before a commit that closes a substantial lot — not as a default step after every edit.
- When in doubt about whether a change reached a given project, a quick `git diff --stat`/dependency check settles it faster than just running that project's tests "to be safe".

---

## I18N (ENGLISH/FRENCH) STRATEGY

The solution is being incrementally refactored to support en-US (default) and fr-FR, one Clean Architecture layer at a time (Domain → Application → Infrastructure → WebAPI → BlazorAdmin). Resource format is `.resx`.

### Scope: user-facing business errors only
Only translate messages a **user or admin actually sees** (validation failures, business-rule violations, HTTP error payloads, UI text). Do **not** touch developer-facing invariant violations — `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull`, assertion/guard-clause messages, log-only diagnostic text aimed at engineers. Those stay in plain English BCL exceptions; they are out of i18n scope entirely.

### Domain layer stays framework-free
`ExcelETL.Domain` must never reference `Microsoft.Extensions.Localization` or any other localization package — that would put a framework dependency in the innermost layer, violating Clean Architecture. Instead:
- Domain throws `DomainValidationException` / `DomainArgumentOutOfRangeException` / `DomainRuleViolationException` (in `ExcelETL.Domain/Exceptions`), each carrying a `DomainErrorCode` enum value plus the raw `Args` that were interpolated into the (English) `Message`.
- The `DomainErrorCode` member name doubles as the resource key.

### Application layer owns the resource tables
`ExcelETL.Application/Resources/` holds the `.resx` files, resolved via `IStringLocalizer<T>` marker classes (no generated designer — resolution is by naming convention, so no Visual Studio tooling is required):
- `DomainErrorMessages.resx` / `.fr.resx` — the single translation table for every `DomainErrorCode`, shared by both WebAPI and BlazorAdmin so the mapping is never duplicated between the two hosts.
- `ApplicationMessages.resx` / `.fr.resx` — Application-owned messages (service results, Data Annotations validation text via `ErrorMessageResourceType`/`ErrorMessageResourceName`).

`Microsoft.Extensions.Localization.Abstractions` (interfaces only, no ASP.NET Core coupling) is an accepted Application-layer dependency, consistent with the existing `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Abstractions` reference.

### Host wiring
- **WebAPI**: `RequestLocalizationOptions` negotiates culture from `Accept-Language` only (it's M2M — no cookie/query-string relevance). The `GlobalExceptionHandler` resolves a thrown `DomainErrorCode` + `Args` against `DomainErrorMessages` to build the localized HTTP error payload.
- **BlazorAdmin**: default provider order (query string, then culture cookie, then `Accept-Language`) so an admin's language choice persists. A circuit is long-lived, so switching language requires a real navigation — done via the `/culture/set` minimal API endpoint (`CultureEndpointRouteBuilderExtensions`), not a component re-render. **User-facing entry point (2026-07-31)**: `/profile`'s "Language" section — two toggle buttons (English/Français) linking to `/culture/set`, the current language highlighted via `CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture`. This is the only UI trigger for this mechanism — no selector on the sidebar or the Login page.

### Workflow
Work proceeds milestone by milestone (one Clean Architecture layer, or one batch of Razor components, at a time). At the end of each milestone: stop, summarize, and wait for validation plus the next milestone's files before continuing. Each milestone's deliverable includes an EN/FR resource key table.

---

## ARCHITECTURAL OVERSIGHT (CRITICAL)

The user is not a professional software architect and expects to make mistakes in architectural or strategic direction from time to time. **Proactively flag it** — before implementing — whenever a requested change, an instruction in this file, or an existing pattern in the codebase:
- Contradicts Clean Architecture, SOLID, or another established best practice
- Contradicts the architecture already in place elsewhere in this solution (e.g. one app going through repositories while another talks to EF Core directly)
- Introduces inconsistency between the Web API and the Blazor admin app's data-access patterns
- Trades away testability, layering, or maintainability for short-term convenience without the user asking for that trade-off explicitly

Say so directly, explain the concrete downside, and propose the alternative — then wait for a decision rather than silently complying or silently "fixing" it. Do not assume a past instruction (including one written in this file) is correct just because it's already written down; instructions can contain mistakes too.

## Configuration locale divergente entre développeurs/serveurs (`appsettings.Development.json`)

**Ne jamais modifier une valeur de `appsettings.Development.json` committée pour la faire correspondre à sa propre machine** si un autre développeur ou un serveur de déploiement dépend déjà de cette valeur — ce fichier est partagé via Git, donc l'écraser casse l'environnement de l'autre au prochain `git pull`/déploiement.

**Incident réel (2026-07-28)** : `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/appsettings.Development.json`'s `ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection` pointait vers `Server=localhost` (modifié par Johnny PAIN pour son serveur de test, qui a une vraie instance SQL Server locale) ; sur le poste de Simon, seul LocalDB (`(localdb)\MSSQLLocalDB`) est installé — le lancement en Debug échouait avec une `SqlException` (canaux nommés, erreur 40) au moment où le sink Serilog `MSSqlServer` tentait de créer la table `SystemLogs`.

**Convention retenue** : quand une valeur de config doit différer par poste/environnement (chaîne de connexion, clé d'API, chemin de fichier local, etc.), le fichier `appsettings.Development.json` committé garde la valeur du serveur de référence/du dernier éditeur, et chaque développeur pose sa propre surcharge via **.NET User Secrets** (jamais committés, stockés hors du repo dans `%APPDATA%\Microsoft\UserSecrets\<UserSecretsId>\secrets.json`, prioritaires sur `appsettings.{Environment}.json`) :

```bash
dotnet user-secrets set "ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection" "Server=(localdb)\MSSQLLocalDB;Database=AM-OXO-ETL-MAD-REL;Trusted_Connection=True;TrustServerCertificate=True;" --project src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin
```

`ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.csproj`/`ExcelETL.WebAPI.csproj` ont déjà un `UserSecretsId` — aucune modification de projet nécessaire pour appliquer cette convention. C'est déjà l'approche suivie pour `ApiKeyAuthentication:ApiKey` en production (valeur attendue via user secrets/variable d'environnement, jamais committée) ; à généraliser à toute valeur de config sujette à divergence entre postes/environnements.

## CURRENT SOLUTION STATE (living reference — read this before exploring the codebase; update it at the end of each milestone)

### Projects
- `src/`: `ExcelETL.Domain`, `ExcelETL.Application`, `ExcelETL.Infrastructure`, `ExcelETL.Hosting` (composition-root-only Serilog wiring shared by the two hosts below — see "Lot G3"), `ExcelETL.WebAPI`, `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin`
- `tests/`: mirrors each `src/` project 1:1 (`ExcelETL.Domain.Tests`, etc.)
- `legacy/`: `NewApiPingService`, `ExcelProcessingClientService` (+ `.Tests`) — the .NET Framework 4.8 legacy app's own HTTP client for calling the new API; the style precedent for any new M2M `HttpClient` code (multipart build, timeout translation, header setup).

### Extraction pipeline (new feature area)
- Source docs (renamed to drop date suffixes in the 2026-07-18 doc refactor -- see `docs/convention-nommage-documents.md`; each is a "living" doc, edited in place, no version history stacked in the body): `docs/spec-extraction-fichier-source-oxo.md` (feuille par feuille), `docs/modele-domaine-import-profile.md` (catalogue de primitives + modèle pivot), `docs/tickets-tdd-extraction.md` (Lots A→E, now framed as a regression/business-rule reference rather than a forward plan), `docs/glossaire-ef6-legacy-AMAR-ModelCF.md` (confirmed real `TypeElement`/`Colonne` values from the OXO database — treat this as the source of truth over any literal casing/spelling elsewhere). Test fixtures (real client `.xlsx` files): `tests/Fixtures/`.
- **v6 client clarifications (2026-07-16), landed across Domain + Application in the same session as C2/C3**: `TypeElement.Nom` is used everywhere, including for the parent Equipement (previously assumed to be `.Code`) — see the `EquipementPivot`/`ImportProfile` bullets below. `"PROLOCK VANNES"`/`"DEPROLOCK VANNES"` (ISOLEMENT) confirmed unconditional for every Isolement regardless of `TypeElement`. PLATINES' DEB/FIN variants: back to `DEBUT`-only (final). `"VANNE"` (ISOLEMENT, real D8570 fixture) confirmed absent from the OXO referential — handled by the existing non-blocking-warning policy, no dedicated rule. `ConditionalPointRuleEvaluator` comparison now trims + ignores case (see Lot B4 bullet below).
- This was originally built as a **second, separate pipeline** alongside the older `ExtractionConfig`/`CellMapping`/`SheetConfig` one — different Domain folder (`Extraction/`), deliberately not merged. **As of Lot K4, the older pipeline (and every file backing it) has been removed entirely** — this OXO pipeline is now the only one, exposed over HTTP at `POST /api/oxo/process` (see "Web API surface" below).
- `src/ExcelETL.Domain/Extraction/Primitives/`: the 5-primitive catalogue read-time model — `DirectCell`, `RepeatingBlockLocator`/`BlockFieldDefinition` (the generic repeating-block reader, covers all 6 source sheets incl. PROCEDURE's step=1 special case), the `TextTransform` hierarchy (`RawValue`/`SubstringAfter`/`Concat`+`ConcatPart`(`Literal`/`FieldRef`)), `ConditionalPointRule`/`ConditionOperator`. All `sealed record`s validating via `DomainValidationException`/`DomainArgumentOutOfRangeException`, same convention as the existing `Entities/`. `RepeatingBlockLocator` and `Concat` hold a list property (`Fields`/`Parts`) — both override `Equals`/`GetHashCode` with `SequenceEqual` because default record equality on `IReadOnlyList<T>` is reference equality, not structural; every other primitive is plain scalar fields so default record equality suffices.
- `src/ExcelETL.Domain/Extraction/Pivot/`: the extraction *output* model — `EquipementPivot` (`TypeElementNom`, **renamed from `TypeElementCode` at v6** — client confirmed source and target both use `TypeElement.Nom` throughout, never `.Code`, even for the parent Equipement; **gained a `Localisation` `init` property, Lot D prerequisite** — same "loc1" broadcast as `IsolementPivot` below, purely additive, existing 3-arg constructor unaffected), `IsolementPivot` (its `Localisation` has an `init` accessor specifically so the future `loc1` broadcast, Lot D, can do `isolement with { Localisation = ... }` on an already-built instance without re-running the other fields' validation; **only `Repere`/`TypeElementNom` are required non-blank** — `Designation` and `PositionALaPose` (added at v6, ISOLEMENT's `H20:O21` "Position MAD") are both deliberately left unvalidated: the real D8570 fixture has an ISOLEMENT row with a blank `Designation` that must still be extracted normally (§3.2's non-blocking-warning policy, not a rejection), and only ISOLEMENT has a source cell for `PositionALaPose` at all — PLATINES/ORIFICES CAPACITES/AUTRES JOINTS TOUCHES/DIVERS have none, so their `IsolementPivot`s necessarily leave it blank; a sheet-specific service may still enforce its own stricter "blank is reportable" policy before constructing the pivot, as `IsolementExtractionService` does for `PositionALaPose`), `PointPivot`, `TacheMultiplePivot` (only `Action` is required non-empty — it's PROCEDURE's stop-condition field so always populated, including for `EstFactice` placeholder rows; `Acteur`/`Risques`/`TypeTacheMultipleCode` are deliberately left unvalidated since factice rows can leave them blank per spec §1.2), `ExtractionErrorCode` (intentionally only 3 members for now — `RequiredFieldMissing`/`UnparsableValue`/`UnrecognizedTypeElement`, more will be added as Lot B/C need them, not pre-guessed), `ExtractionError`, `ImportResult` (a plain `sealed class`, not a record — **a second deliberate exception to the project's "no generic Result pattern" rule**, alongside `IdentityOperationResult`; needed because per-block extraction errors accumulate while processing continues, which doesn't fit "throw and stop"). `ImportResult`'s constructor does *not* enforce "Equipement null ⇒ other collections empty" (the whole-file-rejection invariant from the model doc §3.1) — that's tested at the orchestrator level instead (Lot D, via mock verification that the other 5 sheet services are never called), not baked into the Domain constructor. A **second, purpose-built DTO for isolement-style sheets' partial results**, `IsolementSheetExtractionResult` (`Isolements`/`Points`/`Errors`, no `Equipement`), lives in Application (`Extraction/Oxo/`) rather than reusing `ImportResult` — see the Lot C2 bullet below for why.
- `src/ExcelETL.Domain/Extraction/Profile/`: `ImportProfile` (aggregate root, extends `Entity`, has `Id`) and `SheetExtractionRule` (plain class, no `Id` — a configuration value owned by its `ImportProfile`, not an entity in its own right). `ImportProfile.ReperePrefix` defaults to `"MAD-OXO-"` (`ImportProfile.DefaultReperePrefix`) via a constructor overload, per the spec's "paramétrable, défaut MAD-OXO-". `ImportProfile.EquipementTypeElementNom` (**added at v6**, no default overload — the client corrected the earlier `"MAD"` assumption to `"MAD TRAVAUX"` for a MAD dossier, confirmed in the OXO database, and this value is profile-specific, never a service constant) carries the Equipement parent's `TypeElement.Nom`; required non-blank like `ReperePrefix`. `SheetExtractionRule` validates its `SheetName` matches its `Locator.Sheet` (`DomainRuleViolationException`) — a consistency check beyond what the ticket explicitly asked for, added because the two are otherwise redundant data that could silently diverge. `SheetExtractionRule.PointRules` may legitimately be empty (means "always create the Point", not an error) — only `ImportProfile.SheetRules` itself is required non-empty (at least one rule). **`SheetExtractionRule.UnconditionalColonneNames`** (**added at v6, resolves the previously-open PointRules-grouping question** — see Lot C2 bullet below) is a second, independent list of Colonne names created for every extracted row with no condition at all (e.g. ISOLEMENT's `"PROLOCK VANNES"`/`"DEPROLOCK VANNES"`) — `ConditionalPointRule` has no way to represent "no condition" (it always carries a real `SourceFieldName`/`ComparisonValue`), so this couldn't be folded into `PointRules` itself. Also required non-null, empty is valid (e.g. DIVERS, Lot C6, where every Colonne is conditional).
- Persistence (EF Core `ImportProfile`) is deliberately deferred — Lot A-D are being validated against a hardcoded in-memory profile first, per the ticket doc's proposed sequencing. Don't add an `ExtractionProfileConfiguration`/repository until that's explicitly requested.
- **Lot B (Application-layer generic engine, done)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/` — a **new subfolder**, deliberately not flat in the pre-existing `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/` (that's the old `ExtractionConfig` pipeline; putting new OXO code there would collide both on disk and in namespace — see `docs/etat-des-lieux-technique.md`'s "deux dossiers `Extraction/`" note). `IWorkbookReader`: `ReadCellValue(sheet, range)` / `SheetExists(sheet)`. `TextTransformEvaluator`/`RepeatingBlockReader`/`ConditionalPointRuleEvaluator` are all stateless, tested against `Mock<IWorkbookReader>` or plain dictionaries — no real file I/O in their own tests (that's Lot E1 + the per-sheet integration tests below). `RepeatingBlockReader`'s range math (column split + row offset) is factored into a standalone static `BlockFieldRangeCalculator`, added while building Lot C1 so `ProcedureExtractionService` could reuse the same math without going through `RepeatingBlockReader`'s required-field policy (see Lot C1 note below) or duplicating it.
  - None of these three evaluators return an `ExtractionError` directly — they return a plain `(Value, ErrorMessage)`/`(ShouldCreatePoint, WarningMessage)` tuple (just a 2-tuple, not a new named Result type) because they have no `Sheet`/`BlockIdentifier` context to build one with. Wrapping a message into a real `ExtractionError` is the caller's job (Lot C, which does have that context) — don't push `ExtractionError` construction down into these evaluators later without a reason.
  - `RepeatingBlockReader` reads a block's `StopFieldName` cell *first*, before any other field, and bails immediately if it's blank — so a block that fails the stop check is never misreported as "partially empty"; it's simply never read beyond its stop field. A block whose stop field is populated but another field is blank produces one `ExtractionError` (`RequiredFieldMissing`) and is skipped, and reading continues on the next block.
  - `UnknownFieldReferenceException` (new `ApplicationErrorCode` member, follows the existing per-exception `IHasApplicationErrorCode` pattern — there is no shared `ApplicationValidationException` base class in this codebase, despite the ticket doc's conventions section implying one) is thrown — not returned as an `ExtractionError` — whenever a `Concat`'s `FieldRef` or a `ConditionalPointRule`'s `SourceFieldName` names a field that isn't in the already-extracted-fields dictionary. That's treated as a profile/configuration bug (the `SheetExtractionRule` doesn't line up with itself), not per-row file data noise.
  - `RepeatingBlockReadResult` (`Blocks`/`Errors`) is a plain DTO, matching the pre-existing `ExtractionResult`'s precedent — not a third exception to the "no generic Result pattern" rule (that count stays at 2: `IdentityOperationResult`, `ImportResult`).
  - **`ConditionalPointRuleEvaluator`'s comparison trims and ignores case** (fixed post-v6, per spec §7): real fixtures have trailing spaces (`"SOUPAPE "`) and casing variance vs. the confirmed base values. A genuine spelling difference (`"POINT DE FEU"` vs `"POINT FEU"`) is **not** normalized away by this and remains a legitimate non-match, covered by the existing non-blocking-warning path.
- **Lot E1 (`ClosedXmlWorkbookReader`, done ahead of its ticket slot)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.Infrastructure/Excel/`, the first real `IWorkbookReader` implementation. Built alongside Lot C1 rather than deferred, because the Lot C ticket requires each per-sheet service to be tested against the 3 real fixtures, which needs a working reader to open them. Opens the `XLWorkbook` once per instance and owns its disposal (`IDisposable`, not part of `IWorkbookReader` itself — Lot B/C consumers never need to know). Reads a range's top-left cell via `GetString()`, returning `null` for blank (mirrors what the Mock-based tests already assume). Reuses the pre-existing `WorksheetNotFoundInWorkbookException` (from the old `ExtractionConfig` pipeline, `ExcelETL.Application.Extraction` namespace) for an unknown sheet, rather than declaring a near-duplicate. Its own tests use small in-memory workbooks built with ClosedXML directly (merged range, blank cell, missing sheet) — real-fixture coverage comes from the per-sheet integration tests instead (see below).
- **Lot C1 (`ProcedureExtractionService`, done)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/Procedure/`. Reads PROCEDURE's header (`M2:O2` repère, `P2:Q2` numéro révision, `R2:T2` date révision — all 3 ranges hardcoded in the service, not part of `SheetExtractionRule`, since the spec only calls the repère *prefix* profile-configurable, not the header cell coordinates themselves) into `EquipementPivot`, plus its `TacheMultiple` repeating block (`Step=1`, stop field `Action`/`C:L`) into `TacheMultiplePivot`s, and creates 2 unconditional `PointPivot`s ("TRAVAUX COMPLET"/"TRAVAUX DETAIL") for the Equipement per spec §1.3 — no `ConditionalPointRuleEvaluator` involved, these aren't conditioned on anything.
  - **Deliberately does not use `IRepeatingBlockReader`** for the TacheMultiple block, despite `RepeatingBlockLocator`/`Step=1` otherwise fitting: every field in a TacheMultiple row *except* `Action` is optional at the pivot level, not just `Ordre` (the documented "ligne de mise en page" → `EstFactice` rule, spec §1.2) but `Acteur`/`Risques`/`TypeTacheMultipleCode`/`DateValidation` too (`TacheMultiplePivot` itself leaves them unvalidated — confirmed against the real fixtures, e.g. C7401 has section-header rows with blank `Ordre` scattered well past row 9, and populated `Acteur`("ADF")/`Risques` values are the exception, not the rule). `RepeatingBlockReader`'s shared policy (any non-stop field blank ⇒ `ExtractionError`, block dropped) would wrongly reject/drop valid rows, so `ProcedureExtractionService` walks the block itself, calling `BlockFieldRangeCalculator.BuildRange` directly for the range math only. Field names read from `sheetRule.Locator.Fields` by name (constants in `ProcedureFieldNames`) so the field list stays profile-configurable exactly like every other sheet's locator.
  - **Whole-file rejection** (model doc §3.1): if `M2:O2` is blank, doesn't start with the configured repère prefix, or `R2:T2` is blank/unparsable, `Extract` returns immediately with `Equipement = null` and exactly one blocking `ExtractionError` — the TacheMultiple block is never read in that case (verified by `Mock.Verify(..., Times.Never)` in the unit tests).
  - **Date handling**: `R2:T2`/`T:U` cells are parsed via `DateTime.TryParseExact` (`"dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss"` / `"dd/MM/yyyy"`, invariant culture) into `DateOnly`, then reformatted as `dd/MM/yyyy` for `Designation` — deliberately **not** a raw pass-through of `IWorkbookReader.ReadCellValue`'s string, because probing the real C7401 fixture showed ClosedXML's `GetString()` on a date cell returns `"12/12/2025 00:00:00"` (a full datetime, locale/number-format dependent), not a clean `dd/mm/yyyy` string — the spec's format requirement is enforced in code, not assumed from the source file's display formatting.
  - **`Extract` takes `equipementTypeElementNom` as a parameter** (reworked post-v6; originally hardcoded to the literal `"MAD"`). The client confirmed this value (`"MAD TRAVAUX"` for a MAD dossier) is profile-specific (`ImportProfile.EquipementTypeElementNom`), never a service constant — an explicit test (`Extract_UsesEquipementTypeElementNomFromProfile_NotAHardcodedConstant`) asserts the service passes through whatever value it's given, as an architecture guard-rail per the ticket's own requirement.
  - Tested against `Mock<IWorkbookReader>` (`ProcedureExtractionServiceTests`, Application.Tests) and, per the Lot C ticket's requirement, against all 3 real fixtures via the real `ClosedXmlWorkbookReader` (`ProcedureExtractionServiceIntegrationTests`, **Infrastructure.Tests**, not Application.Tests — it needs both the Application service and the Infrastructure reader, and Infrastructure already references Application, so putting it there avoids adding a new Application.Tests → Infrastructure reference).
- **Lot C2 (`IsolementExtractionService`, done)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/Isolement/`. Reads ISOLEMENT's block (`Step=7`, stop field `Identification`/`B:E`), composing each Isolement's `Repere` as `{K6:T6}-{Identification}` — `K6:T6` read directly off the ISOLEMENT sheet itself, **not** the PROCEDURE-derived `Equipement.Repere` passed in as a parameter: confirmed against all 3 real fixtures that ISOLEMENT's `K6:T6` is a shorter, different value (e.g. `"C7401"` vs PROCEDURE's composed `"38-C7401"`) — the spec calls for composing from `K6:T6` specifically, so the service is fully self-contained, no cross-sheet context needed. Returns `IsolementSheetExtractionResult`, not `ImportResult` — reusing `ImportResult` here would blur its null-`Equipement` = whole-file-rejection meaning, since isolement-style sheets never produce an `Equipement` at all (a non-rejection reason for null would be indistinguishable from the real rejection case at Lot D's merge step).
  - **Resolves the `PointRules`-grouping question**: creates a `PointPivot` for every name in `sheetRule.UnconditionalColonneNames` (`"PROLOCK VANNES"`/`"DEPROLOCK VANNES"`, unconditionally, regardless of `TypeElement`) **plus** groups `sheetRule.PointRules` by `ColonneName` and evaluates via `ConditionalPointGroupEvaluator` (see below) — ISOLEMENT has exactly one conditional group, the `"ZÉRO ENERGIE..."` Colonne.
  - **Deliberately does not use `IRepeatingBlockReader`**, for a narrower reason than PROCEDURE: the real D8570 fixture has a row (Identification `"V4"`, TypeElement `"VANNE"`, confirmed absent from the OXO referential) with a blank `Designation` cell that must still be extracted normally (§3.2's non-blocking-warning policy, not a rejection) — so `Designation` is read leniently while `Identification`/`TypeElement`/`PositionALaPose` stay required (blank ⇒ `RequiredFieldMissing`, block skipped), a mixed policy `RepeatingBlockReader`'s uniform strictness can't express. Walks the block itself via the shared `BlockFieldRangeCalculator`.
  - **Every non-`"ZERO ENERGIE"` isolement legitimately produces an `UnrecognizedTypeElement` warning** in the 3 real fixtures (none contain a `ZERO ENERGIE`-typed row in this sheet) — not a bug, expected given ISOLEMENT has only one conditional Colonne (see `ConditionalPointGroupEvaluator` below).
  - Verified against all 3 real fixtures: 8/15/3 Isolements for C7401/D8570/G6306B; D8570's `"VANNE"` row is asserted explicitly per the ticket's requirement (extracted normally, blank `Designation`, the 2 unconditional Points still created, one warning, no `"ZERO ENERGIE"` Point).
- **`ConditionalPointGroupEvaluator`** (`Extraction/Oxo/`, static helper, extracted while starting Lot C6) evaluates every conditional Colonne-group for one isolement and **aggregates the warning across the whole sheet**: a warning fires only when *none* of the groups matched, not whenever any single group's own condition fails to match. For a sheet with one conditional group (ISOLEMENT, AUTRES JOINTS TOUCHES) this is behaviorally identical to evaluating that group alone; it's required correctness for DIVERS (multiple mutually-exclusive conditional types) — without it a SOUPAPE isolement there would incorrectly also warn for not matching INSTRUMENTATION's/POINT FEU's groups. `IsolementExtractionService` and `AutresJointsTouchesExtractionService` were both retrofitted to call it (no behavior change, their tests passed unmodified) so all Lot C conditional-Point sheets share one aggregation rule rather than three copies.
- **Lot C3/C4 (`UnconditionalIsolementSheetExtractionService`, done)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/` (flat, not a `Platines/`-scoped folder — **renamed from `PlatinesExtractionService`** once building C4 confirmed ORIFICES CAPACITES' cell ranges/step/offsets are byte-identical to PLATINES per the spec: same `K6:U6` repere echo, `Step=8`, `B17:E18`/`H16:V17`/`B20:E22`). Every field is genuinely required (confirmed against all 3 real fixtures, no blanks observed) and every Colonne is unconditional for both sheets, so this delegates the block walk to the shared `IRepeatingBlockReader` and needs no `IConditionalPointRuleEvaluator` dependency at all — configured purely via `SheetExtractionRule` (sheet name differs, Colonne list differs: 7 for PLATINES — the `FIN` variants stay deliberately excluded per spec, no test covers them as nominal — vs. 4 for ORIFICES CAPACITES). Reuses `IsolementFieldNames` (`Identification`/`Designation`/`TypeElement`) rather than a sheet-specific field-names class, since they're identical across sheets. Verified against all 3 real fixtures: PLATINES 15/21/5 Isolements for C7401/D8570/G6306B, no errors; ORIFICES CAPACITES 0/5/2 (C7401's sheet is present but entirely empty for that dossier — a legitimate zero-isolement result, not an error), all `"TROU D'HOMME"`, no errors.
- **Lot C5 (`AutresJointsTouchesExtractionService`, done)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/AutresJointsTouches/`. Reads its block (`Step=7`), 2 unconditional Colonnes plus 1 conditional (`"POSE ÉTIQUETTES"` unless `TypeElement = "TUBING"`) via the same `UnconditionalColonneNames` + `ConditionalPointGroupEvaluator` pattern as ISOLEMENT. Every field is genuinely required (confirmed against all 3 real fixtures), so — unlike ISOLEMENT — this delegates the block walk to the shared `IRepeatingBlockReader`. **Discovered while probing the real fixtures**: the Equipement repere echo does **not** live at `"K6:U6"` as the spec documents for this sheet — that cell is blank in all 3 files; the actual value sits at a plain, unmerged `N6` instead. Hardcoded as sheet-specific business knowledge (like PROCEDURE's header ranges), not trusted from the spec's stated range. Verified against all 3 real fixtures: C7401's sheet is empty (0 Isolements, same "unused sheet" pattern as C4); D8570 has 13 `TUYAUTERIE` (all get `"POSE ÉTIQUETTES"`); G6306B has 2 `TUYAUTERIE` + 2 `TUBING` (the latter correctly excluded, each producing one non-blocking warning).
- **Lot C6 (`DiversExtractionService`, done — completes Lot C)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/Divers/`. Reads its block (`Step=3`), every Colonne conditional across 4 mutually-exclusive `TypeElement` values (`INSTRUMENTATION`×1/`ZERO ENERGIE`×1/`SOUPAPE`×2/`POINT FEU`×3 — 7 `ConditionalPointRule`s, zero unconditional) via `ConditionalPointGroupEvaluator` — **the sheet that aggregation fix exists for**. Every field required, uses the shared `IRepeatingBlockReader`. Also reads **`loc1`** (`B6:E6`, raw value, no transformation per spec §6) into a dedicated `DiversSheetExtractionResult` (not the shared `IsolementSheetExtractionResult` — `Loc1` is meaningless for the other 4 isolement-style sheets) for Lot D's orchestrator to broadcast onto the Equipement and every Isolement of the whole run; this service does **not** apply it to its own Isolements. Same `N6` repere-echo discrepancy as C5. **Verified directly against the real G6306B fixture**, which happens to cover all 4 `TypeElement` values in a single 4-row sheet, including the client-confirmed `"POINT DE FEU"` (real cell) vs. `"POINT FEU"` (confirmed base value) spelling mismatch — produces zero PF Points and **exactly one** aggregate warning for that Isolement, proving the aggregation fix end-to-end against real data, not just a hand-built unit test. D8570 (13× `ZERO ENERGIE`, all matching) and C7401 (0 rows, but `loc1` = `"ZONE 1"` still read from the header) round out the 3-fixture coverage.
- **Lot C complete.**
- **Lot D (`ImportPipelineOrchestrator`, done — pipeline complete)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/`. Runs PROCEDURE first; if its `Equipement` is `null` (whole-file rejection, model doc §3.1), returns that same `ImportResult` immediately — verified by `Mock.Verify(..., Times.Never)` that the other 5 services are never even invoked, not just that their output is discarded. Otherwise runs ISOLEMENT/PLATINES/ORIFICES CAPACITES/AUTRES JOINTS TOUCHES/DIVERS, aggregates every `IsolementPivot`/`PointPivot`/`ExtractionError` (`TachesMultiples` come only from PROCEDURE), and broadcasts DIVERS's `loc1` onto the Equipement and every Isolement of the run via `with { Localisation = loc1 }` — applied unconditionally ("sans exception" per spec §1.5), even when `loc1` is blank (a no-op against the default-empty `Localisation`).
  - **Sheet roles are resolved from `ImportProfile.SheetRules` by matching `SheetName` against 6 fixed literal names** (`PROCEDURE`/`ISOLEMENT`/`PLATINES`/`ORIFICES CAPACITES`/`AUTRES JOINTS TOUCHES`/`DIVERS`), not a role enum — `SheetExtractionRule` has no explicit "role" tag independent of its configured name, and all 3 real fixtures plus the ticket's planned hardcoded profile use exactly these tab names. Revisit only if a client profile ever needs to rename a tab while keeping the same logical role; nothing today requires it.
  - `PLATINES` and `ORIFICES CAPACITES` both resolve to `IUnconditionalIsolementSheetExtractionService` (the shared C3/C4 service), called twice with two different `SheetExtractionRule`s — a unit test (`Times.Exactly(2)`) guards against accidentally collapsing them into one call.
  - **Lot D2 integration tests** (`ImportPipelineOrchestratorIntegrationTests`, Infrastructure.Tests) run the real C1-C6 services + `ClosedXmlWorkbookReader` against all 3 real fixtures with a hardcoded `ImportProfile` assembling every sheet's already-validated cell ranges — also the regression guard-rail the ticket calls for going forward. Isolement totals per file are the sum of each sheet's independently-confirmed count and matched on the first run: C7401 23 (8+15+0+0+0), D8570 67 (15+21+5+13+13, including the `"VANNE"` isolement), G6306B 18 (3+5+2+4+4). Assertions favor "no `RequiredFieldMissing` anywhere" and "`loc1` present on every Isolement" over hand-computed total Point/warning counts, which would be fragile arithmetic for no real benefit — the ticket only asks for isolement counts, known values, and absence of blocking errors.
- **Pipeline complete (Lot A→D).**
- **Lot E2 (EF Core persistence of `ImportProfile`, done)**: `IImportProfileStore` lives in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/` (not the flat `Extraction/` folder `IExtractionConfigRepository` uses — deliberately deviated from the ticket's literal instruction to keep OXO Application code consistently under `Extraction/Oxo/`, per the same disk/namespace-collision rationale as Lot B/C/D; confirmed with the user before implementing). `EfImportProfileStore` (`src/ExcelETL.Infrastructure/Persistence/Repositories/`) follows `ExtractionConfigRepository`'s short-lived-DbContext-per-method pattern. `DeleteAsync`/unknown-id `GetByIdAsync` are no-ops/`null`, not exceptions (no ticket requirement either way, chosen for symmetry with simple CRUD).
  - **`SaveAsync` is a true upsert, keyed by `profile.Id`** — editing an existing profile so it matches a client's source Excel layout is this ETL's core workflow ("on ne recrée pas un nouveau profil à chaque fois"), confirmed by the user after an initial (wrong) "insert-only, revisit later" implementation. `Entity` (`Domain/Common/`) gained a second constructor, `protected Entity(Guid id)` (guards `id == Guid.Empty` with a plain `ArgumentException` — a developer-invariant check, not a user-facing business error, so no `DomainErrorCode`/localization per the i18n-scope rule above), alongside the existing parameterless one that mints a fresh `Guid.NewGuid()`. `ImportProfile` gained a matching 5-arg constructor (`Guid id, name, reperePrefix, equipementTypeElementNom, sheetRules`) that the two "create new" constructor overloads now funnel into (avoids tripling the validation logic) — this is how an edited profile is reconstructed under its original Id and handed back to `SaveAsync`. `SheetExtractionRule` (and everything nested under it) still has no identity of its own by design, so there's no meaningful old-vs-new nested-row diff to compute on update: `EfImportProfileStore.SaveAsync` removes the existing row's whole owned graph and inserts the new one under the same Id, as **two separate `SaveChangesAsync` calls** — tracking a `Removed` and an `Added` instance under the same key at the same time throws in EF Core's change tracker (confirmed empirically), so the delete must commit before the insert is attempted.
  - **EF mapping is owned types, not sibling entities** (unlike `ExtractionConfig`/`SheetConfig`/`CellMapping`, which are full `Entity`s in their own tables): `SheetExtractionRule` (`OwnsMany`, own table `ImportProfileSheetRules` + shadow `int Id` key, since it has no natural key), nested `RepeatingBlockLocator` (`OwnsOne`, table-split into the same row) whose own `Fields` is a second-level `OwnsMany` (`ImportProfileSheetRuleBlockFields`), and `PointRules` (`OwnsMany`, `ImportProfileSheetRulePointRules`) — both keyed the same shadow-`int Id` way. `UnconditionalColonneNames` (`IReadOnlyList<string>`) is a primitive collection, not an owned entity — EF Core 10 maps it to a `nvarchar(max)` JSON column on SqlServer, no extra configuration needed. All in `ImportProfileConfiguration.cs`.
  - **Required a Domain-layer change beyond the ticket's Infrastructure-only scope, discovered empirically**: EF Core's constructor-binding materialization cannot bind a constructor parameter to an entity-collection navigation at all ("Navigations to related entities... cannot be bound", confirmed by the actual EF exception) — so `RepeatingBlockLocator.Fields`, `SheetExtractionRule.PointRules`, and `ImportProfile.SheetRules` (all previously plain get-only auto-properties assigned straight from the constructor's `IReadOnlyList<T>` parameter) were changed to a private mutable `List<T>` backing field + a private parameterless constructor, exactly mirroring `ExtractionConfig._sheets`/`SheetConfig._cellMappings`'s existing pattern minus a public `AddX()` method (these three types are meant to stay fully immutable post-construction from any *caller's* perspective — the private ctor exists purely for EF's reflection-based materialization to overwrite immediately afterward, same trick `Entity.Id` already relies on). Scalar auto-properties (`Name`, `Sheet`, `SourceFieldName`, etc.) needed no change — EF's default backing-field convention already handles those. `UnconditionalColonneNames`/`ConditionalPointRule`/`BlockFieldDefinition` needed no change either (no entity-collection param in their constructors).
  - Tests: `EfImportProfileStoreTests` (Infrastructure.Tests, real EF Core InMemory provider, `TestDbContextFactory` reused from the existing `Persistence/` folder) cover full nested round-trip (Locator/Fields/PointRules/UnconditionalColonneNames), a dedicated `EquipementTypeElementNom` regression guard (the field this ticket was originally written to protect), multiple sheet rules persisting independently (no FK/data bleed across owned collections), `GetAllAsync` ordering, and not-found/no-op paths for `GetByIdAsync`/`DeleteAsync`.
  - Migration `20260717113850_AddImportProfile` adds `ImportProfiles`, `ImportProfileSheetRules`, `ImportProfileSheetRuleBlockFields`, `ImportProfileSheetRulePointRules` (all FK cascade-delete to their owner) — not yet applied to any real SQL Server database.
- **Lot F1.1 (`ImportProfiles.razor` list page, done)** lives in `src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Components/Pages/Admin/`, following `Mappings.razor`'s direct-repository-injection pattern (`IImportProfileStore`, not `DbContext`). Route is `/import-profiles` (**deliberately not** the ticket's literal `/admin/profils-import`/`/nouveau` — every sibling admin page uses a flat, single-English-word route segment despite living under the `Admin/` folder on disk, so this stays consistent with that existing convention rather than the ticket's suggested path; only the UI labels are localized, not the URL). "Créer" (`create-profile-button`) navigates to `/import-profiles/new` — a placeholder route with no page behind it yet (that's F1.2, the construction form) — rather than an inline create form, because unlike `ExtractionConfig` (name-only to create), `ImportProfile`'s constructor requires a non-empty `SheetRules` list, so there's no valid "create with just a name" flow to offer inline. "Dupliquer" (`duplicate-profile-button-{id}`) builds a new `ImportProfile` via the 4-arg (name, reperePrefix, equipementTypeElementNom, sheetRules) constructor — mints a fresh `Guid` automatically — with the name suffixed by a localized `ImportProfiles_DuplicateSuffix` string (`"(Copy)"`/`"(Copie)"`), then calls `SaveAsync` (insert, new Id) and reloads the list; no server navigation. `IImportProfileStore`/`EfImportProfileStore` newly registered `AddScoped` in `Program.cs` (shares the existing `IDbContextFactory<ExcelEtlDbContext>` registration — no new DbContext needed). Nav link added to `NavMenu.razor`/`.razor.css` (`bi-collection` icon) inside the existing admin-only `AuthorizeView` block alongside Users/Upload Test. Tests (`ImportProfilesTests.cs`) follow `MappingsTests.cs`'s real-EF-InMemory-provider convention, not a mock. Only a list page — no create/edit form yet; `/import-profiles/new` is an intentional dead link until F1.2.
- **Lot F1.2/F1.3 (`ImportProfileEditor.razor` construction form, done)** lives alongside `ImportProfiles.razor`, at route `/import-profiles/new` (the placeholder route F1.1's "Créer" button already linked to). One page, not incremental persistence: unlike `ExtractionConfig` (Mappings.razor), `ImportProfile` has no in-place mutation API -- it's built once via its constructor from a complete `SheetRules` list -- so this page accumulates root fields (`Name`/`ReperePrefix`/`EquipementTypeElementNom`) and a locally-held `List<SheetExtractionRule>` in component state, only calling `IImportProfileStore.SaveAsync` once, on a final "Save profile" click. `ReperePrefix` is prefilled with `ImportProfile.DefaultReperePrefix`; `EquipementTypeElementNom` deliberately has **no** default (client-confirmed 2026-07-17: no hardcoded "MAD TRAVAUX" suggestion in the component, echoing the exact anti-pattern already fixed once in `ProcedureExtractionService`, Lot C1).
  - "Add sheet rule" is its own nested mini-form: a repeating-block locator (`SheetName`/`FirstBlockStartRow`/`Step`/`StopFieldName`) plus three sub-lists built up field-by-field (`BlockFieldDefinition`, `UnconditionalColonneNames` as plain strings, `ConditionalPointRule`), each held in a `NewSheetRuleModel`/`NewBlockFieldModel`/`NewPointRuleModel` local class (mirrors `Mappings.razor`'s `NewSheetModel`/`NewMappingModel` convention). Clicking "Add sheet rule" constructs a real `RepeatingBlockLocator` then `SheetExtractionRule` -- validation (e.g. `Step > 0`) is enforced by the Domain constructors themselves, not duplicated client-side; a thrown `DomainValidationException`/`DomainArgumentOutOfRangeException`/`DomainRuleViolationException` is caught and localized via `BusinessExceptionLocalizer`, and the pending sub-form is deliberately **not** cleared on error (so the user can fix just the offending field) -- only reset on success.
  - **Discovered gap, fixed as part of this work**: `DomainErrorMessages.resx`/`.fr.resx` had zero entries for any of the 27 `DomainErrorCode` members the OXO extraction Domain layer (`Primitives`/`Pivot`/`Profile`) added -- the i18n milestones only ever covered the original `ExtractionConfig`/`SheetConfig`/`CellMapping`/`ExtractionHistory` surface. Added the 17 entries this form can actually throw (`ImportProfile_*`, `SheetExtractionRule_*`, `RepeatingBlockLocator_*`, `BlockFieldDefinition_*`, `ConditionalPointRule_*`); the remaining 10 (`DirectCell_*`, `SubstringAfter_*`, `Concat_*`, `FieldRef_*`, `EquipementPivot_*`, `IsolementPivot_*`, `PointPivot_*`, `TacheMultiplePivot_*`, `ExtractionError_*`) are still missing -- none are reachable from any host yet (the OXO pipeline isn't wired to a host), so left alone rather than speculatively translated; revisit when F2 or a future host surfaces them to a user.
  - Tests (`ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`) cover root-field defaults, the three empty-required-field validation errors (Name/ReperePrefix/EquipementTypeElementNom, each verified to reach the domain constructor's actual check order rather than a client-side duplicate), the `ImportProfile_NoSheetRules` case, a full add-sheet-rule happy path asserting the nested `Locator`/`Fields`/`UnconditionalColonneNames`/`PointRules` round-trip, the `Step <= 0` Domain-invariant mirror, and an end-to-end save-and-navigate-back-to-list test.
  - Not yet built: editing an existing profile (`/import-profiles/{id}` -- no ticket for this yet, F1.1's list page has no "Edit" action either).
- **Lot F2 (`ImportProfileTest.razor`, done -- Lot F complete)** lives alongside the other `ImportProfiles*` pages, at route `/import-profiles/test`. Picks a persisted `ImportProfile` (dropdown, `IImportProfileStore`) then an uploaded `.xlsx` (`InputFile`) is run **in process**: `new ClosedXmlWorkbookReader(fileStream)` (Infrastructure) straight into `IImportPipelineOrchestrator.Run(...)` (Lot D) -- no HTTP round trip to the Web API at all, unlike `UploadTest.razor`/`ExcelProcessingClient`, which this page deliberately does not reuse (ticket's explicit instruction; the two pages now demonstrate both integration styles side by side under `Components/Pages/Admin/`).
  - **First host to ever need the 6 per-sheet extraction services + `ImportPipelineOrchestrator` wired via DI** -- newly registered `AddSingleton` in `Program.cs` (`ITextTransformEvaluator`, `IConditionalPointRuleEvaluator`, `IRepeatingBlockReader`, `IProcedureExtractionService`, `IIsolementExtractionService`, `IUnconditionalIsolementSheetExtractionService`, `IAutresJointsTouchesExtractionService`, `IDiversExtractionService`, `IImportPipelineOrchestrator`) -- all stateless, so Singleton; WebAPI still only exposes the older `ExtractionConfig` pipeline and has none of these.
  - **Blocking vs. non-blocking is read directly off `ImportResult`, not a separate flag**: `Equipement is null` means the whole file was rejected (model doc §3.1) -- rendered as a single "File rejected" alert listing `Errors` -- otherwise every entry in `Errors` is a non-blocking warning (e.g. D8570's `"VANNE"` -> `UnrecognizedTypeElement`), rendered in its own "Non-blocking warnings" table alongside the successfully-extracted `Equipement`/`Isolements`/`Points`/`TachesMultiples` read-only tables. No separate blocking/non-blocking classification exists anywhere in Domain -- the orchestrator's contract already implies it.

Also covers the upload-mechanics gate (no profile selected -> error, no processing attempted) and a synthetic minimal rejected workbook (blank PROCEDURE header, built inline with ClosedXML) for the blocking-rejection path, since building an intentionally-invalid real fixture wasn't necessary just to hit that one check.
