- **Lot G1 (`ILogger` instrumentation of the OXO pipeline, done)**: the audit-flagged gap ("`Extraction/Oxo/` has zero logging") is now closed for the extraction path. `ImportPipelineOrchestrator` takes `ILogger<ImportPipelineOrchestrator>`: `LogInformation` at the start and successful end of a `Run` (profile name, then isolement/point/error counts), `LogWarning` when PROCEDURE rejects the whole file, and the entire method body (after the `ArgumentNullException` guard clauses) is wrapped in `try`/`LogError`/`rethrow` for unexpected exceptions (e.g. `FindRule`'s `.First()` throwing if a profile is missing a sheet rule) — mirrors `ProcessExcelFileService`'s existing try/log/rethrow shape, the only prior precedent in the codebase. All 5 sheet services (`ProcedureExtractionService`, `IsolementExtractionService`, `UnconditionalIsolementSheetExtractionService`, `AutresJointsTouchesExtractionService`, `DiversExtractionService`) take their own `ILogger<T>` and log every `ExtractionError` they produce (including ones surfaced from `IRepeatingBlockReader.Read(...).Errors`, which the reader itself still doesn't log -- Lot B stays untouched, sheet-level services are where the sheet/business context lives) through a new shared static helper, `ExtractionErrorLogging.Log(ILogger, ExtractionError)` (`Extraction/Oxo/ExtractionErrorLogging.cs`): `ExtractionErrorCode.UnrecognizedTypeElement` (the explicitly non-blocking case, model doc §3.2) logs at `Warning`; `RequiredFieldMissing`/`UnparsableValue` (a block, or for PROCEDURE the whole file, being skipped/rejected) log at `Error`.
  - **Test convention decision (2026-07-18, user-confirmed)**: the ticket that requested this lot assumed `Mock<ILogger<T>>` + `Verify` was already an established pattern in this repo -- an exhaustive grep found zero occurrences; every existing `ILogger<T>` dependency in every test project (`ProcessExcelFileServiceTests`, `ClosedXmlExtractionServiceTests`, `LoginTests`, etc.) uses `NullLogger<T>.Instance` with no assertions on log calls. Flagged to the user before writing any test; **confirmed to follow the existing convention** rather than introduce a new one -- logging is treated as an observability side effect, not TDD-driven business logic, consistent with how `ProcessExcelFileService` originally added its own logging. All existing unit/integration tests for the 5 services + orchestrator (Application.Tests, Infrastructure.Tests) and `ImportProfileTestTests` (BlazorAdmin.Tests, DI-based) were updated to supply `NullLogger<T>.Instance` for the new constructor parameter; no test asserts on logging behavior itself.
  - DI: no `Program.cs` changes needed in either host -- `AddSingleton<TInterface, TImpl>()` resolves the new `ILogger<T>` constructor parameters automatically via the framework's already-registered logging services.
- **Lot G2 (structured log content: duration/sheet count/element count/coordinate, done)**: `ImportPipelineOrchestrator.Run` wraps its body in a `Stopwatch`, added right after the start log. The completion log gained `{ElapsedMs}`, `{SheetCount}` (a literal `SheetsProcessedOnSuccess = 6` -- always exactly 6 once PROCEDURE succeeds, no sheet-name collection exists in this class to count instead), `{TotalElementCount}` (`isolements.Count + points.Count + procedureResult.TachesMultiples.Count`), and the pre-existing per-category counts; the rejection (`LogWarning`) and unexpected-failure (`LogError`) paths also gained `{ElapsedMs}`. The per-`ExtractionError` logging G1 already added (`ExtractionErrorLogging.Log`, `{Code}`/`{Sheet}`/`{BlockIdentifier}`/`{Message}`) already satisfied the "exception type" requirement (`Code`) -- G2's own ticket asked to verify whether `BlockIdentifier` is a genuine merged-cell coordinate before logging it as one.
  - **Coordinate question resolved (2026-07-18, user-confirmed)**: `ExtractionError.BlockIdentifier` is not a reliable cell coordinate today -- checked every construction site: `ProcedureExtractionService`'s whole-file rejection passes a real cell range (`"M2:O2"`), `RepeatingBlockReader` passes a bare row number (`blockStartRow.ToString()`), and `IsolementExtractionService`/`AutresJointsTouchesExtractionService`/`DiversExtractionService` all pass the Isolement's business repère (e.g. `"D8570-V4"`). This is a *re-opening* of a decision already made at the model's original design (`modele-domaine-import-profile.md` §3 deliberately generalized the cahier des charges' "merged cell coordinate" into "repère ou n° de ligne"), not a new gap. Presented as a fork (log `BlockIdentifier` as-is vs. open a "G2bis" to thread a genuine coordinate through the Domain model + 5 services + `RepeatingBlockReader`) -- **user chose to keep `BlockIdentifier` as-is**, no Domain change, no G2bis. `ExtractionErrorLogging` (from G1) needed no changes as a result.
  - **New test double**: `CapturingLogger<T>`/`CapturedLogEntries` (`tests/ExcelETL.Infrastructure.Tests/Excel/CapturingLogger.cs`) -- a hand-rolled `ILogger<T>` that records `(LogLevel, formatted message, Exception?)` into a shared sink, mirroring the existing `FakeHttpMessageHandler` precedent (avoids adding `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Testing`/`Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.Testing`, neither referenced anywhere in the repo, for one test file's worth of need). Deliberately *not* the `Mock<ILogger<T>>` + `Verify` pattern rejected in G1 -- this captures real formatted output from a full pipeline run for black-box assertions, not per-call-site invocation verification.
  - **`ImportPipelineOrchestratorLoggingIntegrationTests`** (Infrastructure.Tests, duplicates the same hardcoded profile/fixture-path helper as `ImportPipelineOrchestratorIntegrationTests` rather than sharing it, matching this repo's no-shared-test-helper convention): asserts the G6306B completion log contains `"in {N}ms"`, `"6 sheet(s) processed"`, and the exact total element count; asserts the D8570 run's `"VANNE"` isolement produces a `Warning`-level log entry (via `IsolementExtractionService`'s own `ILogger`) containing `UnrecognizedTypeElement` and the isolement's repère -- proving the non-blocking-warning case is visible in logs, not only in `ImportResult.Errors`.
- **Lot G3 (shared Serilog host config, done -- decision recorded 2026-07-19, see `docs/tickets-tdd-corrections-audit-coherence.md`)**: closes the gap the 2026-07-19 status snapshot (`docs/etat-avancement-lot-g-logging-oxo-2026-07-19.md`) flagged as never decided. **Decision**: *"Le pipeline OXO n'a pas de persistance de log dédiée ; il s'appuie sur `ILogger<T>` + la configuration Serilog de l'hôte appelant. Tout hôte qui résout un service du pipeline OXO doit appeler `AddOxoHostLogging(...)` pour garantir l'écriture dans `SystemLogs`."* No dedicated persistence for the OXO pipeline (no `ExtractionHistory`-like table/migration) -- the pipeline stays exactly as G1 left it, `ILogger<T>`-only in `ExcelETL.Application`/`ExcelETL.Infrastructure`, with no reference to the concrete Serilog package anywhere in those two projects. The problem G3 actually closes is duplication: before this lot, `ExcelETL.WebAPI/Program.cs` and `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Program.cs` each carried their own independently-typed `UseSerilog(...)` block (console + `MSSqlServer`/`SystemLogs` sinks, `Enrich.WithProperty("Application", ...)`) -- nothing guaranteed a third host resolving OXO pipeline services would reproduce it correctly.
  - **New project, `src/ExcelETL.Hosting/`**: a small class library referenced **only** by the two host projects (`ExcelETL.WebAPI`, `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin`), not by `Application`/`Infrastructure`/`Domain` -- keeps the "no concrete Serilog dependency below the host" rule intact while still de-duplicating the config. `SerilogHostLoggingExtensions.AddOxoHostLogging(this IHostBuilder, string applicationName, string connectionString)` is the call site each `Program.cs` now uses (`builder.Host.AddOxoHostLogging("ExcelETL.WebAPI", connectionString)` / `"ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin"`); the actual sink/enrichment setup is factored into a second public method, `Configure(LoggerConfiguration, applicationName, connectionString, IConfiguration)`, so it's testable without needing a real `IHostBuilder`/`WebApplicationBuilder`. Both host `.csproj` files dropped their own direct `Serilog.AspNetCore`/`Serilog.Sinks.MSSqlServer` `PackageReference`s (now pulled in transitively via the new `ProjectReference`) and gained a `ProjectReference` to `ExcelETL.Hosting`.
  - **Tests (`tests/ExcelETL.Hosting.Tests/SerilogHostLoggingExtensionsTests.cs`)**: call `Configure(...)` directly (no `IHostBuilder` involved) with `Serilog:EnableMsSqlServerSink=false` (same test-safe switch the two hosts' own tests already relied on) so nothing opens a real SQL Server connection. `[Theory]` over both host names (`"ExcelETL.WebAPI"`/`"ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin"`) proves the shared method behaves identically regardless of caller: the `Application` enrichment property matches whichever name was passed, `MinimumLevel.Override("Microsoft.AspNetCore", Warning)` filters/keeps events the same way for both, and building+logging never throws even with an unreachable connection string. This is the "non-duplication" guard-rail the ticket asked for -- it fails if a future edit reintroduces two independently-drifting copies of this configuration.
  - No regressions: `ImportPipelineOrchestratorLoggingIntegrationTests` (Lot G1/G2) and the full existing suite all still pass unmodified after the `Program.cs` rewiring -- see the test run below.
- **Lot G4 (auto-apply EF Core migrations on host startup, done -- decision recorded 2026-07-21)**: closes the gap that caused a real incident -- `AddImportProfile`/`AddExportProfile` (Lot E2/I6) had been merged for days but never applied to the dev LocalDB, so `/import-profiles` 500'd with `Invalid object name 'ImportProfiles'`. **Decision (user-confirmed)**: applying migrations was previously a manual `dotnet ef database update` step, defensible while the project was pure greenfield dev; now that the client is starting to use a test environment, that manual step is a standing risk, so **both hosts now apply their own pending migrations automatically on every startup**, relying on EF Core's built-in distributed migration lock (`sp_getapplock` on `__EFMigrationsLock`, observed directly in the SQL log during this lot) rather than an enforced host-startup order -- confirmed safe for two hosts sharing `ExcelEtlDbContext` (WebAPI + BlazorAdmin): whichever starts first applies pending migrations, the other blocks briefly on the lock then finds nothing left to do.
  - **New shared extension, `ExcelETL.Hosting/DatabaseMigrationHostExtensions.cs`** (same "define once, both hosts call it" precedent as `AddOxoHostLogging`, Lot G3): `IServiceProvider.MigrateIfEnabledAsync<TContext>(IConfiguration)` resolves `TContext` via its registered `IDbContextFactory<TContext>` (not `AddDbContext` directly -- neither host registers `ExcelEtlDbContext` that way) and calls `Database.MigrateAsync()`, gated by two checks: the `Database:AutoMigrate` config switch (default `true`) and `Database.IsRelational()`. `ExcelETL.Hosting` gained a `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Relational` package reference for this (still no reference to `Infrastructure`/`Domain`, stays a pure host-composition-root helper).
  - **Call sites**: `ExcelETL.WebAPI/Program.cs` calls it for `ExcelEtlDbContext` only, right before `app.Run()`. `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Program.cs` calls it for both `ExcelEtlDbContext` and `ApplicationIdentityDbContext`, placed **before** the existing `IdentitySeeder.SeedAsync()` call (seeding needs the Identity schema to already exist).
  - **Why `IsRelational()` alone isn't enough for tests**: `ExcelProcessEndpointTests`/`HistoryDownloadEndpointTests` swap `ExcelEtlDbContext` to the EF Core InMemory provider via `ConfigureTestServices` -- `IsRelational()` correctly skips those. But `HealthPingTests`/`ApiKeyAuthenticationTests` (WebAPI) never swap the DbContext at all (they don't touch the DB, so today's lazy `IDbContextFactory` registration never opens a connection) -- an unconditional `Migrate()` call would newly force these to require a real, reachable SQL Server just to start the host, which they never needed before. All 4 `WebApplicationFactory<Program>`-based test fixtures in the solution now explicitly set `builder.UseSetting("Database:AutoMigrate", "false")`, mirroring the pre-existing `IdentitySeeding:Enabled` switch's precedent (`HistoryDownloadEndpointTests` already had that exact problem solved for seeding, same shape of fix applied here for migration).
  - **Verified against the real LocalDB `ExcelEtl` database** (not just unit tests): rolled back to before `AddImportProfile`/`AddExportProfile`, ran `dotnet run` for `ExcelETL.WebAPI`, confirmed in the startup log that it acquired the migration lock and applied both migrations before `Application started`, then confirmed via `dotnet ef migrations list` that both are applied. Full suite (622 tests) green after the `Program.cs`/test-fixture changes.
  - **Deliberately out of scope for this lot**: no pre-migration backup step, no CI/CD-gated migration pipeline. Acceptable while this is a test/pre-prod environment with no real client data at stake; flagged to revisit once the client's data in this environment has real value.
- **Lot I (target-workbook writing pipeline, done -- ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-ecriture-fichier-cible.md`)**: the symmetric counterpart to the read side (Lot A-E) -- an `ImportResult` (pivot) + a new `ExportProfile` in, a generated `.xlsx` out. **Not wired to any host as of this lot** (Blazor UI came later as Lot J; Web API exposure is still out of scope), so no `Program.cs` changes in either host at this point.
  - **I1/I2 (Domain, `src/ExcelETL.Domain/Generation/`)**: `ExportProfile`/`SheetGenerationRule`/`ColumnDefinition`/`PointColumnDefinition` (`Generation/Profile/`) are **all `sealed record`s with structural equality** -- a deliberate, ticket-mandated departure from the import side's `ImportProfile : Entity`/`SheetExtractionRule` (plain class) pattern. `ExportProfile` still carries its own `Guid Id` (plain property, not `Entity`-derived, since `Entity`'s identity-only `Equals`/`GetHashCode` override would conflict with record equality) so Lot I6's store can upsert by Id, using the same "reconstruct under an existing Id" constructor-overload trick as `ImportProfile`. `ColumnDefinition.Source` is `PivotFieldRef?` -- `null` is a valid, tested state ("column reserved in the schema, no rule wired yet, writes an empty cell"). `SheetGenerationRule` validates no duplicate `Header` across its `ColumnDefinitions`+`PointColumnDefinitions` combined, no duplicate `ColonneNom` among `PointColumnDefinitions`, and (added by I2) that every non-null `ColumnDefinition.Source`'s pivot type matches the rule's own `PivotSource` -- **checked at profile-construction time** (`DomainRuleViolationException`), not silently at generation time, per the ticket's explicit requirement. `PivotFieldRef` (`Generation/Fields/`, 9 members incl. `EquipementLocalisation`/`IsolementLocalisation` for the `loc1` broadcast) + `PivotFieldResolver.Resolve(EquipementPivot|IsolementPivot, PivotFieldRef)` give reflection-free typed field access; `PivotFieldResolver.GetPivotSource` is what `SheetGenerationRule` calls for its cross-validation.
  - **I3 (Application, `src/ExcelETL.Application/Generation/`)**: `GeneratedRow`/`GeneratedSheet`/`GeneratedWorkbook` are the ClosedXML-free intermediate output (plain records, list properties, no `SequenceEqual` override needed -- mirrors `RepeatingBlockReadResult`'s precedent, since tests use `BeEquivalentTo` rather than object equality). `SheetGenerationEngine : ISheetGenerationEngine` turns `(ImportResult, ExportProfile)` into a `GeneratedWorkbook`: header order is descriptive columns then Point columns; a Point column is marked with its `MarkValue` only when a `PointPivot` exists matching **both** `ParentRepere` and `ColonneNom` for that row. **User-confirmed decision**: when `ImportResult.Equipement is null` (upstream whole-file rejection), the Equipement-sourced sheet is still emitted with its header row and **zero data rows** (not omitted entirely) -- keeps the generated workbook's sheet count stable regardless of upstream outcome, symmetric to the import side's "an empty sheet is a legitimate result" precedent (ORIFICES CAPACITES, Lot C4). `IWorkbookWriter` (`Write(GeneratedWorkbook, Stream)`) is the Infrastructure-facing abstraction.
  - **I4 (Infrastructure, `src/ExcelETL.Infrastructure/Excel/ClosedXmlWorkbookWriter.cs`)**: new, explicitly **not** built on the legacy `ClosedXmlGeneratorService` (POC, consumes `ExtractionResult`/`ExtractedSheet`, not the OXO pivot model) per the ticket's own "hors périmètre" list. Tested against the real ClosedXML library (never mocked), same convention as `ClosedXmlWorkbookReader` (Lot E1). `TargetWorkbookFileNameBuilder.Build(equipementRepere, generatedAt)` implements the spec doc's `MAD_{Equipement.Nom}_{AAAAMMDDHHmmss}.xlsx` naming convention as its own testable function, separate from the write path -- `EquipementPivot` has no literal `Nom` property (only `Repere`/`Designation`/`TypeElementNom`/`Localisation`), so `Repere` (the glossary-confirmed `BaseElement` business search key) stands in for the spec's informal "Nom", documented inline as an interpretation call.
  - **I5 (`GenerationPipelineIntegrationTests`, Infrastructure.Tests)**: full chain (`ClosedXmlWorkbookReader` -> `ImportPipelineOrchestrator` -> `SheetGenerationEngine` -> `ClosedXmlWorkbookWriter` -> reread via ClosedXML) against all 3 real fixtures, duplicating the hardcoded `ImportProfile` from `ImportPipelineOrchestratorIntegrationTests` per this repo's no-shared-test-helper convention. The test `ExportProfile` is a **deliberate partial approximation** of the client's real `OXO_TRAME_IMPORT_MAD.xlsx` (2 sheets, `Parents`/`Enfants`) -- not full column fidelity, per the ticket's own "approximation de travail" framing; only a representative subset of descriptive/Point columns is mapped. Confirms known repère/`loc1` values land in generated cells, and that D8570's `"VANNE"` isolement (non-blocking `UnrecognizedTypeElement` warning upstream) still appears as an ordinary `Enfants` row. **Discovered while writing this test**: a generated-sheet `Repere` (Numéro/Numéro-equivalent) column is **not** a safe row-lookup key on its own -- it's composed independently per source sheet (`{K6:T6}-{Identification}`), so two different isolement-style sheets can coincidentally produce the same composed `Repere` text for otherwise-unrelated rows; the D8570 `"VANNE"` lookup disambiguates on `Repere` **and** `TypeElementNom` together.
  - **I6 (EF Core persistence, done -- Lot I complete)**: `IExportProfileStore` (`src/ExcelETL.Application/Generation/`, alongside the other Lot I Application types) / `EfExportProfileStore` (`src/ExcelETL.Infrastructure/Persistence/Repositories/`) mirror `IImportProfileStore`/`EfImportProfileStore` exactly -- short-lived `DbContext` per method, two-round-trip upsert keyed by `profile.Id` (delete-then-insert across two `SaveChangesAsync` calls, same change-tracker constraint as the import side). Owned-type EF mapping (`ExportProfileConfiguration.cs`): `SheetGenerationRule` (`OwnsMany`, own table + shadow `int Id`), nested `ColumnDefinitions`/`PointColumnDefinitions` (both second-level `OwnsMany`, their own tables, FK `SheetGenerationRuleId`). `ColumnDefinition.Source` is mapped `HasConversion<string>()` **without** `IsRequired()` so `null` round-trips as a genuine `null`, not a default enum value -- covered by a dedicated regression test (`SaveAsync_WithNullColumnSource_PersistsAndReloadsAsNull_NotADefaultValue`), the ticket's explicit requirement. Migration `20260718092214_AddExportProfile` adds `ExportProfiles`/`ExportProfileSheetRules`/`ExportProfileSheetRuleColumnDefinitions`/`ExportProfileSheetRulePointColumnDefinitions` (FK cascade-delete to their owner) -- not yet applied to any real SQL Server database.
- **Lot J (Blazor export-profile screens, done -- ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-blazor-profil-export.md`, symmetric to Lot F on the import side)**: wires Lot I's generation pipeline into BlazorAdmin. All 3 pages live in `Components/Pages/Admin/`, mirroring the `ImportProfiles`/`ImportProfileEditor`/`ImportProfileTest` file layout and TDD/bUnit conventions exactly.
  - **J1 (`ExportProfiles.razor`, route `/export-profiles`)**: list + create/edit/duplicate nav, `IExportProfileStore`-backed. Unlike `ImportProfiles.razor` there's no `EquipementTypeElementNom` column (`ExportProfile` has no such root field) and there's an `#edit-export-profile-button-{id}` per row navigating to `/export-profiles/{id}/edit` (J2) -- Import's list page has no edit action at all, since Lot F never added import-profile editing.
  - **J2 (`ExportProfileEditor.razor`, two `@page` routes on one component: `/export-profiles/new` and `/export-profiles/{Id:guid}/edit`)**: **deliberately supports editing an existing profile, unlike `ImportProfileEditor.razor`** -- the ticket's explicit rationale is that a target-format export profile gets adjusted regularly (columns going from unmapped to mapped, new sheets), so forcing duplicate-then-rename per tweak would be a real usability cost, not an architectural nicety worth copying from Lot F by default. `[Parameter] public Guid? Id { get; set; }` stays `null` on the `/new` route; `OnInitializedAsync` loads via `GetByIdAsync` when set, prefilling `_name` and the `_sheetRules` list, and tracks the loaded profile's `Id` separately (`_editingId`) so `SaveProfileAsync` reconstructs the profile under the **same** Id (`ExportProfile`'s `Guid`-taking constructor) rather than minting a new one. An unknown `Id` on the edit route sets a `_notFound` flag that suppresses the whole form (no exception, no empty-form flash). The "add sheet rule" sub-form's column `Source` select is filtered live to only the `PivotFieldRef` members matching the sheet's chosen `PivotSource` (`PivotFieldResolver.GetPivotSource`), with an explicit "not mapped yet" option mapping to `Source = null` -- keeps the UI from ever building a profile `SheetGenerationRule`'s own constructor would reject anyway (I2's cross-validation), though that constructor check still runs and is still what actually enforces it. Only 2 root-field validation tests (empty name, no sheet rules) vs Import's 4, since `ExportProfile` has only `Name` as a root field.
  - **Gap discovered and fixed while building J2**: `DomainErrorMessages.resx`/`.fr.resx` had **zero** entries for any of the 10 `DomainErrorCode` members Lot I's Generation domain types added (`ExportProfile_*`, `SheetGenerationRule_*`, `ColumnDefinition_*`, `PointColumnDefinition_*`) -- `BusinessExceptionLocalizer` was silently falling back to the raw resource-key string (e.g. literal text `"ExportProfile_EmptyName"` rendered to the user) for every one of them until J2's own tests caught it. Same class of gap as the one F1.2 fixed for the import-side OXO extraction errors; the two gaps were independent (different Domain folders, different ticket authors), not a repeat of the same oversight.
  - **J3 (`ExportProfileTest.razor`, route `/export-profiles/test`)**: covers both ends of the pipeline on one page, since generation needs an `ImportResult` as input. An **import**-profile select plus file upload (`e.File.OpenReadStream` → `ClosedXmlWorkbookReader` → `IImportPipelineOrchestrator.Run`, identical flow to `ImportProfileTest.razor`/F2) is followed by an **export**-profile select plus a "Generate workbook" button (`ISheetGenerationEngine.Generate` → `IWorkbookWriter.Write` into a `MemoryStream`), both entirely in process -- no HTTP anywhere, verified by a dedicated test that greps the component's own source text for `HttpClient`/`ExcelProcessingClient`/`IExcelDownloadInterop`. **Generation is structurally blocked, not just logically disabled, when the import is rejected** (`ImportResult.Equipement is null`): the export-profile select and Generate button simply aren't part of the render tree in that branch, proven by a `Mock<ISheetGenerationEngine>` test asserting `Generate` is never invoked. The download link needs no JS interop at all (unlike `UploadTest.razor`'s `IExcelDownloadInterop`) -- the generated bytes are already in memory, so it's a plain `<a href="data:application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet;base64,...">`. One HTML `<table id="generated-sheet-{sheetName}-table">` per `GeneratedSheet`, matching the ticket's "no need to visually reproduce Excel" note. Reuses the same hardcoded `ImportProfile` fixture as `ImportProfileTestTests`/`ImportPipelineOrchestratorIntegrationTests` and the same hardcoded `ExportProfile` fixture (`Parents`/`Enfants`) as `GenerationPipelineIntegrationTests`, per this repo's no-shared-test-helper convention -- confirms the D8570 `"VANNE"` isolement (non-blocking `UnrecognizedTypeElement` warning upstream) still lands as an ordinary row in the generated `Enfants` preview, mirroring I5's requirement at the UI level.
  - **J4 (DI, `Program.cs`)**: `IExportProfileStore`/`EfExportProfileStore` registered `AddScoped` (matches `IImportProfileStore`'s lifetime); `ISheetGenerationEngine`/`SheetGenerationEngine` and `IWorkbookWriter`/`ClosedXmlWorkbookWriter` registered `AddSingleton` (stateless, matches the 9 OXO pipeline services' lifetime). No DI-registration integration test was added -- no precedent for one exists anywhere in `BlazorAdmin.Tests` (the import-pipeline services aren't tested that way either), so per the ticket's own fallback instruction this is verified by reading `Program.cs` plus the fact that `ExportProfileTestTests` exercises the same services end-to-end via its own bUnit-local DI setup.
  - Two new nav-icon CSS classes added to `NavMenu.razor.css` for the Lot J nav links (`bi-file-earmark-spreadsheet-nav-menu` for the list page, `bi-download-nav-menu` for the test page) -- Import's `bi-collection-nav-menu`/`bi-play-circle-nav-menu` icons were deliberately not reused, to keep the two lots visually distinct in the sidebar.
  - **Lot J complete.**
- **Lot K (Web API exposure of the OXO pipeline, migration and POC retirement, done — ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-migration-webapi-oxo.md`)**: closes the gap that had blocked K4 for months — the OXO pipeline had no HTTP route, so the old `ExtractionConfig` pipeline (`ExcelController`/`ProcessExcelFileService`) couldn't be retired even after it was fully superseded.
  - **K0/K0bis**: audit found zero `ILogger` usage in `SheetGenerationEngine`/`ClosedXmlWorkbookWriter` (the generation half of the pipeline) — added, mirroring `ImportPipelineOrchestrator`'s Lot G1/G2 convention exactly (start/success/error logs with elapsed time, `NullLogger<T>.Instance` in tests, no logging assertions).
  - **K1/K2**: `POST /api/oxo/process` (`OxoController`, `src/ExcelETL.WebAPI/Controllers/OxoController.cs`) is the OXO pipeline's HTTP entry point — see the "Web API surface" bullet below for its contract. `ProcessOxoFileService` (`src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/`) is the new HTTP-facing orchestration service, the OXO counterpart of `ProcessExcelFileService`: resolves both profiles (`ImportProfileNotFoundException`/`ExportProfileNotFoundException`, new `ApplicationErrorCode` members), runs the pipeline, distinguishes rejection from success, generates + archives (reuses `IFileStorageService`). `TargetWorkbookFileNameBuilder` moved from `ExcelETL.Infrastructure.Excel` to `ExcelETL.Application.Generation` (pure string logic, no ClosedXML dependency — Application needed it and can't reference Infrastructure).
  - **Real bug found and fixed during K1**: `ClosedXmlWorkbookReader.ReadCellValue` rendered date-typed cells via ClosedXML's `GetString()`, which uses `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture` — under the WebAPI host, `RequestLocalizationOptions` negotiates `en-US` by default per request, producing a date string `ProcedureExtractionService.TryParseDate`'s fixed `dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss` format couldn't parse, **rejecting every real fixture** the instant the pipeline ran behind an actual HTTP request (never caught before: no prior host ran this pipeline under ASP.NET Core's culture negotiation). Fixed by rendering `XLDataType.DateTime` cells with `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` explicitly instead of trusting `GetString()`.
  - **K3**: `legacy/ExcelProcessingClientService.ProcessAsync` now takes `(Guid importProfileId, Guid exportProfileId, HttpPostedFileBase file)`, posts to `api/oxo/process` — direct one-shot contract switch, no feature flag. Scoped strictly to that legacy library; the real ASP.NET MVC 5 call site lives outside this repository and was not touched (confirmed via `docs/etat-des-lieux-technique.md`: `legacy/` "ne fait pas partie de la solution .NET 10").
  - **K4 (POC retirement, done)**: removed the entire old pipeline — `ExcelController`, `ProcessExcelFileService`, `ClosedXmlExtractionService`/`ClosedXmlGeneratorService`, `IExtractionConfigRepository`/`IExtractionHistoryRepository` + EF configurations, Domain entities `ExtractionConfig`/`SheetConfig`/`CellMapping`/`ExtractionHistory` (+ `CellDataType`/`ExtractionStatus` enums), and BlazorAdmin's `Mappings.razor`/`UploadTest.razor`/`Dashboard.razor`/`History.razor`/`AdminEndpointRouteBuilderExtensions.cs`/`ExcelProcessingClient`/`IExcelDownloadInterop`/`WebApiClientOptions`/`wwwroot/js/fileDownload.js`. **Scope extended beyond the original ticket** once code review showed `ExtractionHistory` (POC-only data, confirmed single-writer `ProcessExcelFileService`) was still read by `Dashboard.razor`/`History.razor`/the history-download endpoint — all three retired together rather than left pointing at a frozen, permanently-stale table. `ImportProfiles.razor` gained a second `@page "/"` route as the new landing page (verified via browser: hitting `/` now triggers the `[Authorize]` redirect to `/Account/Login` instead of a 404, and `NavMenu` shows only `Logs`/`Register`/`Login` for an unauthenticated visitor). New EF Core migration `20260721095640_RemoveExtractionConfigPoc` drops `ExtractionConfigs`/`SheetConfigs`/`CellMappings`/`ExtractionHistories` — applied and verified against the real LocalDB via the dev server's auto-migrate (Lot G4). `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design` added directly to `ExcelETL.WebAPI.csproj` (PrivateAssets="all") since Infrastructure's own reference is private and doesn't flow to the startup project `dotnet ef` needs. `ApplicationErrorCode`/`DomainErrorCode` and their `.resx` entries pruned to POC-only members (`WorksheetNotFoundInWorkbookException`'s error code kept — reused by the OXO reader). Full suite green after removal: Domain 215, Application 94, Infrastructure 101, WebAPI 13, BlazorAdmin 77, Hosting 6, legacy 15.
  - **Lot K complete — this was the last lot on the extraction/generation pipeline's roadmap; `ExtractionConfig`/`SheetConfig`/`CellMapping`/`ExtractionHistory` and every file listed above no longer exist in this repo.**
- **Default-profile seeding (done — ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-seed-profils-defaut.md`; the doc's own header calls it "Lot M", but that letter was already used for the unrelated NavMenu username/Profile-link merge above — referred to here by ticket doc name to avoid confusion, not renumbered)**: `DefaultProfileSeeder` (`src/ExcelETL.Infrastructure/Seeding/DefaultProfileSeeder.cs`) bootstraps the standard OXO import/export profiles on every `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin` startup, mirroring `IdentitySeeder`'s pattern exactly — scoped, idempotent, gated by a config switch (`ProfileSeeding:Enabled`, default `true`, checked in `Program.cs` right after the `IdentitySeeding:Enabled` block, not inside the seeder itself). Looked up by two stable hardcoded `Guid` constants (`DefaultProfileSeeder.ImportProfileId`/`ExportProfileId`), never by `Name` — an admin can rename a seeded profile, so `Name` is not a safe identity check. **Strict idempotence, client-confirmed**: once a profile exists under that Id, the seeder never touches it again regardless of admin edits since — same "an existing account is never reset" behavior as `IdentitySeeder`.
  - The default import profile's 6 `SheetExtractionRule`s are transcribed verbatim from the already-validated hardcoded profile in `ImportPipelineOrchestratorIntegrationTests`/`GenerationPipelineIntegrationTests` (same cell ranges, steps, `UnconditionalColonneNames`, `ConditionalPointRule`s) — the ticket's own pre-implementation checklist re-verified every literal against the 5 real extraction services with zero divergence found. `EquipementTypeElementNom = "MAD TRAVAUX"` is the one business value hardcoded in the seeder itself (the Domain model deliberately has no default for it). PROCEDURE's header cell ranges and its 2 unconditional Points (`"TRAVAUX COMPLET"`/`"TRAVAUX DETAIL"`) and DIVERS' `loc1` (`B6:E6`) are **not** expressed in the seeded `SheetExtractionRule`s — confirmed still hardcoded inside `ProcedureExtractionService`/`DiversExtractionService` themselves, out of any profile's reach.
  - The default export profile is **deliberately minimal** (client-confirmed): only the descriptive columns the import profile already produces (`Repère`/`Type Elément`/`Zone`/`Désignation` for `"Parents"`; `Numéro`/`Type`/`Zone`/`Désignation`/`Position à la pose` for `"Enfants"`, all via `PivotFieldRef`) plus every Point `Colonne` the import profile actually produces (2 for `"Parents"` — the hardcoded PROCEDURE Points; 17 deduplicated across ISOLEMENT/PLATINES/ORIFICES CAPACITES/AUTRES JOINTS TOUCHES/DIVERS for `"Enfants"`) — no `Source = null` placeholder columns anticipating extraction rules that don't exist yet, unlike `GenerationPipelineIntegrationTests`' own fixture (which does include a couple of `Source = null` columns as a deliberate approximation for that unrelated test). Point column `Header`s reuse the raw `Colonne` name verbatim — no localized-label catalogue exists for these yet.
  - Tests (`tests/ExcelETL.Infrastructure.Tests/Seeding/`, real EF Core InMemory via a colocated `TestExcelEtlDbContextFactory`, never mocked): `DefaultProfileSeederTests` covers empty-database creation, no-duplicate-on-repeat-run, "never overwrite an admin-modified profile" for both profile types, and content assertions for every sheet rule/column (including the cross-consistency check that every `Enfants` `PointColumnDefinition.ColonneNom` is actually produced by the import profile, and the DIVERS `"POINT FEU"` vs `"POINT DE FEU"` literal guard). `DefaultProfileSeederPipelineIntegrationTests` re-runs the same non-regression assertions as `ImportPipelineOrchestratorIntegrationTests`/`GenerationPipelineIntegrationTests` (isolement counts per fixture, `loc1` broadcast, D8570's `"VANNE"` warning, generated headers/values) but against the profile **as seeded and fetched back via `IImportProfileStore`/`IExportProfileStore`**, not rebuilt inline — proving the seeder's transcription is correct, not just a hand-built test double.
  - **No dedicated test for `ProfileSeeding:Enabled = false`**: unlike `IdentitySeeding:Enabled`, which is exercised indirectly by WebAPI's `WebApplicationFactory`-based tests, there is no `WebApplicationFactory<Program>` integration-test project for `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin` in this repo at all (`bin`/`obj` aside, confirmed by search) — so this flag has the same test coverage as its `IdentitySeeding:Enabled` sibling had before this lot: none, verified by code reading only. Building a new host-level test harness just for this one flag was judged disproportionate and out of this ticket's scope; revisit if/when a BlazorAdmin host-level test project is ever added for other reasons.

### Web API surface
- `POST /api/oxo/process` — `OxoController` (`src/ExcelETL.WebAPI/Controllers/OxoController.cs`), the sole HTTP entry point since Lot K4 retired the old `ExtractionConfig` pipeline. `multipart/form-data`, `[FromForm] ProcessOxoFileRequest { Guid? ImportProfileId; Guid? ExportProfileId; IFormFile File }` — **`Guid?`, not `Guid`, since Lot 036.1** (a public-contract change): a field totally absent from the multipart body is now a `400` naming the missing parameter (checked first, in order `ImportProfileId` then `ExportProfileId` — when both are missing only the first is reported), distinct from the pre-existing `404` for a syntactically-valid `Guid` matching no profile (via `IImportProfileStore`/`IExportProfileStore` → `ImportProfileNotFoundException`/`ExportProfileNotFoundException`); a syntactically-invalid `Guid` value is still rejected earlier by ASP.NET Core's own `[ApiController]` model binding, regardless of nullability. A missing `File` field entirely is also caught by that same automatic model-state validation (still `400`, but with ASP.NET Core's own generic "field is required" message, never reaching the controller body) — confirmed by test rather than assumed; only a **present-but-zero-length** file reaches the controller's own explicit `request.File.Length == 0` check (localized `EmptyFileUploadRequired` message). **Since Lot 036.2**, a file whose content can't be opened as a valid Excel workbook (`XLWorkbook`'s constructor throws `System.IO.FileFormatException`, confirmed by direct investigation, not assumed) is now caught explicitly in `OxoController` and translated to `400` (`InvalidExcelFileFormat`) — never an unqualified `500`; this exception type is a bare BCL one, not `IHasDomainErrorCode`/`IHasApplicationErrorCode`, so `GlobalExceptionHandler`/`BusinessExceptionLocalizer` alone would never have caught it (translation happens at the controller level, the same level that already handled the empty-file case, not via `GlobalExceptionHandler.StatusCodeFor`). Runs `ImportPipelineOrchestrator` then `SheetGenerationEngine`/`ClosedXmlWorkbookWriter`; a whole-file rejection (`ImportResult.Equipement is null`, model doc §3.1) returns `422 Unprocessable Entity` with a `ProblemDetails.Extensions["errors"]` list instead of attempting generation. On success, returns `FileStreamResult` (`application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet`), archived via `IGeneratedFileWriter`/`IGeneratedFileArchiveStore` (Lot 034 — the sole archiving mechanism since Lot 046 removed the older, redundant `IFileStorageService`). Max 10 MB, 5-min server timeout policy (`UploadLimits.MaxExcelFileSizeBytes` / `ExcelProcessingTimeout` / `ExcelProcessingTimeoutPolicy`) — kept identical to BlazorAdmin's `ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor` client-side cap so those pages exercise the server's real limit. **`legacy/ExcelProcessingClientService` unaffected by the `Guid` → `Guid?` change**: confirmed it always sends both fields as real, non-null `Guid` values (never omits them), so the new `400` path is unreachable from that client — no signal needed to Simon per the ticket's own contingency.
- API key auth: header `X-Api-Key` (`ApiKeyAuthenticationDefaults.HeaderName`), constant-time compare in `ApiKeyAuthenticationHandler`, value at appsettings key `ApiKeyAuthentication:ApiKey` (only set in `appsettings.Development.json` today — prod value expected via user secrets/env var). Applied as the fallback auth policy for the whole WebAPI app.
- Kestrel (WebAPI `Program.cs`): `MaxRequestBodySize`=10 MB, `KeepAliveTimeout`=5 min, `RequestHeadersTimeout`=2 min.
- Logging: Serilog, not the bare `ILogger` console provider — `WriteTo.Console()` plus a SQL Server sink (`Serilog.Sinks.MSSqlServer`, table `SystemLogs`, `AutoCreateSqlTable = true`, schema owned by Serilog itself and intentionally outside the EF Core Code-First migrations used for the domain database). **As of Lot G3, this setup is defined exactly once**, in `ExcelETL.Hosting/SerilogHostLoggingExtensions.cs` (`AddOxoHostLogging(this IHostBuilder, applicationName, connectionString)`) — both `ExcelETL.WebAPI/Program.cs` and `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Program.cs` call it with only their own host name and connection string differing (`"ExcelETL.WebAPI"` / `"ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin"`, enriched via `Enrich.WithProperty("Application", ...)`), rather than each carrying its own copy of the sink/enrichment setup as before Lot G3. Both write to the same `SystemLogs` table so BlazorAdmin's `Logs.razor` page can render a unified log view distinguishing which host emitted each entry — `SystemLogsDbContext` (BlazorAdmin) reads this table read-only via its own `IDbContextFactory`, no migrations of its own. The sink is gated by config key `Serilog:EnableMsSqlServerSink` (default `true`); both `WebApplicationFactory`-based WebAPI integration tests and BlazorAdmin bUnit tests set it `false` so host startup never opens a real SQL Server connection. **As of Lot K4, this mechanism covers only the OXO pipeline** (the old POC pipeline and its separate `ExtractionHistory` table no longer exist) — the OXO pipeline still has no dedicated log persistence of its own (no Serilog dependency in `ExcelETL.Application`/`ExcelETL.Infrastructure`), it only emits through `ILogger<T>`, relying on whichever host resolves its services to have wired `AddOxoHostLogging(...)` so those events actually reach `SystemLogs`.

### BlazorAdmin state
- Auth pattern for admin pages: `@attribute [Authorize(Roles = IdentitySeeder.AdminRoleName)]` (see `Components/Pages/Admin/Users.razor`).
- Rendermode is set **once, globally**: `App.razor` → `<Routes @rendermode="InteractiveServer" />`, `Program.cs` → `.AddInteractiveServerRenderMode()`. Individual pages must not declare their own `@rendermode`.
- i18n: `src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Resources/BlazorAdminMessages.resx`/`.fr.resx` exists and is used by Logs/Users/ImportProfiles/ImportProfileEditor/ImportProfileTest/ExportProfiles/ExportProfileEditor/ExportProfileTest/GeneratedFiles/ApiTest via `IStringLocalizer<BlazorAdminMessages>`. Key convention: `{PageName}_{Element}` (e.g. `Users_PageTitle`, `Logs_Loading`, `ImportProfiles_*`, `ImportProfileEditor_*`, `ImportProfileTest_*`, `ExportProfiles_*`, `ExportProfileEditor_*`, `ExportProfileTest_*`, `ApiTest_*`).
- **As of Lot K4**: `ExcelProcessingClient`/`IExcelDownloadInterop`/`WebApiClientOptions`/`wwwroot/js/fileDownload.js` no longer exist — they existed only to support `/upload-test` (`UploadTest.razor`), retired along with the rest of the old `ExtractionConfig` pipeline. `ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor` run the OXO pipeline in-process (see Lot F2/J3). **As of Lot 038, `ApiTest.razor` (`/api-test`) is the one deliberate, documented exception to "BlazorAdmin never talks to WebAPI over HTTP"** — a real `HttpClient` (`OxoApiTestClient`) calling the deployed Web API's `POST /api/oxo/process`, for manual post-deployment verification/demo/debug. Treat any *other* future page needing HTTP as a new decision, not an extension of this one.
- Nav-item icons (`NavMenu.razor`): every `<span class="bi bi-{name}-nav-menu" aria-hidden="true">` markup reference must have a matching CSS rule in `Components/Layout/NavMenu.razor.css` defining its `background-image` (inline Bootstrap Icons SVG data URI, `fill='white'`, matching the existing entries' format). The two files are edited as a pair — adding a `NavLink`/icon span in the `.razor` without its `.razor.css` counterpart (or vice versa) silently renders a blank icon with no build error or warning.
- **Favicon**: a plain square in `--m3-primary` (`#D81F11`) with "ETL" in `--m3-on-primary` white, matching the theme's own primary color — not the default Blazor favicon. Files (`favicon.ico`, `favicon-16x16.png`, `favicon-32x32.png`, `apple-touch-icon.png`) live in `wwwroot/`, referenced via `<link>` tags in `Components/App.razor`'s `<head>`. A future lot regenerating branding assets should keep this exact color/glyph, not silently revert to the framework default.
- **Dark/light theme toggle (done — 2026-07-31)**: `wwwroot/css/theme-m3.css` has defined a complete `[data-bs-theme="dark"]` M3 palette since Lot 060 (and its Bootstrap remaps since the theme file's own creation) — this was CSS-only and unused for months, since nothing in the app ever set the `data-bs-theme` attribute. Closed by 3 small, deliberately dependency-free pieces: `wwwroot/js/theme.js` (`window.amOxoTheme` — `get`/`set`/`toggle`/`apply`, backed by `localStorage["am-oxo-theme"]`, falling back to `prefers-color-scheme` when the visitor has never chosen explicitly), referenced as the **very first element** inside `Components/App.razor`'s `<head>` (before every stylesheet, no `defer`/`async`/`type="module"`) so the correct attribute is set before first paint — no flash of the wrong theme. The toggle button (`#theme-toggle-button`, sun/sun+moon `AdminIconMarkup.Sun`/`Moon` icons) sits in `NavMenu.razor`'s `sidebar-theme-toggle-container`, next to the client logo/version footer, outside every `AuthorizeView` (visible regardless of authentication state). It uses a **plain HTML `onclick="window.amOxoTheme.toggle()"`, not `@onclick`/`IJSRuntime`** — same convention as the pre-existing mobile hamburger toggler in the same file — so it works without any Blazor circuit round trip. Both icon spans are always in the DOM; `NavMenu.razor.css` shows only the one matching the current theme via a `:root[data-bs-theme="dark"] .theme-toggle-icon-*` attribute selector (the `:root` half of the selector deliberately isn't scoped by Blazor's CSS isolation — only the trailing `.theme-toggle-icon-*` compound gets the scope attribute appended, so the rule still matches the real `<html>` element). Tests: `Layout/NavMenuTests.cs` (button wiring/aria/icons/visibility), `Styling/ThemeToggleScriptTests.cs` (theme.js content), `Styling/ThemeToggleAppHeadOrderTests.cs` (script-before-stylesheets ordering in App.razor), `Styling/NavMenuThemeToggleCssTests.cs` (icon show/hide rules) — all plain-text/DOM-structure checks per this project's established "CSS/JS behavior isn't testable in bUnit" convention. **Verified live in the real dev server**: clicking the button flips `data-bs-theme` dark↔light, swaps the icon, changes `document.body`'s computed background color (`#201A19` ↔ `#FFFBFF`, matching `--m3-background`), and persists the choice to `localStorage`. No Domain/Application/Infrastructure/WebAPI change — pure `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin` static assets + one component.
  - **Follow-up, same day (client-reported): `.card.bg-light` illegible in dark theme**. `SheetRuleForm.razor`/`SheetGenerationRuleForm.razor`/`Users.razor` group their nested sub-forms ("Champs du bloc", "Colonnes inconditionnelles", "Colonnes", "Colonnes Points", etc.) inside `<div class="card bg-light">`. Bootstrap's `.bg-light` utility resolves through `--bs-light`/`--bs-light-rgb`, declared once at `:root` in the vendored `bootstrap.css` and **never touched by Bootstrap's own `[data-bs-theme=dark]` block** (only the `-subtle`/`-text-emphasis` variants are) — so the card kept a fixed near-white `#f8f9fa` background in dark theme, while its text still read `--bs-card-color` (`--m3-on-surface`, a *light* color meant for a dark `.card` surface) — light text on a light background. **Fixed** by adding `--m3-surface-rgb` (new token, both theme blocks) and aliasing `--bs-light`/`--bs-light-rgb` onto `--m3-surface`/`--m3-surface-rgb` in both blocks of `theme-m3.css` — the exact same tone a plain `.card` already uses (none of these `.bg-light` cards nest inside a second `.card` in this app, so there's no visual-hierarchy need for a distinct shade). `.bg-dark` (the one other untouched Bootstrap neutral, used only for the `BatchFileStatus.TechnicalError` badge on `ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor`) was checked and found **not** broken — Bootstrap's `.badge` hardcodes `--bs-badge-color: #fff`, always legible against that badge's own dark background regardless of theme. New test `Styling/BgLightDarkThemeContrastTests.cs` (same plain-text-CSS-read + WCAG contrast-ratio convention as `ThemeSecondaryButtonContrastTests.cs`). **Verified live**: a synthetic `.card.bg-light > .card-body` element computes `rgb(245, 221, 218)`/`rgb(32, 26, 25)` (bg/text) in light theme and `rgb(83, 67, 65)`/`rgb(237, 224, 222)` in dark theme — same pairing a plain `.card` already uses, both legible.
  - **Follow-up, same day (client-reported, dark-mode design review): sidebar too bright, "Ajouter la colonne" button clashing, one real CSS bug found in the process**. Two independent client-reported symptoms turned out to have two independent root causes, both real.
    - **Bug 1 (build-breaking typo, unrelated to the sidebar complaint but found while investigating the button)**: `theme-m3.css`'s `[data-bs-theme="dark"]` block had one comment opened with `/*` (documenting the `--m3-secondary-container` tokens) closed with the **HTML** comment marker `-->` instead of CSS's own `*/` — CSS doesn't recognize `-->` as a terminator, so the comment silently swallowed the next 3 declarations (`--m3-secondary-container`/`-rgb`/`--m3-on-secondary-container`) until the next real `*/`, several lines later. Those custom properties then **fell back to the light theme's own values** (declared at `:root`, same specificity, earlier in source order) even under `[data-bs-theme="dark"]` — exactly why `.btn-secondary` ("Ajouter la colonne", "Ajouter le champ", etc., the established Add-button convention since Lot 041/053/058) rendered with light theme's bright cyan container color (`#C4EBFF`) instead of the intended dark navy (`#132831`) while the rest of the page was dark. **Fixed** by replacing `-->` with `*/`. New permanent guard-rail `Styling/ThemeM3CssCommentIntegrityTests.cs`: asserts no literal `-->` anywhere in the file, every `/*` is balanced by a real `*/`, and the dark block's own secondary-container tokens differ from light's (the actual regression this bug caused).
    - **Bug 2 (self-inflicted while fixing bug 3 below, caught before it shipped)**: while rewriting `NavMenu.razor.css`'s active/hover nav-item rule, the new explanatory comment used the shorthand `--m3-sidebar-active-*/-hover-bg` to name two tokens at once — the literal `*/` inside that shorthand closed the CSS comment early, again turning the rest of the sentence into invalid CSS and silently dropping the very `.nav-item ::deep a.active { ... }` rule the comment was documenting. Caught via `grep -c` comment-balance counting (12 `/*` vs 13 `*/`) before ever committing — same class of bug as Bug 1, this time introduced in the same session. Fixed by rewording the comment to avoid a literal `*/` sequence; added a repo-wide sweep (all first-party `.css` files, comment open/close counts) as part of this session's own verification, not as a committed test (no existing convention for a solution-wide CSS lint in this repo).
    - **Design fix (the actual client complaint)**: the sidebar filled with `--m3-primary` (`#FFB4AB` in dark theme — a *light* salmon; M3's dark scheme deliberately keeps `primary` light so it reads as a *text/accent* color on a dark surface, never as a bright fill covering a large area) made the whole sidebar the brightest, most attention-grabbing element on the page — the opposite of dark mode's own elevation model (differentiate surfaces via low luminance, not a saturated/bright fill). **Fixed** by introducing 6 new indirection tokens in `theme-m3.css` (`--m3-sidebar-bg-start`/`-bg-end`/`-fg`/`-active-bg`/`-active-fg`/`-hover-bg`), consumed by `MainLayout.razor.css`'s `.sidebar` rule and every `--m3-on-primary`/`--m3-primary` usage in `NavMenu.razor.css` (hamburger toggler, nav-link default/active/hover, theme-toggle button, version-info text) instead of those two tokens directly. **Light theme reproduces the pre-existing brand-forward sidebar exactly** (identical `color-mix` formulas — zero visual change there, the client never complained about light mode). **Dark theme** now consumes the same near-black `--m3-background` as the page body (the sidebar recedes instead of dominating) and marks the active nav item with `--m3-primary` as a **text accent** (`#FFB4AB`) plus a faint 16% background tint, rather than a solid on-primary fill — M3's own intended dark-theme role for that token, and the same design direction independently suggested by a third-party review Simon relayed. `.card`/`--m3-surface`'s own "muddy brown" tone was investigated too and left unchanged: borders (`--bs-border-color`/`--m3-border`) and card backgrounds were confirmed already correctly wired through the theme hierarchy (background → surface → border, each progressively lighter for visibility) — the perceived "boueux" quality is the algorithmically-correct M3 output for a warm-red seed, expected to read much less jarring now that it's no longer competing with a bright pink sidebar for attention; revisit only if it's still reported as a problem once this fix is live. New test `Styling/SidebarDarkThemeLuminanceTests.cs` covers all 6 tokens in both themes plus the "no more direct `--m3-on-primary` in `NavMenu.razor.css`" invariant. **Verified live in the real dev server** (had to force a hard navigation + fresh fingerprinted asset — the Blazor "Map Static Assets" fingerprint is computed once per server process start, so a stale browser tab loaded before a rebuild will keep serving the *old* fingerprinted bundle even after `preview_stop`/`preview_start`, a real gotcha worth remembering for any future live CSS verification in this project): dark theme's `.sidebar` computes a flat `linear-gradient(rgb(32, 26, 25) 0%, rgb(32, 26, 25) 100%)` (`#201A19`, matching the body), the active nav-link computes `color: rgb(255, 180, 171)` (`#FFB4AB`) with a `16%`-opacity background tint, and `--m3-secondary-container` correctly resolves to `#132831` (not the leaked `#C4EBFF`).
  - **Follow-up, same day (client feedback on the above): sidebar flat-fill (`--m3-background`) made it indistinguishable from the page content behind it**. Correctly diagnosed by the client as dark mode's own classic pitfall — light mode differentiates surfaces with `box-shadow`, which dark mode can't lean on the same way, so two adjacent blocks sharing the *exact* same background read as one flat, undifferentiated surface. M3's own elevation model handles this via *lightness*, not shadow: a raised surface gets very slightly lighter, not darker/shadowed. **Fixed**: `--m3-sidebar-bg-start`/`-bg-end` (dark block only, light theme's brand gradient untouched) changed from a flat `var(--m3-background)` to `color-mix(in srgb, var(--m3-background) 85%, var(--m3-surface) 15%)` — a deliberately modest 15% step toward the (lighter) card-surface tone, not all the way to it, so the sidebar reads as sitting visually "behind" a card rather than at the same elevation. `SidebarDarkThemeLuminanceTests.cs`'s own dark-background test was fixed in place (not duplicated) to assert the new `color-mix(...)` value instead of the flat one, plus two new tests: a numeric guard computing the same `color-mix` math in C# and asserting the result sits strictly between `--m3-background`/`--m3-surface` on every channel (catches a future edit silently drifting back to either endpoint), and a WCAG AA contrast check for `--m3-sidebar-fg` against this new elevated tone.
- **Lot L1 (`NavMenu.razor` admin-link visibility, done — 2026-07-21, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-blazor-navmenu-visibilite-authentification.md`)**: the admin-links block was already wrapped in a single `<AuthorizeView Roles="@IdentitySeeder.AdminRoleName">` (pre-existing from Lot K4, no `<NotAuthorized>` branch) — so admin links were already absent from the DOM for a non-Admin visitor before this lot; no real security regression existed, purely a UI-completeness gap. This lot added: a stable `id` (`nav-{route}-link`) on each of the 5 admin `NavLink`s (`nav-users-link`/`nav-import-profiles-link`/`nav-import-profiles-test-link`/`nav-export-profiles-link`/`nav-export-profiles-test-link` — previously none of `NavMenu.razor`'s links had `id` attributes at all, unlike `Components/Pages/Admin/*.razor`'s `id="..."-button"` convention). L1 also added a `<NotAuthorized>` branch to that same `AuthorizeView` with its own `#nav-login-link` — **this created a duplicate login link, fixed by L2 below, do not reintroduce it.**
- **Lot L2 (correctif, done — 2026-07-21, same ticket doc, opened after manual browser verification of L1)**: two regressions caught by hand in the browser, both traceable to L1's exhaustive search only covering `Authorize(Roles = IdentitySeeder.AdminRoleName)` — too narrow for the rest of `NavMenu.razor`. **Fix 1 (duplicate login link)**: removed the `<NotAuthorized>` branch L1 had added to the Admin-role `AuthorizeView` (reverted to `<Authorized>`-only, 5 ids kept); the single canonical login link is now the **pre-existing** generic `<AuthorizeView>` block's `Account/Login` link (any authenticated user — Profile/Logout/Register/Login), which now carries `id="nav-login-link"` (moved there, not duplicated — exactly one occurrence anywhere in the file, test-verified via `FindAll(...).Should().HaveCount(1)`). **Fix 2 ("Journaux" visible unauthenticated)**: the `logs` link had never been wrapped in any `AuthorizeView` at all (sat above both blocks, always rendered) — an exhaustive re-audit of every `@page`/`@attribute` in `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin` found `Logs.razor` was the **only** menu-linked page with no explicit `@attribute` at all, relying entirely on the app-wide `FallbackPolicy` (`Program.cs:100-105`, `RequireAuthenticatedUser()`, no role) for its real protection — same level as `Profile.razor`, which *does* declare `[Authorize]` explicitly. Fixed both for consistency: added `@attribute [Authorize]` to `Logs.razor` (no server-behavior change, `FallbackPolicy` already covered it — just makes the declared protection match the codebase's own convention of every protected page self-declaring it), and wrapped the `logs` `NavLink` (now `id="nav-logs-link"`) in its own `<AuthorizeView><Authorized>` (no `Roles=`, since its real requirement is "authenticated, any role", not Admin) at its original top-of-menu position. `ImportProfileEditor.razor`/`ExportProfileEditor.razor` confirmed to still have no menu entry at all (unchanged since F1.2/J1/J2, not a gap). Tests (`NavMenuTests.cs`): the 2 pre-existing EN/FR culture tests had a now-incorrect assertion removed (they asserted "Logs"/"Journaux" visible *while unauthenticated*, which directly contradicted fix 2 — this was the bug, not a false test); added `..._ShowsLoginLink_ExactlyOnce`, `..._HidesLogsLink` (unauthenticated), `..._WithoutAdminRole_ShowsLogsLink` (authenticated, no role — same `SetAuthorized`-without-`SetRoles` pattern the pre-existing Logout test already used, not a fabricated non-admin account). Full proof in `docs/etat-avancement-lot-l2-navmenu-correctif-2026-07-21.md`; L1's own proof doc is `docs/etat-avancement-lot-l-navmenu-visibilite-authentification-2026-07-21.md` (still accurate for the 5 admin links + ids, superseded only on the login-link placement).
- As of Milestone 11 (self-service `/profile` page, `Components/Pages/Admin/Profile.razor`): `IUserRepository` (`Application/Identity/`) was extended beyond its original list-only `GetAllAsync` with `GetByIdAsync` (plain EF projection via `IDbContextFactory<ApplicationIdentityDbContext>`, returns nullable `UserProfile`), `UpdateProfileAsync`, and `ChangePasswordAsync` (both backed internally by `UserManager<ApplicationUser>`, added as a second constructor dependency on `UserRepository`). This resolved a pre-existing architectural inconsistency (`Users.razor` went through `IUserRepository` while `Login.razor`/`Register.razor` injected `UserManager<ApplicationUser>` straight from Infrastructure into components) — `Profile.razor` only ever injects `IUserRepository`, never `UserManager` directly. Identity failures (wrong current password, duplicate email, etc.) are surfaced as a new framework-free `IdentityOperationResult(bool Succeeded, IReadOnlyList<string> Errors)` record — `UserRepository` maps `IdentityResult.Errors[].Description` (already localized via the existing `LocalizedIdentityErrorDescriber`) into it, so Application stays free of `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity` types. "User id not found" is treated as a developer invariant (`InvalidOperationException`, out of i18n scope), not a translatable `IdentityOperationResult` failure, since callers always pass the current authenticated user's own id.
- Post-password-change re-authentication: Interactive Server components cannot write the auth cookie mid-circuit (same constraint documented on `IdentityComponentsEndpointRouteBuilderExtensions` for Logout), so `Profile.razor` cannot silently refresh the sign-in after `ChangePasswordAsync` succeeds. Instead it swaps its password form for a success message plus a raw (non-`EditForm`) `<form action="Account/Logout" method="post">` + `<AntiforgeryToken />`, identical to `NavMenu.razor`'s existing logout form — reuses the existing `/Account/Logout` endpoint verbatim, no new backend code.
- `Profile.razor` reads the current user id via `[CascadingParameter] Task<AuthenticationState> AuthState` (first consumer of this cascading value in the app; `AddCascadingAuthenticationState()` was already registered) + `ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier`, not `UserManager.GetUserId(principal)`, to avoid injecting `UserManager` into the component.
- `NavMenu.razor`'s generic `<AuthorizeView><Authorized>` block (any signed-in user, not just admins) now includes a `href="profile"` link (`bi-person-circle-nav-menu` icon) alongside the username display and logout form. **As of Lot M (below), the username display and the Profile link are a single merged element, not two.**
- **Bug fixed while building Milestone 11, found only by manual browser verification, not bUnit**: `StatusMessage.razor`'s `[CascadingParameter] HttpContext HttpContext` threw `NullReferenceException` in `OnInitialized()` when the component is reached via in-circuit client-side navigation (e.g. clicking a `NavLink` to another page while already connected to an established Interactive Server circuit) — `HttpContext` only cascades during the HTTP request that (re)established the circuit, so it's `null` for any later same-circuit navigation. Every prior `<StatusMessage>` usage (`Login.razor`, `Register.razor`) happened to always be reached via a fresh full-page request, so this never surfaced until `Profile.razor` became the first page reached from an in-app `NavLink` that also renders it. Fixed by making the cascading parameter nullable (`HttpContext?`) with null-conditional access; regression-tested in `StatusMessageWithoutHttpContextTests` (a separate `BunitContext` class deliberately *without* the `CascadingValue<HttpContext>` wrapper the other `StatusMessageTests` add). Existing bUnit tests for Login/Register/Profile all explicitly add that wrapper, so none of them would have caught this — worth remembering when writing new tests for any page reachable via in-app navigation that renders `StatusMessage`.
- **Fixed 2026-07-19**: `LoginTests.cs` selected the stale `#Input\.Email` selector (field has been `Input.UserName` since commit `b67ddf5`) and `MainLayoutTests.cs` asserted markup contained "About"/"À propos" chrome that commit `b4caa3b` removed — both were test-side drift, not production bugs. Updated the 3 `LoginTests` selectors to `#Input\.UserName` and dropped the dead "About"/"À propos" assertion from the 2 `MainLayoutTests` (the "unhandled error"/"Reload" assertions in the same tests were already correct and are kept). `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` is now 112/112 green.

The standalone span had no `id` and used the `bi-person-fill-nav-menu` icon; the Profile `NavLink` itself had no `id` either (the ticket's assumed `nav-profile-link` didn't actually exist yet — verified by reading the file before coding, per the ticket's own instruction) — `nav-profile-link` was added fresh here, matching Lot L's `nav-{route}-link` convention, not "reused" in the literal sense the ticket phrased it. `bi-person-fill-nav-menu`'s CSS rule in `NavMenu.razor.css` was removed as an orphan (last markup reference gone) — no rule references it anymore. - **`ImportProfileEditor.razor` labels + inline sheet-rule editing, done — 2026-07-21, ticket doc `docs/ticket-profil-import-edition.md`**: the 3 root-field inputs (`profile-name-input`/`profile-repere-prefix-input`/`profile-equipement-type-element-nom-input`) gained `<label class="form-label" for="...">` elements (new `.resx` keys `ImportProfileEditor_NameLabel`/`ReperePrefixLabel`/`EquipementTypeElementNomLabel`, following the `Login.razor`/`Profile.razor` label convention — placeholders were left as-is, separate from the new labels). Existing sheet rules, previously plain read-only text with no way to edit/delete them, now get a "Modify"/"Delete" button each (`modify-sheet-rule-button-{index}`/`delete-sheet-rule-button-{index}`); Modify swaps that one `<li>` into an edit form pre-filled from the rule (`_editingIndex` tracks at most one rule in edit mode at a time), Delete removes it from the in-memory `_sheetRules` list immediately (no confirmation step, matching this page's existing no-confirmation pattern for other actions).
  - **New shared component, `SheetRuleForm.razor`** (colocated next to `ImportProfileEditor.razor`, since nothing else uses it): the sheet-rule sub-form (locator fields, block-field/unconditional-colonne/point-rule add-lists) used to live only inline in the "Add a sheet rule" card; it's now a single component reused for both the always-present "Add" card (`IdPrefix=""`, so its element ids are unchanged from before this ticket — `sheet-rule-name-input` etc. — no existing test needed updating) and each rule's edit-mode instance (`IdPrefix="edit-{index}-"`, e.g. `edit-0-sheet-rule-name-input`, freshly mounted per `@if (_editingIndex == index)` block so "Cancel" needs no explicit revert logic — the component is simply unmounted, discarding its in-progress edits, and a fresh instance re-reads the untouched original rule if "Modify" is clicked again). Submitting (`SubmitButtonId`/`SubmitLabel` differ per usage: `add-sheet-rule-button`/"Add sheet rule" vs. `save-sheet-rule-button-{index}`/"Save changes") builds a `SheetExtractionRule` via the Domain constructors (same validation/error-display as before) and invokes `EventCallback<SheetExtractionRule> OnSubmit` — the parent either appends it (`HandleAddSheetRule`) or replaces the existing list entry in place (`HandleSaveSheetRule(index, updated)`, also closes edit mode), so "Enregistrer le profil" (unchanged) picks up whichever `_sheetRules` state resulted.
  - **Real bug caught by the ticket's own tests, not a rewrite-only refactor**: `SheetExtractionRule.UnconditionalColonneNames` stores its constructor argument **by reference** (unlike `PointRules`, which the constructor copies via `[.. pointRules]` — see the type's own Domain source comment). `SheetRuleForm`'s `Submit()` originally passed its own mutable `_unconditionalColonneNames` list straight into the constructor, then called `ResetForm()` (which `.Clear()`s that same list) right after — silently wiping the just-submitted rule's colonne names for both the Add and Edit paths. Caught by `AddSheetRule_WithValidInput_DisplaysSheetSummaryAndResetsForm` (pre-existing test, unmodified) failing after the initial extraction. Fixed by passing a snapshot (`[.. _unconditionalColonneNames]`) into the `SheetExtractionRule` constructor instead of the live list.
  - Tests (`ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`, +12 vs. before this ticket): label-association, modify-button presence, edit-mode-affects-only-that-rule (2 sheet rules), full-field prefill (locator + block field + unconditional colonne + conditional point rule), save-updates-in-place-without-duplicating, cancel-restores-original-values, delete-removes-from-list, and a genuine reload-based persistence test (`SaveProfile_PersistsEditedSheetRule_VisibleAfterReload`, renders a **second**, fresh `ImportProfileEditor` instance against the same profile Id rather than just re-querying the store, per the ticket's "recharge les données" wording). All pre-existing tests for this page pass unmodified. **Not done, per the ticket's own "(si pertinent)" hedge**: no per-item removal of an individual block field / unconditional colonne / conditional point rule while building up a sheet rule (add-only, matching the pre-existing "Add a sheet rule" card's own long-standing limitation — never had removal either).
  - **Follow-up, same day**: the "(si pertinent)" block-field removal hedge above was resolved once the user asked for it directly, live in the browser (screenshot of the PROCEDURE rule's edit form with an unlabeled locator-field row and a plain read-only "Champs du bloc" list). Two additions, both extending the same reuse pattern rather than inventing a new one: (1) the `SheetRuleForm`'s 4 root locator inputs (`sheet-rule-name-input`/`-first-block-start-row-input`/`-step-input`/`-stop-field-name-input`, and their `edit-{index}-`-prefixed edit-mode counterparts) gained `<label>`s too, via 4 new `.resx` keys (`ImportProfileEditor_SheetNameLabel`/`FirstBlockStartRowLabel`/`StepLabel`/`StopFieldNameLabel` — deliberately separate from the pre-existing same-text placeholder keys, matching how the 3 root-profile-field labels were kept separate from their placeholders above). (2) A second new shared component, **`BlockFieldForm.razor`** (same file location, same add-vs-edit reuse shape as `SheetRuleForm.razor` itself — `IdPrefix`/`InitialField`/`SubmitButtonId`/`SubmitLabel`/`ShowCancel`/`CancelButtonId`/`OnCancel`/`OnSubmit<BlockFieldDefinition>`), lets each already-added `BlockFieldDefinition` inside a `SheetRuleForm` (whether that `SheetRuleForm` itself is in Add or Edit mode) get its own Modify/Delete/Save/Cancel — a third, nested level of the same "list of editable items" pattern used for sheet rules. Id scheme composes rather than repeats: the add-new-field row keeps its pre-existing id (`{outerPrefix}block-field-name-input`, via `BlockFieldForm IdPrefix="{outerPrefix}block-field-"`), while editing already-added field `j` uses `{outerPrefix}block-field-{j}-` as `BlockFieldForm`'s `IdPrefix` (e.g. top-level Add form editing field 0 → `block-field-0-name-input`; inside sheet-rule edit-mode 0 editing its field 2 → `edit-0-block-field-2-name-input`) — reuses `ImportProfileEditor_ModifyButton`/`DeleteButton`/`SaveChangesButton`/`CancelButton` verbatim, no new resx keys needed for those. `SheetRuleForm.AddBlockField` simplified from doing its own Domain-constructor try/catch to a one-line `_fields.Add(field)`, since `BlockFieldForm` now owns that validation/error-display itself (mirrors how `SheetRuleForm.Submit` already owns the sheet-rule-level validation). **Razor gotcha hit while building this**: putting a bare `@field.Name (...)` expression directly inside an `@if`/`else` block with sibling `<button>` elements (no wrapping tag of its own) produced nonsensical parser errors (`CS8802`/`CS9348`, "top-level statements", markup read as C#) — Razor needs an explicit element boundary around loose interpolated text sitting next to markup siblings; fixed by wrapping it in `<span>...</span>`. Tests (`ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`, +8): labels on both add- and edit-mode locator inputs, per-field modify/delete-button presence, prefill-on-modify, save-updates-field-in-place-without-duplicating, cancel-discards, delete-removes, and one true nested-edit-then-persist test (edits a field inside an already-in-edit-mode sheet rule, saves the field, saves the rule, saves the profile, reads back from the store). Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 102/102 green (was 94 before the first round of this ticket, 102 after both rounds).
- **Lot N (`ImportProfileEditor.razor` block-field ranges shown/entered as absolute Excel coordinates, done — 2026-07-22, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-blazor-profil-import-lisibilite-plages-excel.md`)**: client feedback on a real ISOLEMENT screenshot — `TypeElement (B:E, 3-4)`'s raw `RowOffsetStart`/`RowOffsetEnd` (relative to the rule's `FirstBlockStartRow`) meant nothing to someone reading a cell reference in the real source file. Domain's `BlockFieldDefinition` is unchanged (still relative offsets — `Step` needs that to project onto every following block); only the Blazor presentation layer translates, both ways, both in the read-only list and the add/edit form (one representation everywhere, per the ticket's own explicit decision against showing offsets in one place and absolute ranges in another).
  - **New pure utility, `src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Formatting/BlockFieldRangeFormatter.cs`** (+ `BlockFieldRangeParseResult.cs`, same folder) — deliberately BlazorAdmin-layer, not Domain: this is a presentation concept `BlockFieldDefinition` has no business knowing about, consistent with the project's "architecture by relevance, not mimicry" precedent already noted elsewhere. `ToAbsoluteRange(firstBlockStartRow, columnRange, rowOffsetStart, rowOffsetEnd)` is a plain static string builder (single-column + equal offsets → single-cell notation `"B9"`, everything else → `"B19:E20"`). `FromAbsoluteRange(absoluteRange, firstBlockStartRow)` returns a `BlockFieldRangeParseResult` (a Result type, not a thrown exception — the ticket's own specified signature; this is a third, ticket-mandated exception to the "no generic Result pattern" rule, but scoped to this one BlazorAdmin-only presentation type, not a precedent for Domain/Application) with two independent validation levels: level 1 (blocking) rejects anything outside a real workbook's technical bounds (column `A`-`XFD`/1-16384, row `1`-`1 048 576`) or with the end before the start (row or column); level 2 (non-blocking) sets `IsBeyondPracticalRange` when column exceeds `AZ`(52) or row exceeds `1000` — arbitrary, client-confirmed thresholds based on the real fixtures observed to date (rightmost column `U`, highest start row `19`), kept as separate named constants from the level-1 Excel-format constants specifically so a future adjustment to the threshold is never confused with an actual workbook limit. Column-letter comparison goes through a base-26 `ColumnToNumber` conversion (not string comparison — `"Z" < "AA"` numerically but not lexicographically).
  - Tests (`tests/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests/Formatting/BlockFieldRangeFormatterTests.cs`, pure xUnit, no bUnit needed): the ticket's own table cases (`Identification`→`B19:E20`, `TypeElement`→`B22:E23`, negative-offset `Designation`→`H18:U19`, single-cell), plus the most important regression guard of this lot — a round-trip test (`ToAbsoluteRange` then `FromAbsoluteRange` must reproduce the exact original `ColumnRange`/`RowOffsetStart`/`RowOffsetEnd`) run against **every block field of all 6 sheets of the real seeded default import profile** (`DefaultProfileSeeder`, run against the real EF InMemory provider via the project's existing `TestDbContextFactory`, not hand-built) — proves the conversion never silently drifts a coordinate already validated in production. Plus blocking-error cases (invalid format, reversed row/column order, column beyond `XFD`, row beyond `1 048 576`, row `0`) and the exact `AZ`/`BA`/`1000`/`1001` plausibility-threshold boundary cases from the ticket.
  - **`ImportProfileEditor.razor`/`SheetRuleForm.razor` (N2)**: both the top-level read-only sheet-rule display and `SheetRuleForm`'s own already-added-fields list (visible while a sheet rule — Add or Edit — is open) now render `BlockFieldRangeFormatter.ToAbsoluteRange(...)` instead of `{ColumnRange}, {RowOffsetStart}-{RowOffsetEnd}`; no HTML id changes, pure text-output change. Verified live against the real seeded profile in the dev server: `ISOLEMENT`'s `Identification`/`TypeElement`/`Designation` render exactly `B19:E20`/`B22:E23`/`H18:U19` (the negative-offset case), matching the ticket's own worked example verbatim.
  - **`BlockFieldForm.razor` (N3)**: the 3 inputs (`{prefix}column-range-input`/`{prefix}row-offset-start-input`/`{prefix}row-offset-end-input`) collapsed into one text field, `{prefix}absolute-range-input` (new `<label>`, `.resx` key `ImportProfileEditor_AbsoluteRangeLabel`/placeholder `ImportProfileEditor_AbsoluteRangePlaceholder`), plus a new required `[Parameter] public int FirstBlockStartRow` (supplied by the parent `SheetRuleForm`'s own `_firstBlockStartRow`, live-bound so it reflects whatever the user has currently typed, not just the value at mount time). Edit-mode prefill now calls `ToAbsoluteRange` in `OnInitialized` instead of copying the raw offset properties. `Submit()` calls `FromAbsoluteRange` first: failure → new `.resx` key `ImportProfileEditor_InvalidExcelRangeError` (one shared blocking-error message covering every rejection reason — invalid format, reversed range, out-of-Excel-bounds — per the ticket's own singular-key wording), no `BlockFieldDefinition` constructed, no `OnSubmit` invoked; success → build `BlockFieldDefinition` from the parsed `ColumnRange`/offsets (still inside the pre-existing `DomainValidationException` catch, for the `Name`-empty case), and if `IsBeyondPracticalRange` show a **separate, non-blocking** `.resx`-keyed warning (`ImportProfileEditor_ExcelRangeBeyondPracticalRangeWarning`) alongside still submitting/saving the field. Verified live in the dev server: opening ISOLEMENT's `TypeElement` field in edit mode prefills the text input with exactly `B22:E23` (confirmed via the rendered `edit-1-block-field-3-absolute-range-input` element's value), matching N3's worked example.
  - **Hors périmètre, explicitly deferred by the ticket**: `ExportProfileEditor.razor` (Lot J) has the likely-same readability defect but uses an unrelated model (`ColumnDefinition`/`PivotFieldRef`, not `BlockFieldDefinition`) — left untouched pending client validation of this lot on the import side first.
  - Tests (`ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`): every existing block-field test rewritten for the single-input UI (id changes only, same assertions in spirit); added the N2 seeded-ISOLEMENT absolute-range display test, the N3 `B19:E20`→offsets(0,1) round-trip-through-the-UI test, the TypeElement `B22:E23` prefill test, invalid-format/reversed-range rejection, out-of-Excel-bounds rejection, and the `BA1` beyond-practical-threshold warning-but-still-saved case. Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 127/127 green (was 102 before this lot; +25 across the new `BlockFieldRangeFormatterTests` file and the rewritten/added `ImportProfileEditorTests`).
