  - **O1 — `SheetRuleForm.razor`'s block-field list** (the only place per-field Modify/Delete buttons exist — confirmed by reading the file first, per the ticket's own instruction; the read-only summary list in `ImportProfileEditor.razor` has no per-field buttons at all, only a single Modify/Delete per whole sheet rule): each `<li>` restructured from one `<span>Name (Range)</span>` + two text buttons into `<div class="block-field-info">` (name in a bold `.block-field-name` span, range on its own line in a `.block-field-range` span carrying Bootstrap's built-in `text-muted font-monospace` utility classes — no new CSS custom properties or font stack introduced, `--bs-font-monospace` already ships with the project's vendored Bootstrap) + `.block-field-actions` (two square 34px icon-only buttons, `btn-outline-secondary`/`btn-outline-danger`, inline SVGs matching Bootstrap Icons' `bi-pencil-square`/`bi-trash` shapes with `fill="currentColor"` so they tint with the button's own state color — no bootstrap-icons font/CSS ever referenced anywhere in this project, so this follows the same "inline SVG shape, not a font dependency" convention `NavMenu.razor.css` already established). New scoped `SheetRuleForm.razor.css` adds the flex layout, the `border-bottom` row separator, and the 34px icon-button sizing. **Ids unchanged** (`{prefix}modify-block-field-button-{index}`/`{prefix}delete-block-field-button-{index}`, confirmed by reading the file before touching it), `aria-label` added to both buttons since their text content is gone. 3 pre-existing tests (`BlockField_SaveChanges_UpdatesFieldInPlace_AndClosesEditMode`, `BlockField_Cancel_DiscardsChanges`, `BlockField_Delete_RemovesFieldFromList`) had asserted on the old concatenated `"Name (Range)"` markup string — rewritten to assert on `.block-field-name`/`.block-field-range` separately, per the ticket's own "two separate selectors, not one concatenated string" test guidance. Added `BlockField_DisplaysNameAndRangeAsSeparateElements_WithMonospaceRangeClass` (against the existing `BuildProfileWithIsolementSheetRule` fixture, ticket's own N0 example) and `BlockField_IconButtons_HaveAriaLabels`.
  - **O2 — `ImportProfileEditor.razor`'s read-only sheet-rule summary**: unconditional-colonne names, previously one comma-joined `<p>`, are now a `<ul><li>` (same shape as the point-rules list right below it) preceded by the `UnconditionalColonnesHeading` label reused verbatim from the edit form; the point-rules list gains the same treatment with the `PointRulesHeading` label. **Both label+list pairs are conditional on their own collection being non-empty** — verified live against the real seeded default profile: PLATINES/ORIFICES CAPACITES (no `ConditionalPointRule`s) show only the unconditional-colonnes label, DIVERS (no unconditional colonnes, all-conditional sheet) shows only the point-rules label, PROCEDURE (neither) shows neither. Added 3 tests scoped to the summary `<li class="list-group-item">` specifically via `QuerySelectorAll` (not `cut.Markup.Should().Contain(...)`) — the always-visible "Add a sheet rule" card at the bottom of the page renders a `SheetRuleForm` whose own two headings are unconditional, so a page-wide markup search would have falsely passed even before O2's conditional-rendering fix; this was caught during implementation, not anticipated up front.

O2's conditional labels confirmed against the full 6-sheet real profile via `read_page`'s accessibility tree.

  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: `ExportProfileEditor.razor`'s likely-identical styling gap, deferred pending client validation on the import side (same deferral pattern as Lot N).
  - **Follow-up, same day (client feedback on a live screenshot, not a new ticket doc)**: two visual gaps O1 didn't close. (1) The read-only sheet-rule summary's field ranges (`ImportProfileEditor.razor`) still used the old plain `Name (Range)` inline text — visually inconsistent side by side with the edit form's new two-level/monospace styling right above it in the same screenshot. Fixed by rendering the summary's field list with the exact same `.block-field-item`/`.block-field-name`/`.block-field-range` markup as `SheetRuleForm.razor`'s own list (minus the per-field buttons, which the summary never had). (2) `SheetRuleForm`'s Save/Cancel (and Add) buttons were left-aligned, inconsistent with the right-aligned per-field icon buttons above them. **Discovered while fixing (1)**: Blazor CSS isolation scopes a `.razor.css` file's rules to only that component's own render output (a distinct `b-xxxxx` attribute per component) — reusing `.block-field-*` class names in `ImportProfileEditor.razor`'s markup would not have picked up `SheetRuleForm.razor.css`'s scoped rules at all. Fixed by moving every `.block-field-*` rule (plus a new right-aligned-actions helper class for the button fix — **renamed to `.right-aligned-actions` by Lot P below**, originally `.sheet-rule-form-actions`) from the now-deleted `SheetRuleForm.razor.css` into global `wwwroot/app.css`, since the same visual language is now genuinely shared by two different components — this is the only such shared-across-components CSS in `BlazorAdmin` today, every other page/component still keeps its own scoped `.razor.css` (`Logs.razor.css`, `NavMenu.razor.css`, etc.), so don't default to "put it in app.css" for anything that stays single-component. Both Save/Cancel and the standalone Add button (in the always-present "Add a sheet rule" card) are now wrapped in the right-aligned actions div. Verified live in the dev server (real seeded profile): the summary's `Action`/`C9:L9` now computes the same `font-family`/muted color as edit mode's fields, and both the Add-card button and the edit-mode Save/Cancel pair sit inside a `justify-content: flex-end` container. Added 3 tests to `ImportProfileEditorTests.cs` (`Summary_DisplaysFieldNameAndRangeAsSeparateElements_WithMonospaceRangeClass_LikeEditMode`, `SheetRuleForm_AddModeActionButton_IsInRightAlignedContainer`, `SheetRuleForm_EditModeActionButtons_AreInRightAlignedContainer`); full suite 135/135 green (was 132).
- **Lot P (per-sheet-rule cards + right-aligned action buttons, done — 2026-07-22, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-blazor-profil-import-cartes-regles-feuille.md`, plus `docs/convention-ui-blazor-alignement-boutons.md`)**: closes a client-reported gap in the summary view left by N/O — sheet rules (`PROCEDURE`, `ISOLEMENT`, `PLATINES`, …) were separated only by the same thin rule used *inside* a rule between fields, with no visual card boundary and no typographic hierarchy between the sheet name and its metadata.
  - **New convention doc, `docs/convention-ui-blazor-alignement-boutons.md`** (referenced by this ticket, not written by it — read first per the user's instruction): formalizes "every action-button row is right-aligned within its enclosing container" as a standing rule for all of `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin`, not just this screen. Backed by a single global CSS class, **`.right-aligned-actions`** (`wwwroot/app.css`, `display: flex; justify-content: flex-end; gap: 0.5rem;`) — **renamed from Lot O's follow-up's `.sheet-rule-form-actions`**, since that name was already too narrowly scoped to "sheet rule form" the moment a second, unrelated context (the P1 card's Modify/Delete, P2's root `save-profile-button`) needed the identical treatment. All 3 usages (`SheetRuleForm.razor`'s Save/Cancel/Add row, the new per-card Modify/Delete row, `save-profile-button`'s wrapper) share this one class.
  - **P1 — sheet-rule-card**: each non-editing rule in the summary (`ImportProfileEditor.razor`) is now `<li class="sheet-rule-card">` (bordered, rounded, padded) inside `<ul class="sheet-rule-list">` (`display:flex; flex-direction:column; gap:1.5rem` — a real gap between cards, 3× the 0.5rem internal padding used between block fields inside one card, so the eye reads card boundaries unambiguously per the ticket's own requirement). A `<div class="sheet-rule-card-header">` holds the sheet name in a bold `<h4 class="sheet-rule-card-title">` (page heading hierarchy is now h1 → h3 "Sheet rules" → h4 sheet name → h5 "Unconditional colonnes"/"Conditional point rules" from O2 — consistent, no level skipped) next to a `<span class="sheet-rule-card-meta text-muted">` for the row/step/stop-field metadata. **Resolves the ticket's own open question by reading the code first, as instructed**: a rule being edited is *replaced in place* by `SheetRuleForm` inside the same `<li>` (now `class="sheet-rule-editing-item"`, no card border) — it was never duplicated into the summary while its edit panel is open, confirmed by a new regression test (`EditingRule_IsNotRenderedAsACardOrDuplicatedInTheSummary`) rather than left as a hypothetical.
  - **Split resource key**: `ImportProfileEditor_SheetSummary` (`"{0} -- start row {1}, step {2}, stop field '{3}'"`, one string mixing the sheet name into the same template as its metadata) is **replaced**, not kept alongside, by `ImportProfileEditor_SheetMetadata` (`"start row {0}, step {1}, stop field '{2}'"`, no name placeholder) — the sheet name itself is rendered directly from `rule.SheetName` (a raw field value, not a template), consistent with O1's precedent of not localizing plain data. `ImportProfileEditor_SheetSummary` had exactly one call site (this one), so removed outright rather than left as an orphaned key, matching Lot K4's precedent of pruning unused resource entries rather than accumulating them.
  - **P2 — `save-profile-button`**: wrapped in its own `.right-aligned-actions` div, no id/behavior change. Explicitly not a "sheet-rule-card" concern (it belongs to the root profile form), but governed by the same convention doc.

Only `confirm-delete-sheet-rule-button-{index}` actually calls `DeleteSheetRule(index)`. **Researched first, per this project's "no existing pattern → don't invent one blindly" habit**: the codebase has zero prior confirmation-dialog precedent (no Bootstrap modal, no `window.confirm`, `ReconnectModal.razor`'s `<dialog>` is framework-owned reconnect UI, not reusable) and is on record (Lot K4 notes) as deliberately avoiding JS interop beyond `Logs.razor`'s one clipboard call — so this uses the same **inline two-step state toggle** idiom already established throughout this exact file (`_editingIndex`, `_pendingDeleteIndex` now sibling fields), not a new interop dependency. The exact causal mechanism (stale server binary vs. a replayed/duplicated click during a circuit reconnect) was not conclusively identified, and wasn't worth over-investigating on a pre-prod-only database. **Fix, user-approved before executing** (a destructive action, not something to silently self-authorize even when self-caused): `DELETE FROM ImportProfiles WHERE Id = '...'` via `sqlcmd` (FK cascade removed the remaining 4 rows' children automatically, per the existing cascade-delete mapping), then one fresh app startup let `DefaultProfileSeeder` recreate the profile from scratch — verified restored to all 6 rows again via direct `sqlcmd`, not through the same unreliable UI that surfaced the problem. **Takeaway for future sessions**: when this Browser pane preview infrastructure is visibly flaky (servers dying within seconds, a tab's URL going blank mid-click), prefer verifying structural/behavioral correctness through the bUnit suite over live-clicking against the real seeded dev profile — and if live verification is still needed, use a disposable throwaway profile (created via `/import-profiles/new`, never saved) rather than the shared seeded one, exactly as this session ultimately did once the risk became clear.

Pure presentation, no Domain/Application change, applied to `ImportProfileEditor.razor`/`ExportProfileEditor.razor` simultaneously per the ticket's own explicit parity requirement — confirmed first (per the ticket's own "check before choosing an approach" instruction) that `.sheet-rule-card`/`.block-field-list`/`.right-aligned-actions` all already live in the shared global `wwwroot/app.css` (Lot O/P's own doing), not duplicated per component, so R1/R2's CSS changes are each a single edit that applies to both editors identically with no risk of drift.
  - **R1**: `<ul class="sheet-rule-list">` gained a second class, `sheet-rule-grid` (`display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(480px, 1fr)); gap: 1.5rem;`, `app.css`) — kept as a class distinct from `.sheet-rule-list` itself (which stays just the list-reset rules) specifically so a dedicated selector exists for the ticket's own "assert on the class, not a visual render" test requirement. `.sheet-rule-editing-item` (the in-place edit form swapped in for a card) gained `grid-column: 1 / -1` so an open edit form isn't squeezed into a single ~480px column.
  - **R2**: the read-only summary's field/column list — `Locator.Fields` on the import side, the combined `ColumnDefinitions`+`PointColumnDefinitions` list on the export side — gained a second class, `block-field-grid` (`display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(220px, 1fr));`, border-bottom removed per item). **Deliberately scoped to only the read-only `.sheet-rule-card` summary**, per the ticket's own "à l'intérieur d'une .sheet-rule-card" wording — `SheetRuleForm.razor`/`SheetGenerationRuleForm.razor`'s own always-editable add/edit lists (which reuse the same `.block-field-list` class) were **not** given the grid class and keep their pre-existing one-per-row layout; those forms' own tests needed no changes.
  - **R3**: `UnconditionalColonneNames`+`PointRules` (import) / `ColumnDefinitions`+`PointColumnDefinitions` (export) are now collapsed behind a `<details class="sheet-rule-sublist-details"><summary>` per card, always closed by default, guarded by `if (either collection non-empty)`. **The full list markup is genuinely absent from the DOM while collapsed** (a component-state `HashSet<int> _expandedSheetRuleDetails` + `@if` around the content — not `<details>`'s own native open/closed CSS, which would leave the children in the DOM either way) — same "FindAll empty, not a display:none check" rule as L2/NavMenu, per the ticket's explicit test requirement. The `<summary>` binds `@onclick`+`@onclick:preventDefault="true"` to a `ToggleSheetRuleDetails(index)` toggle rather than letting the browser's native `<details>` open/close state drive anything, avoiding a fight between Blazor's diffing and the browser's own toggle. New resx keys `ImportProfileEditor_SheetRuleDetailsSummary` (`"{0} unconditional colonnes, {1} conditional rules"`) / `ExportProfileEditor_SheetRuleDetailsSummary` (`"{0} columns, {1} point columns"`) — deliberately different wording (there's no "unconditional/conditional" concept on the export side) but the same two-count `"{0} X, {1} Y"` shape, satisfying the ticket's "même structure de résumé" parity requirement without forcing identical vocabulary onto a different domain model. Both counts are read live off the rule's own collections on every render (no cached/first-render value), so the summary text updates immediately after an in-place edit adds/removes an item, before the profile itself is saved.

Tests scoped to the always-visible add/edit forms (`SheetRuleForm`/`SheetGenerationRuleForm`/`BlockFieldForm`/`ColumnDefinitionForm`/`PointColumnDefinitionForm`, and any test that clicks "Modify" to enter edit mode) needed no changes at all — R3 only touches the non-editing read-only summary.

- **Ticket R3 (correctif): `sheet-rule-sublist-details` accordion reported "does nothing" when clicked in a real (Visual Studio-launched) browser, done — 2026-07-23, ticket doc `docs/ticket-r3-accordeon-sous-listes.md`**. Investigated by driving the real dev server (`dotnet run`, HTTPS Kestrel, real EF/LocalDB) through the Browser pane and dispatching genuine mouse clicks (not bUnit's direct-handler-invocation `.Click()`, which bypasses the browser's own default-action/event-dispatch pipeline entirely) against a throwaway duplicate of the seeded default import profile (`0ee23ac0-79e1-462c-9ae6-ccab7146914d`, named "Profil OXO standard (Copy)" — **left in the local LocalDB, not deleted**, since SQL `DELETE` was denied by the auto-mode classifier as a destructive action outside this session's write authority; harmless clutter, safe to remove by hand via SSMS/`sqlcmd` if desired). **Root cause was not a click-wiring defect**: the ISOLEMENT card's toggle opened and closed correctly on repeated real clicks, proving the `@onclick`/`@onclick:preventDefault` + `HashSet<int> _expandedSheetRuleDetails` mechanism (Lot R3) works as designed in an actual browser, not just under bUnit. The **actual, reproducible gap** was PROCEDURE-shaped sheet rules (`UnconditionalColonneNames.Count == 0 && PointRules.Count == 0`, true for the seeded profile's PROCEDURE rule): the outer `@if` guard omitted the whole `<details>` block for these, so there was **no element at all** to click — indistinguishable from "clicking does nothing" to a user who doesn't know that specific card has zero sub-items. This matched the ticket's own Étape 3 edge case ("une sous-liste vide... affiche un état vide cohérent") almost exactly, and is the most likely real trigger for the report.
  - **Fix, both `ImportProfileEditor.razor` and `ExportProfileEditor.razor` (kept in parity, per this project's established convention even though the ticket only named the import page)**: removed the outer `@if (collections non-empty)` guard around the `<details>` — it's now **always rendered**. When both collections are empty, the expanded content shows a new localized message instead of the unconditional-colonnes/point-rules lists (`ImportProfileEditor_NoSheetRuleSublistItems` / `ExportProfileEditor_NoSheetRuleSublistItems`, EN/FR). The pre-existing `Summary_WithNeitherCollectionPopulated_ShowsNeitherLabel` test (Lot O2) needed no change — it only asserts zero `<h5>` headings in the empty case, still true (the new empty-state text is a plain `<p>`, no heading).
  - **Accessibility fix, Refactor step's explicit ask**: the `<details>` element's `open` attribute is now bound to `_expandedSheetRuleDetails.Contains(index)` (`open="@_expandedSheetRuleDetails.Contains(index)"`, Blazor's compile-time-inferred boolean-attribute rendering — present when true, omitted when false). Previously `open` was never set by anything (the native toggle that would set it is blocked by `@onclick:preventDefault`, and the C# state only drove the separate `@if` around the content div) — sighted users saw correct behavior via the conditional content, but assistive technology relying on `<details>`'s native open/closed semantics had no signal at all.
  - **New tests** (`ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`/`ExportProfileEditorTests.cs`, mirrored): bidirectional toggle (click twice collapses again — previously untested, though the `HashSet.Add`/`Remove` toggle logic already made this trivially correct), two-card independence (expanding one card's disclosure leaves a second card's collapsed — previously untested, `HashSet<int>` membership already made this correct by construction), empty-sublist still renders a working toggle + shows the coherent empty-state message, and the `open` attribute reflects expanded/collapsed state. Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 202/202 green (was 192; +10, 5 per editor), 0 removed.
  - **`ProfileEditorParityTests.cs` needed no changes** — its R3 fixtures (`BuildImportProfileWithTwoSheetRules`/`BuildExportProfileWithTwoSheetRules`) both use non-empty sublists, so the always-render change doesn't affect their assertions.
- **Bug fix: `/import-profiles/test` and `/export-profiles/test` threw "Synchronous reads are not supported." on every real upload, done — 2026-07-23**. Root cause: `IBrowserFile.OpenReadStream()` returns a stream backed by the live Interactive Server circuit — it only supports the async `ReadAsync` overloads, since the upload streams in over SignalR rather than being a real seekable file. `new ClosedXmlWorkbookReader(stream)` hands that stream straight to `XLWorkbook`'s constructor, which opens the `.xlsx` as a zip package via synchronous `Stream.Read()` calls — throwing the instant it touches a real browser upload. **Never caught by bUnit**: `InputFileContent`'s test double is backed by a plain byte array and supports synchronous reads fine, so all existing `ImportProfileTestTests`/`ExportProfileTestTests` passed both before and after this fix — this class of bug is only reproducible against a real browser (or a hand-built fake stream that actually rejects sync reads, which is what the regression test below does).
  - **Fix**: new `src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Excel/BrowserFileStreamBuffering.cs` — `BufferToSeekableStreamAsync(Stream)` copies the browser stream into a `MemoryStream` via `CopyToAsync` (the one operation the browser stream does support) before it's ever handed to `ClosedXmlWorkbookReader`. Both `ImportProfileTest.razor` and `ExportProfileTest.razor`'s `OnInputFileChangeAsync` now buffer before constructing the reader. WebAPI's `OxoController` needed no change — `IFormFile.OpenReadStream()` is already fully buffered to memory/disk by multipart model binding before the controller runs, so it never hit this.
  - **Regression test**: `tests/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests/Excel/BrowserFileStreamBufferingTests.cs` — a hand-rolled `AsyncOnlyReadStream` whose synchronous `Read`/`ReadByte` overloads throw `NotSupportedException("Synchronous reads are not supported.")` (mirroring the real browser stream), proving `BufferToSeekableStreamAsync` never touches them. Deliberately a unit test on the extracted helper, not a bUnit reproduction through the two pages — bUnit's `InputFile` test double can't be made to reject sync reads without bypassing bUnit's own upload helpers entirely, and the helper is where the actual fix lives. Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 203/203 green (was 202; +1).
- **Back-to-list button on `ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor`, done — 2026-07-23**: same pattern as the editors' own `back-to-import-profiles-button`/`back-to-export-profiles-button` (see the "Back to list" bullet above) — `#back-to-import-profiles-button` → `import-profiles`, `#back-to-export-profiles-button` → `export-profiles`, both via a newly-injected `NavigationManager` and a new page-scoped resx key each (`ImportProfileTest_BackToListButton`/`ExportProfileTest_BackToListButton`, EN "Back to list"/FR "Retour à la liste" — kept separate per-page even though the wording matches the editors', consistent with this file's established per-page-key convention). Reusing the same element id across the editor and test page for the same profile type is safe — they're different routes, never rendered together. Tests: `BackToListButton_NavigatesToImportProfileList`/`BackToListButton_NavigatesToExportProfileList` in `ImportProfileTestTests.cs`/`ExportProfileTestTests.cs` (the latter file gained a synchronous `WithCulture` helper alongside its pre-existing `WithCultureAsync`, mirroring the import side's file). Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 205/205 green (was 203; +2).
- **Lot T (`PivotSource.TacheMultiple`, dynamic per-code target-workbook sheets, done — 2026-07-23, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-export-taches-multiples.md`)**: extends the export/generation side (Lot I/J) to cover `TacheMultiplePivot`, explicitly out of scope when Lot I/J were built. Implemented T1→T8 in the ticket's own recommended order, one commit per ticket (T8 added post-delivery, see below).
  - **T1/T2 (Domain)**: `PivotSource` gained `TacheMultiple`; `PivotFieldRef` gained 5 members (`TacheMultipleOrdre`/`Action`/`Acteur`/`Risques`/`DateValidation` — no `TypeTacheMultipleCode` member, since that field drives sheet *grouping*, not a column, and no `EstFactice` member, never displayed). `PivotFieldResolver` gained a third `Resolve(TacheMultiplePivot, PivotFieldRef)` overload (`Ordre`/`DateValidation` render via `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`, `DateValidation` as `dd/MM/yyyy` matching `ProcedureExtractionService`'s own convention; both render `""` when null). `SheetGenerationRule`'s existing cross-validation (`GetPivotSource(column.Source) != pivotSource`) already rejected `Equipement`/`Isolement` columns under `PivotSource.TacheMultiple` with zero code changes — the check was already fully generic. Added one genuinely new invariant: any `PointColumnDefinition` under `PivotSource.TacheMultiple` throws a dedicated `DomainRuleViolationException` (`SheetGenerationRule_PointColumnDefinitionsNotAllowedForTacheMultiple`) — a TacheMultiple has no associated Point, structurally distinct from Equipement/Isolement. `SheetName` is documented (comment only, no behavior change) as reinterpreted to an admin-facing internal label for this `PivotSource` — the generated workbook never uses it as an actual sheet name here (see T3).
  - **T3/T4 (Application, `SheetGenerationEngine`/new `ExcelSheetNameSanitizer`)**: `Generate` changed from `.Select(rule => GenerateSheet(...))` to `.SelectMany(rule => GenerateSheets(...))` — `PivotSource.TacheMultiple` groups `ImportResult.TachesMultiples` by `TypeTacheMultipleCode` (strict string equality, no normalization — codes are clean internal constants), sorted alphabetically (`StringComparer.Ordinal`, deterministic, not `GroupBy`'s encounter order) into one physical `GeneratedSheet` per distinct code; rows keep `TachesMultiples`' own order within a group (LINQ `GroupBy` preserves source order per group), so an `EstFactice` row stays at its original position, never resorted by `Ordre` (which is null for factice rows). Zero `TachesMultiples` ⇒ zero TacheMultiple sheets (no empty-sheet placeholder, unlike the Equipement-null case). Equipement/Isolement rules are otherwise unaffected (`GenerateSheets` returns a 1-item list for them, delegating to the pre-existing `GenerateSheet`). New `ExcelSheetNameSanitizer.Sanitize` (`src/ExcelETL.Application/Generation/`) replaces Excel-forbidden characters (`\ / ? * [ ] :`) with `_` and truncates to 31 chars — applied to the raw `TypeTacheMultipleCode` before it becomes a `GeneratedSheet.Name`; defensive only, today's real codes (`TM_PROC_MAD`/`TM_PROC_REL`) need no sanitization, but a future client-added code isn't guaranteed Excel-safe.
  - **T5 (`DefaultProfileSeeder`)**: the seeded default export profile gained a third `SheetGenerationRule` ("Tâches multiples", `PivotSource.TacheMultiple`, 5 columns, no Point columns), now factored into a shared `BuildTacheMultipleSheetRule()` helper (T8 reuses it) — needs no Guid of its own (`SheetGenerationRule` has no identity property). **Verified against all 3 real fixtures which sheets actually produce `TacheMultiplePivot`s before writing T5's own test** (per the ticket's own efficiency note): all 3 do, every one via PROCEDURE's `TacheMultiple` block — C7401 98 total (`TM_PROC_MAD`=59/`TM_PROC_REL`=39), D8570 88 (`TM_PROC_MAD`=88 only, no REL), G6306B 24 (`TM_PROC_MAD`=11/`TM_PROC_REL`=13). `DefaultProfileSeederPipelineIntegrationTests`' pre-existing C7401 sheet-name assertion updated to expect `"Parents", "Enfants", "TM_PROC_MAD", "TM_PROC_REL"`; a new dedicated test asserts each dynamic sheet's row count matches `ImportResult.TachesMultiples`' own per-code group count.
  - **T6 (`ExportProfileEditor.razor`/`SheetGenerationRuleForm.razor`)**: pivot-source select gained a third `TacheMultiple` option (new resx keys `ExportProfileEditor_PivotSource_TacheMultiple`/`SheetNameHelp`/`SheetNameHelpTacheMultiple`). The column-source select's existing filter (`PivotFieldResolver.GetPivotSource(fieldRef) == PivotSource`) needed no change — it's already fully generic over `PivotFieldRef`. The `PointColumnDefinition` add/edit subform is hidden entirely (`@if (_pivotSource != PivotSource.TacheMultiple)`), not just disabled, matching T2's Domain invariant; switching to `TacheMultiple` also clears any already-added point columns via a new `@bind:after="OnPivotSourceChanged"` handler (avoids a hidden, confusing submit-time `DomainRuleViolationException` for state the user can no longer see) — switching back only restores the *ability* to add new ones, it does not resurrect cleared ones. `SheetName`'s label gained contextual help text explaining its reinterpreted role for this `PivotSource`.
  - **T7 (`ExportProfileTest.razor`)**: needed **zero** code changes — the preview already renders one HTML `<table>` per entry in `_generatedWorkbook.Sheets` via a plain `@foreach`, and T3 already made that collection dynamically include one sheet per distinct `TypeTacheMultipleCode`. New bUnit tests in `ExportProfileTestTests.cs` prove this end-to-end against the real C7401 fixture: a profile with a `TacheMultiple` rule renders both `#generated-sheet-TM_PROC_MAD-table` (59 body rows) and `#generated-sheet-TM_PROC_REL-table` (39 rows) with the expected headers; the same fixture through a profile *without* that rule renders no `table[id^='generated-sheet-TM_']` at all (non-regression, proving the UI only reflects what the engine actually generated, not what the source data contains).
  - **T8 (`DefaultProfileSeeder`, post-delivery correctif — real-environment gap discovered after T1-T7 shipped)**: `DefaultProfileSeeder` never modifies an already-existing profile (by design, so an admin customization is never overwritten) — which meant an export profile seeded *before* T5 landed (i.e. from Lot M, before the "Tâches multiples" rule existed) would never receive it, even after the code shipped: the nominal seeding path (`SeedExportProfileAsync`) is a no-op the moment a profile exists under `ExportProfileId`. Confirmed in the real environment: the rule was genuinely missing from the already-seeded default profile, and `/export-profiles`/`/export-profiles/test` showed no `TM_PROC_*` sheets despite T1-T7 being deployed and green. **Fix**: a new, narrow, additive-only migration, `MigrateTacheMultipleSheetRuleIfMissingAsync` — runs only when the export profile already exists *and* has no `SheetGenerationRule` with `PivotSource.TacheMultiple`; appends exactly `BuildTacheMultipleSheetRule()` (the same T5 definition, now shared) to the existing `SheetRules`, leaving `Parents`/`Enfants` — and any admin customization already made to them — completely untouched. **Deliberately not a general reseed**: the check is the simplest possible ("rule absent ⇒ add"), with no dedicated migration marker — an admin who later deliberately removes the rule will see it reappear on the next restart under this rule, a known limitation flagged in the ticket itself, to be revisited with the user only if it proves to be a real problem in practice (not pre-emptively solved here). Tests (`DefaultProfileSeederTests`, +4): migration adds the rule once to a profile lacking it; `Parents`/`Enfants` (including an admin rename and a trimmed Point-column set) survive structurally unchanged; a profile that already has the rule is left completely alone (byte-for-byte `BeEquivalentTo` before/after a second `SeedAsync` call); repeated `SeedAsync` calls after migration never produce more than one `TacheMultiple` rule. Note: owned-collection (`SheetRules`) order isn't guaranteed by the EF Core InMemory provider across a delete+reinsert round trip (no explicit ordering column configured) — these new tests assert rule *membership*, not sequence, unlike T5's own single-fresh-insert test which happens to get a stable order for free.
  - Full suite after Lot T: Domain 234 (was 215), Application 109 (was 94), Infrastructure 118 (was 113), WebAPI 13 (unaffected), BlazorAdmin 217 (was 205), Hosting 6 (unaffected) — all green.
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: no exposure of `DataTacheMultiple`/`ItemTacheMultiple` (legacy `AvancementRecette` fields, not part of the pivot model at all); no configurable sort/pagination of generated sheets; the dynamic per-code grouping mechanic stays deliberately specific to `PivotSource.TacheMultiple`, not generalized into a reusable primitive for `Equipement`/`Isolement`; no dedicated migration marker for T8 (see above).
- **`ExportProfileTest.razor` collapsible sheet tables + readable wide-column wrapping, done — 2026-07-23**: client feedback after Lot T shipped dynamic `TM_PROC_*` tables.
  - **Collapsible sections**: each entry in `_generatedWorkbook.Sheets` (`Parents`/`Enfants`/every dynamic `TM_PROC_*`) is now wrapped in a `<details class="test-table-details">`/`<summary id="generated-sheet-{sheet.Name}-details-toggle">`, mirroring `ImportProfileTest.razor`'s own `equipement-details-toggle`/`test-table-scroll` mechanic exactly (content genuinely removed from the DOM while collapsed, not just hidden, via a `HashSet<string> _collapsedSections` keyed by sheet name, same idiom as the import page's `_collapsedSections`). CSS added to `ExportProfileTest.razor.css` (component-scoped, so duplicated verbatim from `ImportProfileTest.razor.css` rather than shared — Blazor CSS isolation means a shared class name across two components needs the rule in each component's own `.razor.css`, or moved to global `app.css` as Lot O/P did for other shared classes; not done here since only these two pages need it and they're not otherwise CSS-coupled). 4 new tests in `ExportProfileTestTests.cs` mirror the import page's own toggle tests.
  - **Page-width containment bug fixed**: `MainLayout.razor.css`'s `main { flex: 1; }` had no `min-width: 0` — a flex item's default `min-width: auto` means it never shrinks below its content's intrinsic width, so a wide nowrap table was inflating the whole page past the viewport instead of staying inside `.generated-sheet-scroll`'s own `overflow: auto`. Fixed once, globally, in `MainLayout.razor.css` (`main { min-width: 0; }`) — applies to every page, not just this one; not duplicated per-page.
  - **Readable wide-column wrapping**: `.generated-sheet-table th/td` changed from `white-space: nowrap` to `white-space: normal` + `max-width: 320px` + `overflow-wrap: break-word` + `vertical-align: top` — a long-text column (e.g. `TM_PROC_MAD`'s "Action") now wraps within a capped width instead of forcing single-line horizontal overflow, while short columns (`Ordre`, `Date de validation`) stay naturally compact under `table-layout: auto`'s default column-sizing (deliberately not `table-layout: fixed`, which would force equal-width columns regardless of content — worse reading for a profile-driven, variable column count). Applies uniformly to every sheet rendered by this page (`Parents`/`Enfants`/`TM_PROC_*`), since they all share the one `.generated-sheet-table` class/markup template.
  - **New living convention doc, `docs/convention-ui-blazor-tableaux-generes-lisibilite.md`** (same "define once, reference from future tickets" pattern as `convention-ui-blazor-alignement-boutons.md`, Lot P): documents the content-cell rule (wrap + capped `max-width` + `table-layout: auto`, not `fixed`) and the page-container rule (`main { min-width: 0; }`, fixed once in `MainLayout.razor.css`, never per-page) for any future BlazorAdmin table whose column count/content depends on a profile or an imported file, not a fixed known schema. Notes `ImportProfileTest.razor`'s `.test-table` never needed this treatment — it never set `nowrap` in the first place, so the browser's default wrapping already applied; not an oversight, just a problem that never arose there.
  - Purely CSS/markup-toggle change, no new resx keys, no Domain/Application impact. Not independently live-browser-verified by this session (the user's own dev server holds port 7013, no admin credentials available to this session) — confirmed working directly by the user in their own browser session for both the collapsible toggles and the column wrapping.
- **`ExportProfileTest.razor` sheet-summary item counts, done — 2026-07-23**: each collapsible sheet summary now shows an item count next to the sheet name (`Parents (1)`, `Enfants (23)`, `TM_PROC_MAD (59)`, ...), new resx key `ExportProfileTest_SheetNameWithCount` (`"{0} ({1})"`, identical EN/FR — punctuation only, no translatable prose).
  - **`GeneratedSheet` carries no pivot-source metadata** (just `Name`/`Headers`/`Rows`) — resolved instead via a new `_generatedExportProfile` field (the `ExportProfile` actually used for the current generation, set alongside `_generatedWorkbook`, reset to `null` everywhere `_generatedWorkbook` is reset). `GetGeneratedSheetItemCount(GeneratedSheet)` looks up the `SheetGenerationRule` whose `SheetName` matches (`PivotSource.Equipement`/`Isolement` rules always produce exactly one physically-named sheet, per Lot T3/T4) — Equipement present ⇒ 1, else `_importResult.Isolements.Count`. Any sheet with no matching rule is, by elimination, a `PivotSource.TacheMultiple` dynamic sheet (Lot T), matched back to its `TypeTacheMultipleCode` group via the exact same `ExcelSheetNameSanitizer.Sanitize` call the engine used to name it.
  - **Excludes "ligne de mise en page" (factice) rows from the TacheMultiple count**, per explicit client instruction: only `TacheMultiplePivot`s with a non-null `Ordre` are counted (`t.Ordre.HasValue`) — a header/placeholder row within a TacheMultiple block has a blank `Ordre` by definition (model doc §1.2, `TacheMultiplePivot.EstFactice`'s underlying signal) but still physically occupies a row in the generated sheet, so `sheet.Rows.Count` alone would over-count.
  - Test (`ExportProfileTestTests.cs`, `GeneratedSheetSummaries_DisplayItemCountsNextToSheetName_ExcludingFacticeTacheMultipleRows`): computes expected counts independently by running the real `IImportPipelineOrchestrator` against the real C7401 fixture inside the test itself, rather than hardcoding fixture-specific numbers — includes an explicit sanity assertion that C7401 genuinely has at least one blank-`Ordre` row, so the exclusion is actually exercised and not vacuously true. - **Lot U (Tableaux/Applications pivot fields + export profile enrichment, done — 2026-07-24, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-pivot-tableaux-applications-export.md`)**: implemented U1→U6 sequentially, one commit per ticket.
  - **U1**: `ImportProfile` gains `DefaultTableaux`/`DefaultApplicationNames` (`IReadOnlyList<string>`, required non-null, empty allowed, no magic default — same anti-hardcoding rule as `EquipementTypeElementNom`). Added as new required constructor parameters (not an `init`-property-with-default) to stay consistent with this codebase's established no-default-for-collections convention — every `new ImportProfile(...)` call site across Domain/Application/Infrastructure/BlazorAdmin/tests updated mechanically. `ImportProfileEditor.razor` gained minimal add-only list UI for both fields (`default-tableau-name-input`/`add-default-tableau-button`, `default-application-name-input`/`add-default-application-name-button`), mirroring `SheetRuleForm`'s existing `UnconditionalColonneNames` add-list pattern.
  - **U2**: `EquipementPivot` gains `Tableaux`/`Applications`; `IsolementPivot` gains `Tableaux`/`Applications`/`RepereParent` — all `init` properties defaulting to empty (`""`/`[]`), broadcast later via `with` expressions, **exactly the same mechanism as `Localisation`/loc1** (not positional constructor params, despite the ticket's own illustrative pseudocode showing them that way — matching established codebase convention preserves every pre-existing 3-arg/5-arg call site). Both records gained explicit `Equals`/`GetHashCode` overrides (`SequenceEqual` on the new list properties) since default record equality on `IReadOnlyList<string>` is reference equality, same reason as `RepeatingBlockLocator`/`Concat`.
  - **U3**: `ProcedureExtractionService.Extract` gained a `defaultTableaux` parameter — the 2 unconditional PROCEDURE Points are now built from that list (`defaultTableaux.Select(name => new PointPivot(name, repere))`) instead of the 2 retired `private const string` fields (`TravauxCompletColonneName`/`TravauxDetailColonneName`), same guard-rail test pattern as the pre-existing `EquipementTypeElementNom` one. `ImportPipelineOrchestrator` broadcasts `profile.DefaultTableaux`/`profile.DefaultApplicationNames` onto the Equipement and every Isolement via `with` (same call site/principle as the existing loc1 broadcast), and additionally copies `EquipementPivot.Repere` onto every `IsolementPivot.RepereParent`. Non-regression verified against all 3 real fixtures with `DefaultTableaux = ["TRAVAUX COMPLET", "TRAVAUX DETAIL"]` reproducing prior behavior exactly, plus `RepereParent` asserted equal to the Equipement's own `Repere` on every extracted Isolement.
  - **U4**: new `ApplicationColumnDefinition` record (`ApplicationNom`/`Header`/`MarkValue`, `DefaultMarkValue = "X"`), modeled on `PointColumnDefinition` but structurally distinct (an Application isn't a `Point`/`Colonne`). `SheetGenerationRule` gains `ApplicationColumnDefinitions`, cross-validated at construction time exactly like `PointColumnDefinitions`: a **new, separate** `DomainErrorCode` (`SheetGenerationRule_ApplicationColumnDefinitionsNotAllowedForTacheMultiple`, not a reuse of the Point one — kept precise/independently localizable, consistent with this project's one-code-per-distinct-failure convention) rejects any `ApplicationColumnDefinition` under `PivotSource.TacheMultiple`; a second new code (`SheetGenerationRule_DuplicateApplicationNom`) rejects a repeated `ApplicationNom` within one rule; the existing duplicate-`Header` check now also considers Application column headers. **Also lands, ahead of U5's own scope, since it's pure Domain plumbing that U5 needs**: `PivotFieldRef` gains `EquipementTableaux`/`IsolementTableaux`/`IsolementRepereParent`, and `PivotFieldResolver.Resolve` renders Tableaux as `string.Join(", ", pivot.Tableaux)` — reusing the existing `ColumnDefinition`/`PivotFieldResolver` rendering path entirely, so the "Tableaux" column needed **zero** `SheetGenerationEngine` changes (only the Application columns did, see U5). EF: new `OwnsMany` mapping for `ApplicationColumnDefinitions` (table `ExportProfileSheetRuleApplicationColumnDefinitions`, same shadow-`int Id` convention as `PointColumnDefinitions`); one combined migration `20260724005133_AddTableauxApplicationsToProfiles` carries both this table and U1's 2 new `ImportProfiles` columns (not yet applied to any real SQL Server database as of this lot). Blazor: new `ApplicationColumnDefinitionForm.razor` (mirrors `PointColumnDefinitionForm.razor`, Blazor-only default `MarkValue = "O"` — distinct from the Domain type's own `"X"` default, matching the one real seeded Application) wired into `SheetGenerationRuleForm.razor`'s always-present "Add a sheet rule" card, hidden for `PivotSource.TacheMultiple` (same treatment as the Point subform, including clearing already-added Application columns on switch-to-TacheMultiple).
  - **U5**: `SheetGenerationEngine`'s header/cell order becomes descriptive columns → **Application columns** (new) → Point columns. A cell is `MarkValue` when the row's pivot `Applications` list contains `ApplicationNom` (trimmed, case-insensitive — same §7 convention as `TypeElement`/`Colonne.Nom` comparisons elsewhere in the pipeline), else empty; applies identically to `GenerateEquipementRows`/`GenerateIsolementRows`. `GenerateTacheMultipleSheets` untouched (Domain already guarantees `ApplicationColumnDefinitions` is always empty there).
  - **U6**: seeded default import profile: `DefaultTableaux = ["TRAVAUX COMPLET", "TRAVAUX DETAIL"]`, `DefaultApplicationNames = ["PROGRESS"]` (`ProgressApplicationName` const) — reproduces the retired PROCEDURE hardcode exactly, non-regression guaranteed by U3's own tests. Seeded default export profile, both sheets: **"Parents"** gains a "Tableaux" `ColumnDefinition` (`EquipementTableaux`, right after "Désignation") and a "PROGRESS" `ApplicationColumnDefinition` (right after "Tableaux", `MarkValue = "O"`), both before the unchanged Point columns. **"Enfants"**: its pre-existing "Type" column is **renamed** to "Type Elément" (same source field, `IsolementTypeElementNom` — decision #5 confirmed no new pivot field was needed, so this is a header-text change only, not a duplicate column), gains a new "ELEMENT PARENT" column (`IsolementRepereParent`, between "Zone" and "Désignation") plus the same "Tableaux"/"PROGRESS" pair as Parents (positioned after "Position à la pose", before the unchanged 17 Point columns). Tests: `DefaultProfileSeederTests` content assertions updated for the new column lists (`ColumnDefinitions.Select(c => c.Source)` per sheet, `ApplicationColumnDefinitions` presence/values), plus a new cross-profile consistency test (`SeedAsync_ImportAndExportProfiles_ShareConsistentDefaultTableauxAndApplicationNames`) and a rewritten `SeedAsync_ExportParentsPointColumns_MatchImportProfilesDefaultTableaux` (now a genuine cross-profile comparison against `importProfile.DefaultTableaux`, not a literal-vs-literal one, since U3 removed the hardcode this test used to pin). `DefaultProfileSeederPipelineIntegrationTests` gained `Generate_C7401Fixture_WithSeededProfiles_ProducesExpectedHeaderOrderAndNewColumnValues` — full header-order assertion for both sheets against the real seeded-and-refetched profile, plus a per-row check that every one of C7401's 23 Enfants rows shares the same broadcast "ELEMENT PARENT"/"Tableaux"/"PROGRESS" values (`"38-C7401"`/`"TRAVAUX COMPLET, TRAVAUX DETAIL"`/`"O"`). Save reconstructs a fresh `ConditionalPointRule` via its real Domain constructor inside a `try`/`catch (DomainValidationException)` — the constructor already rejects all 3 non-enum-empty cases (`SourceFieldName`/`ComparisonValue`/`ColonneName`), so no new validation logic or resx keys were needed here, only the existing `BusinessExceptionLocalizer` path already used by `AddPointRule`.

- **Lot Y (post-mobile-review corrections, done — 2026-07-24, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-blazor-polish-ux-lot-y.md`)**: 4 fixes from a second round of client mobile screenshots, following the mandatory Y0 investigation (both X6 and X11 were confirmed already merged before touching anything).
  - **Y1 (`NavMenu.razor`/`NavMenu.razor.css`)**: on mobile, the brand text "Alpha - MAD / REL OXO" visually collided with the hamburger toggler. Y0 found the toggler is a **sibling of `.top-row`, not a flex child of it** — it stays absolutely positioned (`top:0.5rem; right:1rem; width:3.5rem`) specifically so `.navbar-toggler:checked ~ .nav-scrollable`'s CSS-only sibling selector keeps working; moving it into the flex row would break that mechanism. Fix: `.container-fluid` gained `d-flex align-items-center` plus a scoped `padding-right: 4.5rem` (reserving the toggler's overlay space so `.navbar-brand`'s new `text-truncate flex-grow-1` classes stop short of it instead of running underneath); `.navbar-brand` gained `min-width: 0` in the scoped CSS (required for `text-truncate` to take effect inside a flex child at all — a flex item's default `min-width:auto` otherwise blocks shrinking). `PageBackNavLink.razor`'s button and the toggler `<input>` both gained the Bootstrap `flex-shrink-0` utility class directly in markup (not a CSS property — matches the ticket's literal ask and gives bUnit something to assert on). No brand-text shortening (explicitly out of scope, an editorial decision).
  - **Y2 (`ColonneNom`/other export-side `form-floating` fields)**: Y0 found every field already respects `form-floating`'s two hard requirements (input before label in the DOM, non-empty `placeholder`) — X4 was already fully and correctly merged, confirmed field-by-field across `ColumnDefinitionForm`/`PointColumnDefinitionForm`/`ApplicationColumnDefinitionForm`/`SheetGenerationRuleForm`/the root name input. **No production change** — added regression-guard tests only (`ExportProfileEditorTests.cs`: DOM-order + non-empty-placeholder for `point-column-nom-input` plus 9 sibling fields via `[Theory]`, and a binding non-regression test), so a future change reintroducing the label-before-input/empty-placeholder violation the client screenshot showed would be caught.
  - **Y3 (`#save-export-profile-button`)**: gained `w-100 w-md-auto btn-lg mt-4 mb-4` — reused V12's exact `w-md-auto` custom breakpoint class (`app.css`, 768px) rather than inventing a new one; X5's outline-button treatment (intermediate Add buttons) is untouched, this button stays `btn-primary`, never `btn-outline-*`.
  - **Y4 (`app.css`, `.sheet-rule-grid`)**: the real cause of the reported left/right text truncation on `/export-profiles/{id}/edit` mobile was **not** a missing container (X6 was already merged) but Lot R's `.sheet-rule-grid { grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(480px, 1fr)); }` — CSS Grid never shrinks a track below its minmax floor, so on any mobile viewport (< 480px) the grid forced page-wide horizontal overflow, and the browser rendered the already-scrolled-right page, truncating text on both edges ("odifier"/"arents"/"Suppr..."). Fixed with one `@media (max-width: 767.98px) { .sheet-rule-grid { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }` rule at the same breakpoint Y3/V2 already use. Since `.sheet-rule-grid` is shared global CSS (Lot R), `ImportProfileEditor.razor` benefits too as an incidental correct side effect of fixing a genuinely broken shared rule — not a scope-creep feature port, so it doesn't conflict with this lot's "don't extend to import without validation" exclusion (that exclusion targets new features/polish, not shared-CSS bug fixes).
  - Tests: `NavMenuTests.cs` (+4: brand truncation/flex-grow classes, toggler flex-shrink-0, real top-row structure guard-rail — back-link+brand inside `.container-fluid`, toggler outside `.top-row` — and a `SectionContent`-active variant proving the back-link also gets `flex-shrink-0` when rendered), `ExportProfileEditorTests.cs` (+14: Y2 structure/binding, Y3 classes + non-regression save/navigate, Y4 grid class + long-name-stays-in-DOM non-regression). Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 312/312 green (was 278); full solution build green.
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: `ImportProfileEditor.razor` (beyond the incidental shared-CSS fix above); reopening X10/X11's `SectionOutlet`/`SectionContent` mechanism itself; brand text shortening; any pipeline/business-logic change.

Tests added in `ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`: `SaveProfileButton_IsFullWidthLargeCta_WithVerticalMargins_OnNewRoute`/`..._OnEditRoute` (the latter rendering with `Id` set via `BuildProfileWithOneSheetRule()`, mirroring the file's existing edit-route test pattern). Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 314/314 green (was 312).
- **Lot 027 (unicité du nom des profils d'import/export, done — 2026-07-24, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-027-unicite-nom-profils.md`)**: triggered by a client screenshot showing 3 profiles named identically `"Profil OXO standard (Copie)"` from the Duplicate button's un-guarded suffixing (Lot J1/F1). Implemented 27.0→27.4 in the ticket's own recommended order, one commit per ticket.
  - **27.0 (Domain, both `ImportProfile`/`ExportProfile`)**: `MaxNameLength = 60` constant on each type; the one reconstructing constructor that actually validates (`ImportProfile`'s `Guid`-taking overload, `ExportProfile`'s only constructor) now also checks `name.Trim().Length <= 60`, throwing `DomainValidationException` with 2 new `DomainErrorCode` members (`ImportProfile_NameTooLong`/`ExportProfile_NameTooLong`) — same mechanism as the pre-existing empty-name check, `MaxNameLength` passed as `Args[0]` for the `{0}`-templated resx message. `ImportProfileConfiguration`/`ExportProfileConfiguration`'s `Name` column changed from a hardcoded `HasMaxLength(200)` to `HasMaxLength(ImportProfile.MaxNameLength)`/`HasMaxLength(ExportProfile.MaxNameLength)`. Blazor: `maxlength="@ImportProfile.MaxNameLength"`/`maxlength="@ExportProfile.MaxNameLength"` added to `#profile-name-input`/`#export-profile-name-input` (defense-in-depth only — the Domain constructor stays the real source of truth, proven by a dedicated bUnit test that bypasses the HTML attribute via `.Change()` with a 61-character string).
  - **27.1 (`ProfileNameAlreadyExistsException`)**: lives in `ExcelETL.Application.Exceptions` (not a `Extraction/Exceptions` subfolder as the ticket's own example literally suggested) — deliberately a **single shared class** reused by both `EfImportProfileStore`/`EfExportProfileStore`, not two mirrored per-type exceptions like `ImportProfileNotFoundException`/`ExportProfileNotFoundException`: the failure mode and message shape ("a profile named 'X' already exists") are identical either way, so a second near-duplicate class would add nothing. New `ApplicationErrorCode.ProfileNameAlreadyExists` member + `ApplicationMessages.resx`/`.fr.resx` entries — picked up automatically by the existing `BusinessExceptionLocalizer` dispatch (`IHasApplicationErrorCode` branch), no new localizer mechanism needed.
  - **27.2 (`EfImportProfileStore`/`EfExportProfileStore.SaveAsync`)**: before any write, loads just the `Name` column of every *other* profile (`Where(p => p.Id != profile.Id)`, own Id excluded so a no-op rename during edit never self-collides) and compares in-memory via `Trim()` + `OrdinalIgnoreCase` — deliberately not translated into a server-side EF query, since the InMemory provider's LINQ translation of `.Trim().ToUpper()` is unreliable and the profile count is small. Throws `ProfileNameAlreadyExistsException` before the existing delete-then-insert upsert sequence runs. `HasIndex(p => p.Name).IsUnique()` added to both `IEntityTypeConfiguration`s as the defense-in-depth DB-level safety net (relies on the target SQL Server's default case-insensitive collation, e.g. `SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS` — no explicit `.UseCollation(...)` set, documented inline rather than forced, since every environment this project targets uses that default). **One combined migration**, `20260724115715_AddProfileNameUniqueIndexAndMaxLength` (per the ticket's explicit "one migration, not two" instruction) — carries both the `Name` column's `nvarchar(200)` → `nvarchar(60)` `AlterColumn` (EF's own scaffolder flagged this as a potential-data-loss operation, expected and accepted per the ticket's "test data only, DB gets dropped and recreated" note) and the two `CreateIndex(... unique: true)` calls.
  - **27.3 (`ImportProfileEditor.razor`/`ExportProfileEditor.razor`)**: `SaveProfileAsync` gained a second `catch (ProfileNameAlreadyExistsException ex)` block alongside the pre-existing `catch (DomainValidationException ex)`, both calling `BusinessExceptionLocalizer.TryLocalize(ex) ?? ex.Message` identically — no navigation on failure, form stays filled as typed (matches the pre-existing Domain-error catch behavior exactly, just a second exception type routed through the same one display mechanism). **Deviated from the ticket's literal ask for 2 new page-scoped resx keys** (`ImportProfileEditor_DuplicateName`/`ExportProfileEditor_DuplicateName`): since `ProfileNameAlreadyExistsException` already implements `IHasApplicationErrorCode` and is fully localized via 27.1's `ApplicationMessages` resx entry, any such page-level key would never actually be read by the code path the ticket itself describes — added would just be dead resources, inconsistent with this project's own precedent of pruning (not accumulating) unused resx entries (see Lot K4). Flagged here rather than silently added.
  - **27.4 (`ImportProfiles.razor`/`ExportProfiles.razor`, Duplicate button)**: `BuildAvailableDuplicateName(profileName)` first strips an already-present `"(Copy[ N])"`-shaped suffix off the *clicked* profile's own name via one `Regex` (built from the localized `*_DuplicateSuffix` resx value, e.g. `"(Copy)"` → inner word `"Copy"`) — this is what makes clicking Duplicate on either the original **or** an existing copy converge on the same next-available `"{BaseName} (Copy N)"`, per the ticket's own worked example, rather than ever nesting `"(Copy) (Copy)"`. Then increments `attempt` from 1 (`"(Copy)"`) upward (`"(Copy 2)"`, `"(Copy 3)"`, ...) checking each candidate against the **already-loaded** `_profiles` list (`Trim()` + `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, same normalization as the Store) — no extra `GetAllAsync()` round trip, per the ticket's explicit efficiency note. Makes 27.2/27.3 unobservable in the normal duplicate flow (the submitted name is always already free) without bypassing the Store's own check, which stays the real safety net for the Create/Modify flows.
  - Full solution after Lot 027: Domain 270 (was 264), Application 123 (was 121), Infrastructure 131 (was 121), BlazorAdmin 328 (was 314), WebAPI 13/Hosting 6/legacy 9+15 (unaffected) — all green.
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: no Domain-constructor-level uniqueness check (depends on Store state, not a construction invariant); no auto-rename-suggestion on Create/Modify collisions (user picks a new name manually after the error, only Duplicate gets auto-increment); no data migration to deduplicate/truncate any already-existing test-only profiles (the real dev database gets dropped and recreated from migrations + `DefaultProfileSeeder` instead); `DefaultProfileSeeder`'s own seeded names are untouched (unique by construction, idempotent by `Guid` not by name).
- **Lot 028 (suppression de profils d'import/export avec confirmation, done — 2026-07-24, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-028-suppression-profils-import-export.md`)**: triggered by a client screenshot of `ExportProfiles.razor` showing no way to remove a profile, then explicitly extended by the client to `ImportProfiles.razor` for parity. Implemented 28.0→28.4 in the ticket's own recommended order.
  - **28.0/28.1**: investigation confirmed `EfImportProfileStore.DeleteAsync`/`EfExportProfileStore.DeleteAsync` already existed (Lot E2/I6 generation) and were already tested (existing-profile removal + idempotent unknown-id) — no new store method needed, exactly as the ticket predicted. The one real gap: neither store's existing delete test asserted `GetAllAsync` also stops containing the deleted profile (only `GetByIdAsync` was checked) — added that one assertion to both `EfImportProfileStoreTests`/`EfExportProfileStoreTests`' existing `DeleteAsync_WithExistingProfile_RemovesItAndItsNestedRows` tests, per the ticket's own "fill the gap, don't duplicate a test" instruction.
  - **28.2/28.3 (`ImportProfiles.razor`/`ExportProfiles.razor`, symmetric)**: a new icon-only `#delete-profile-button-{id}`/`#delete-export-profile-button-{id}` (+ `-card-{id}` mobile variants) sits alongside the existing Edit/Duplicate buttons in both the table row and mobile card. **Deviated from the ticket's literal test-markup example** (`<span class="bi bi-trash" aria-hidden="true">`): this project has never loaded the Bootstrap Icons font (see `NavMenu.razor.css`'s own comment) — every row icon on these two pages (`PencilIconMarkup`/`CopyIconMarkup`) is already an inline `<svg>` constant, so the new `TrashIconMarkup` follows that established pattern instead of the ticket's font-class example, consistent with 28.0's own instruction to reuse the pencil/copy pattern "à l'identique". Clicking it does not call `DeleteAsync` directly — it sets `_profileIdPendingDeletion`/`_profileNamePendingDeletion` (component state, no JS interop, matching Lot V Partie B's established principle), which conditionally swaps that row's Edit/Duplicate/Delete buttons for an inline confirmation (`#delete-profile-confirm-{id}` / `#delete-export-profile-confirm-{id}`, each with `-card-{id}` and non-card variants so the always-both-rendered table+card templates never produce a duplicate id) showing the target profile's name plus `btn-danger` Confirm (`#confirm-delete-profile-button-{id}`) and plain `btn-secondary` Cancel (`#cancel-delete-profile-button-{id}`, no icon per `convention-ui-blazor-icones-boutons.md`'s secondary-action rule). Confirm calls `DeleteAsync` then reloads via `LoadProfilesAsync()`; Cancel just clears the pending state. A single nullable `Guid?` field naturally enforces "only one confirmation open at a time" — opening a second row's confirmation overwrites the field, closing the first with no delete ever attempted for it.
  - **28.4 (resx)**: per-page keys (`ImportProfiles_Delete`/`ConfirmDeleteMessage`/`ConfirmDeleteButton`/`CancelDeleteButton`, mirrored as `ExportProfiles_*`), EN/FR. **Deviated from the ticket's suggestion to consider a shared generic Confirm/Cancel key pair between the two pages**: checked first — no such generic (non-page-prefixed) key exists anywhere in `BlazorAdminMessages.resx` today; every prior Confirm/Cancel pair (`ImportProfileEditor_ConfirmDeleteButton`/`CancelButton`, `ExportProfileEditor_*`) is page-scoped, explicitly kept separate even when the English wording coincides (documented repeatedly, e.g. Lot Q/S1) — introducing a new cross-page "Common_*" prefix here would be a bigger, unrequested naming-convention change for two extra resx entries. Followed the established per-page-key convention instead. `ImportProfiles_Delete`/`ExportProfiles_Delete` are templated (`"Delete profile {0}"`) rather than plain like `_Edit`/`_Duplicate` — a deliberate enhancement over the existing Edit/Duplicate aria-labels, per the ticket's own worked example, since a destructive action benefits from naming its target in the label.
  - **28.5**: no code — decision already acted with the client (documented in the ticket itself) that a seeded default profile deleted via these buttons re-appears on the next `BlazorAdmin` restart (`DefaultProfileSeeder` only checks its own stable `Guid` constants), accepted as-is, not to be reopened without a new explicit request.
  - Tests: 5 new bUnit facts per page (button opens confirmation without calling `DeleteAsync` — `Mock<IImportProfileStore>`/`Mock<IExportProfileStore>` `Times.Never`; confirmation shows the target name; Cancel closes it with `Times.Never` and leaves Edit/Duplicate working; Confirm calls `DeleteAsync` with the exact id and the reloaded list drops the profile; opening a second row's confirmation silently closes the first without deleting it), plus `delete-profile-button`/`delete-export-profile-button` folded into the pre-existing icon-only-row-actions parity test. Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 338/338 green (was 328); `ExcelETL.Infrastructure.Tests`: 131/131 green (unchanged count, existing tests extended in place).

  - **029.0 investigation**: one repo-wide search for `ExcelEtl` found 4 `appsettings*.json` connection strings (`ExcelETL.WebAPI`/`ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin`, both `appsettings.json` and `appsettings.Development.json`) plus **2 design-time factories with their own independently hardcoded connection string**, distinct from any `appsettings.json` — `ExcelEtlDbContextFactory`/`ApplicationIdentityDbContextFactory` (`src/ExcelETL.Infrastructure/Persistence/`, `.../Identity/`), both `IDesignTimeDbContextFactory<T>` implementations used only by `dotnet ef migrations add`. These are exactly the trap the ticket's own 029.0 called out — missing them would mean future migrations get scaffolded against a `ExcelEtl` database that no host actually points at anymore. Everything else matching `ExcelEtl` was either a class name (`ExcelEtlDbContext`, never renamed — a bigger, unrequested rename out of this ticket's scope), an InMemory-provider test factory (`TestDbContextFactory`/`TestExcelEtlDbContextFactory`, no real catalog name involved), an unrelated literal (`FileStorage:ArchiveDirectory`, the legacy client's `ExcelEtlApi:*` config-key prefix), or historical narrative in this file's own past-lot bullets and dated `docs/etat-avancement-*.md` snapshots (left untouched, frozen-in-time records per this file's own living-vs-frozen convention). No SQL scripts reference it.
  - **029.1**: the migrations-history table names (`__EFMigrationsHistory_ExcelEtl`/`__EFMigrationsHistory_Identity`) are explicitly **not** renamed, per the ticket's own out-of-scope note — they're derived from the `DbContext` class name, not the physical database name, and renaming them wasn't confirmed. The retired `ExcelEtl` LocalDB database itself is abandoned, not migrated/dropped by this ticket (Lot G4's auto-migrate-on-startup creates `AM-OXO-ETL-MAD-REL` fresh on the next real host startup) — consistent with this being a dev-environment-only change.
  - Tests: `ConnectionStringConfigurationTests` (new, one per host — `tests/ExcelETL.WebAPI.Tests/Configuration/`, `tests/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests/Configuration/`) load each host's own `appsettings.json`/`appsettings.Development.json` via a real `ConfigurationBuilder.AddJsonFile(...)` (located via a repository-root walk-up to `ExcelETL.slnx`, same walk-up-to-a-marker idiom as the existing `Fixtures`-directory helpers) and assert `ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection` contains the new name and no longer contains the old one — scoped to the connection string value specifically, not a whole-file text search, so `FileStorage:ArchiveDirectory`'s unrelated `ExcelEtl`-containing value never produces a false failure. `ExcelEtlDbContextFactoryTests`/`ApplicationIdentityDbContextFactoryTests` (new, `tests/ExcelETL.Infrastructure.Tests/Persistence/`, `.../Identity/`) call `CreateDbContext([])` and assert the resulting context's `Database.GetConnectionString()` the same way. Every intermediate "Add" button (`add-default-tableau-button`/`add-default-application-name-button`/`add-block-field-button`/`add-unconditional-colonne-button`/`add-point-rule-button`/`add-sheet-rule-button`, plus `SheetRuleForm`'s own Submit/Cancel which double as "Save changes"/"Cancel" in edit mode) became `btn btn-outline-secondary w-100 mt-3` (X5's exact export-side color, not a new choice) — `save-profile-button` (already `btn-primary w-100 w-md-auto btn-lg mt-4 mb-4` since a same-day Lot Y follow-up) needed no change. The Unconditional-Colonne/Point-Rule *inline edit-in-place* rows (Lot W's compact single-row UI, no equivalent on the export side) were deliberately left untouched — out of this ticket's literal field list and a different, already-accepted UX pattern.
  - **Part A test additions** (`ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`, +18): parameterized structural checks (`form-floating` wrapper, container is a bare `mb-3` with no `row`/`col-*`) for every converted root/sub-form field, `bg-light` card presence for all 3 `SheetRuleForm` sub-lists plus Tableaux/Applications, and outline/full-width class checks for every intermediate Add button vs. the still-`btn-primary` save button. **30.5 explicit cross-editor parity** (`ProfileEditorParityTests.cs`, +4): string-equality comparison (not "both non-empty") of the root-field-container class, the `card bg-light` subform-container class, an intermediate Add button's class, and the final Save button's class between `ImportProfileEditor`/`ExportProfileEditor` — all four are character-for-character identical (`"mb-3"` / `"card bg-light mb-3"` / `"btn btn-outline-secondary w-100 mt-3"` / `"btn btn-primary w-100 w-md-auto btn-lg mt-4 mb-4"`).
  - **Part B (30.6, `wwwroot/app.css`) — actual root cause found, not Y2's DOM-order/placeholder fix blindly reapplied**: the reported "label overlapping already-typed text/placeholder" on `/export-profiles`' "Nom de la colonne" field survived a check against the ticket's own literal fault example (DOM order and non-empty placeholder were *already* both correct there) — so 30.0's mandated CSS inspection went further and found `app.css` (present since the project's initial scaffold, boilerplate from the default Blazor Identity template) overriding Bootstrap's own `.form-floating > .form-control::placeholder { color: transparent; }` with `color: var(--bs-secondary-color)` (visible) + `text-align: end`/`start` on focus. Bootstrap's design deliberately keeps the placeholder invisible — the label *is* the only visible text at rest, so making the placeholder visible only matters when its text differs from the label's; every `*Placeholder` resx key in this project (including `Login_UsernamePlaceholder`/`Account_PasswordPlaceholder`, differing from their `*Label` only by casing) was already established to hold the *same* text as its label (documented repeatedly across past lots), so the override was providing zero value anywhere while creating a literal doubled-text render for every empty `form-floating` field with same-case label/placeholder text (like "Nom de la colonne"). **Fixed by deleting the two override rules outright** (not patched per-field) — restores Bootstrap's default transparent-placeholder behavior everywhere at once.
  - **Widened audit found and fixed a second, independent gap**: `Profile.razor`'s 6 `form-floating` fields (FirstName/LastName/Email/CurrentPassword/NewPassword/ConfirmNewPassword) had **no `placeholder` attribute at all** (a `:not(:placeholder-shown)` selector never matches without one, so the label was always rendered pre-floated regardless of value — a lesser, purely cosmetic deviation from the ideal float animation, not the reported overlap bug, but still a violation of form-floating's stated technical requirement). Fixed by adding `placeholder` attributes (`Account_EmailPlaceholder` reused for Email; 5 new resx keys — `Profile_FirstNamePlaceholder`/`LastNamePlaceholder`/`CurrentPasswordPlaceholder`/`NewPasswordPlaceholder`/`ConfirmNewPasswordPlaceholder`, EN+FR, each holding the same text as its `*Label` counterpart per the established convention).
  - **Part B tests** (`tests/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests/Pages/Admin/FormFloatingStructureAuditTests.cs`, new file, +7 across 5 test classes): a shared `FormFloatingStructureAssertions.AssertAllFormFloatingFieldsAreStructurallyValid<TComponent>(IRenderedComponent<TComponent>)` helper generically enumerates every `div.form-floating` on a rendered page and asserts (a) an `input`/`select` precedes its `label` in the DOM, (b) an `input` (not `select`, which is always floated and has no `:placeholder-shown` mechanics) has a non-empty `placeholder` — a parameterized/generic guard-rail per the ticket's own preference over one hand-picked field, so a future field added anywhere in the audited pages can't silently reintroduce the violation. Applied to `ImportProfileEditor`/`ExportProfileEditor` (both the `/new`-equivalent initial state and, separately, after entering sheet-rule + nested-field/column edit mode, to cover the `edit-{index}-`-prefixed markup too), `Profile.razor`, `Login.razor`, `Register.razor` — the full `grep form-floating` scope identified in 30.0.
  - **Part C (30.7, `ImportProfiles.razor`/`ExportProfiles.razor`) — explicit reopening of V4/X2's mobile-stacking decision, not a bug fix**: the header "Tester un profil"/"Créer" buttons' shared wrapper changed from `d-grid gap-2 d-md-flex` (full-width-stacked below `md`) to a plain `d-flex gap-2` (single row at every breakpoint, including mobile); each button dropped its individual `w-100`/`mb-2` in favor of `flex-fill` so the two share the row's width equally rather than reverting to tiny natural-width buttons. A button's label wrapping to two lines *inside* the button at narrow widths is accepted as different from the original bug (two buttons on two lines). Symmetric on both pages. `ImportProfilesTests.cs`/`ExportProfilesTests.cs`: the 2 pre-existing V4/X2 tests per page (`HeaderActionButtons_AreIndividuallyFullWidth`, `*_HeaderButtons_ShareResponsiveStackingWrapper`) were rewritten in place (not left alongside outdated duplicates) to assert the new classes/absence of the old ones — this is a deliberate, client-directed behavior change, not a regression being patched around. New `ProfileListPageParityTests.cs` (+2): string-equality comparison of the wrapper's and each button's class between the two list pages.

Import's 5 section titles are, by contrast, already-localized **static labels** (`ImportProfileTest_EquipementHeading`="Equipement", etc.) with their collections directly at hand (`_result.Isolements`/`.Points`/`.TachesMultiples`/`.Errors`, no cross-referencing needed) — so this lot doesn't reuse or introduce any shared component; each of the 5 existing resx heading values gained its own `{0}` count placeholder instead (e.g. `"Isolements ({0})"`/`"Isolements ({0})"` FR), interpolated at the call site as `@Loc["ImportProfileTest_IsolementsHeading", _result.Isolements.Count]` — same visual format (`"Name (N)"`) as the export side, achieved by a different, simpler mechanism appropriate to static vs. dynamic titles. - **Lot 032 (détection d'incohérence de TYPE dans les tâches multiples PROCEDURE, done — 2026-07-24, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-032-detection-incoherence-type-procedure.md`)**: a data-entry-quality guard-rail, not a business rule or a functional evolution — triggered by a real anomaly Simon spotted in `Dossier_de_MaD_IDL__C7401.xlsx` (PROCEDURE, factice section "10-MISE EN SERVICE DU COMPRESSEUR", tasks 49-88): 3 consecutive TYPE runs (REL 49-72, MAD 73-78, REL 79-88) where the middle run's `Action` wording template matches its REL neighbor, strongly suggesting a copy-paste data-entry error on the client's source file rather than real business intent.
  - **32.0 investigation found no existing section-grouping mechanism** — `ProcedureExtractionService.ReadTachesMultiples` produces a flat `List<TacheMultiplePivot>` with no notion of "section"; a factice (`EstFactice`) row's `Action` is the section title, and the real (non-factice) tasks that follow it up to the next factice row are that section's members — confirmed against all 3 real fixtures via a throwaway ClosedXML dump before writing any code, including the edge case (G6306B) where the sheet's very first task has no preceding factice header at all (section title `""`). Also found `ExtractionErrorLogging.Log`'s Warning/Error routing was a 2-way ternary (`UnrecognizedTypeElement` → Warning, everything else → Error) that would have silently mis-routed the new code to `Error` (blocking-looking) had it not been made explicit — fixed to a 3-way check.
  - **New pure, ClosedXML-free files** in `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/Procedure/` (per the ticket's own "note d'efficacité" #1, most coverage lives here, not in fixture-based tests): `TacheMultipleSectionGrouper.GroupBySection` (the discrete grouping step above, `TacheMultipleSection(Title, Tasks)`); `TacheMultipleTypeCoherenceAnalyzer.Analyze` — computes contiguous TYPE runs (`ComputeRuns`, Trim+case-insensitive comparison per spec §7), determines the majority type by *total task count* (not longest individual run, decision 5), and returns either `MinorityRunAnomalies` (each tagged `Sandwich`/`DebutDeSection`/`FinDeSection` by its position among *all* runs, not just minority ones — decision 1, both bord cases carry the same weight as sandwich, decision 7) when a clear majority exists, or `AmbiguousGroups` (one per tied type, decision 6 — no minority/majority decomposition when 2+ types share the top count) when it doesn't. No length threshold (decision 2: every run break is reported).
  - **New `ExtractionErrorCode.TypeIncoherenceDansTacheMultiple` member** (decision 4) — non-blocking (decision 8: `TacheMultiplePivot`s are always extracted normally regardless), logged at `Warning` alongside `UnrecognizedTypeElement`.
  - **Cabled directly in `ProcedureExtractionService`** (decision 3, not an `ImportProfile`-configurable rule, consistent with the pre-existing Ordre/ligne-factice rule on this same sheet): `Extract` now also calls `DetectTypeIncoherences`, which groups `tachesMultiples` into sections and runs the analyzer per section, building one `ExtractionError` per minority-run anomaly (`BlockIdentifier` = `"{titre section} (tâches {N1}-{N2})"`) or exactly one `ExtractionError` for an ambiguous section (`BlockIdentifier` = the section title alone, no task range — decision 6/7) via a French-only message (no i18n — this pipeline's `ExtractionError.Message` values have never gone through `.resx`, consistent with the pre-existing `UnrecognizedTypeElement`/`RequiredFieldMissing` messages in the other 4 sheet services). The résultat is appended to `ImportResult.Errors`, same channel as every other non-blocking warning.
  - **Égalité-stricte message wording confirmed live with Simon during 32.0** (the one open item the ticket flagged as blocking) — used verbatim as proposed in the ticket.
  - Tests: `TacheMultipleSectionGrouperTests`/`TacheMultipleTypeCoherenceAnalyzerTests` (new files, Application.Tests, +19) cover every 32.1/32.2 scenario purely (single run, C7401's exact 24/6/10 shape, minority run at start/end, two distinct minority runs, strict tie incl. a 3-way tie beyond the ticket's own 2-way example, trim/case-insensitive comparison, sandwich/début/fin classification, plus `ComputeRuns` directly). `ProcedureExtractionServiceTests` (+5, `Mock<IWorkbookReader>`, minimal synthetic sections) cover the 32.3 wiring/message-construction end-to-end for sandwich/début/fin/égalité-stricte/homogeneous-no-warning. `ProcedureExtractionServiceIntegrationTests` (Infrastructure.Tests, +3): C7401 asserts the exact single `ExtractionError` (code, `BlockIdentifier`, full `Message` text) plus non-regression that tasks 73-78 are still extracted normally; D8570/G6306B (`[Theory]`) assert **zero** `TypeIncoherenceDansTacheMultiple` errors — the ticket's own explicit false-positive guard-rail, since these 2 fixtures' sections are all homogeneous. Full suite after Lot 032: Domain 270 (unchanged)/Application 147 (was 123, +24)/Infrastructure 136 (was 133, +3)/BlazorAdmin 373 (unchanged)/WebAPI 15/Hosting 6/legacy 9+15 (unaffected) — all green.
