- **Lot 033 (multi-file upload on `ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor`, done — 2026-07-25, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-033-upload-multiple-fichiers-pages-test.md`)**: client is about to supply ~10 more real source files to validate against; testing them one at a time was tedious. Both pages' `<InputFile>` gained `multiple`, processing the whole selection sequentially (`foreach`, no parallelization, per the ticket's own YAGNI/thread-safety rationale) through the unchanged pipeline.
  - **33.0 investigation confirmed the ticket's own flagged trap was already handled**: both pages already pass `MaxFileSizeBytes` explicitly to `IBrowserFile.OpenReadStream(...)` (fixed during the earlier `BrowserFileStreamBuffering` bug fix), so the Blazor default ~500 KB cap was never a live risk here — no code change needed for that specific piège, just confirmation before writing tests, per the ticket's own instruction.
  - **New shared types, `src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Excel/`**: `BatchUploadLimits` (`MaxFilesPerBatch = 20`, `MaxFileSizeBytes = 10 MB`, `MaxTotalBatchSizeBytes` **derived** as `MaxFilesPerBatch * MaxFileSizeBytes`, per the ticket's explicit "no independently-maintained total" requirement) — the two base constants exist nowhere else in the repo (WebAPI's `UploadLimits.MaxExcelFileSizeBytes`, already referenced by both pages' pre-existing single-file cap comment, is a different, unrelated 10 MB constant for the HTTP endpoint, not reused here since it has no batch/count concept). `BatchFileStatus` enum (`Ok`/`Warning`/`Rejected`/`TechnicalError`) — the 4th status is new this lot: an unhandled exception while reading/processing one file (corrupt/non-Excel content) is now isolated to that file (caught, not rethrown) rather than aborting the whole batch or being indistinguishable from a business `Rejected` (`Equipement is null`). `BatchFileValidator` — pure, no localization (callers build the actual message via their own `IStringLocalizer`), split into `ValidateCount(int)` and `ValidateSizes(IReadOnlyList<IBrowserFile>)` specifically because `InputFileChangeEventArgs.GetMultipleFiles(maxAllowedFiles)` itself throws if the real selection exceeds `maxAllowedFiles` — the count has to be checked against `e.FileCount` *before* a page can safely call `GetMultipleFiles(BatchUploadLimits.MaxFilesPerBatch)` to even obtain the list `ValidateSizes` needs. `FormatMegabytes(long)` (invariant-culture, up to 2 decimals) rounds out the shared helper.
  - **33.1 (batch validation, both pages identically)**: order is count → individual size → total size → process, exactly per the ticket. All 3 rejection messages are separate `.resx` keys (`*_BatchTooManyFilesError`/`*_BatchFileTooLargeError`/`*_BatchTotalSizeTooLargeError`, EN/FR, page-prefixed per this project's established per-page-key convention even though the English text is identical between the two pages) — on any rejection, `_batchResults` is cleared and **no file is processed**, mirroring the existing single-file-rejection "nothing processed" precedent. **The total-size check is provably unreachable through real UI interaction at today's constant values**: 20 files × 10 MB = exactly 200 MB (boundary, accepted), so exceeding 200 MB requires either >20 files or a >10 MB file, both already caught by the other two checks first — confirmed in `BatchFileValidatorTests` by calling `BatchFileValidator.ValidateSizes` directly with 21 fake (`IBrowserFile.Size` set without any real byte allocation) 10 MB files, bypassing the count check on purpose, exactly the workaround the ticket's own test-plan anticipated. Limits are inclusive at both boundaries (20 files exactly / 10 MB exactly / 200 MB exactly all accepted) — asserted explicitly.
  - **33.2 (`ImportProfileTest.razor`)**: `_batchResults` (`List<BatchImportProcessing.Result>`, see 33.4) replaces the old single `_result`. A **file-level** `<details class="test-table-details batch-file-details">` accordion (reusing the Lot V8/R3 pattern verbatim, one level above the pre-existing per-result subsections) wraps each file, titled by its real filename with a Bootstrap `badge` (`bg-success`/`bg-warning`/`bg-danger`/`bg-dark`) showing its status label. **Collapsed by default when the batch has more than 1 file, expanded by default for exactly 1** (so the pre-Lot-033 single-file UX loses no clicks) — content genuinely absent from the DOM while collapsed, same "no `display:none`, no children at all" rule as every other accordion in this codebase (L2/R3/Lot V8 precedent). A `<p id="batch-summary">` above the accordions lists only the non-zero statuses (`"{0} {status}"` per category, comma-joined after `"{N} file(s) processed:"`) — generalizes the ticket's own instruction to omit a zero "Technical error" count to every status, not just that one.
    - **Legacy id preservation, the load-bearing constraint of this lot**: every pre-existing per-result element id (`equipement-table`, `equipement-details-toggle`, `isolements-table`, `warnings-table`, etc.) stays **exactly unsuffixed** when the batch has exactly 1 file (`var suffix = _batchResults.Count == 1 ? "" : $"-{index}";`) — required so every pre-Lot-033 bUnit test (`Run_C7401Fixture_...`, `ResultTables_AreOpenByDefault`, `ClickingSummary_CollapsesTable_...`, etc.) passes **completely unmodified**, per the ticket's own explicit non-regression requirement ("batch d'1 fichier = cas particulier du batch de N"). Multi-file batches get `-{index}` suffixed ids instead (`equipement-table-0`, `equipement-table-1`, ...) to stay valid, non-colliding HTML. Internal `_collapsedSections` keys are always `"{index}:{section}"` regardless of suffix (a C# `HashSet<string>` key, invisible in the DOM, so no legacy constraint applies there).
    - `@onclick='() => ToggleSection(index, "warnings")'` (and the other 4 subsections) needed **single-quoted** attribute delimiters, not the codebase's usual double quotes — a nested C# string-literal argument inside an implicit `@onclick="..."` Razor attribute expression conflicts with the outer double-quote delimiter; caught by a build/render check, not by the type system. Contrast with `open="@(!_collapsedSections.Contains("warnings"))"` elsewhere in the same file, which stays double-quoted because it's an *explicit* `@(...)` expression, which Razor's C#-aware parser handles differently — the pre-existing code already relied on this distinction, this lot just needed to reproduce it correctly for the two new attribute forms it introduced.

A per-file generation exception (rare — the import step already succeeded) is caught and isolated to that file's own `GenerationErrorMessage`, never aborting the rest of the batch, same isolation principle as the import step itself. Each generated file gets its **own** `.card.shadow-sm.bg-success-subtle` result block (download link + per-sheet tables), nested inside that file's own accordion — preserves the pre-existing `GeneratedWorkbookResultBlock_IsACardWithShadowAndSuccessTint_NotAPlainAlert` test's exact DOM shape (download link's parent = card-body, grandparent = card) per file, not just once globally.
    - **Download filename convention changed for every case, not just batch**: `{nom-fichier-source-sans-extension}_export.xlsx` (e.g. `Dossier.de.MaD.IDL.-.C7401_export.xlsx` — `Path.GetFileNameWithoutExtension` only strips the *last* extension, so the fixture's own embedded dots survive) **replaces** the old `TargetWorkbookFileNameBuilder.Build(repere, timestamp)` convention outright, including for a batch of 1 — confirmed safe because no pre-existing test ever asserted the `download` attribute's actual value (only that the `href` started with the right `data:` URI prefix), so this is a real, deliberate behavior change with zero risk of silently breaking an unrelated assertion, not a batch-only special case needing a fork.
    - `#export-test-status` (Processing/Error alert id) is **not** reused for the batch-results container — re-reading the pre-Lot-033 source showed that id was already scoped *only* to the Processing/Error/Rejected-alert states, never to the successful-generation branch (which had no wrapping id of its own at all) — so introducing a new `#export-batch-results` wrapper for the batch content required no compromise on either side of that pre-existing asymmetry.

Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 402/402 green (was 373 before this lot; +11 `BatchFileValidatorTests`, +10 `ImportProfileTestTests`, +8 `ExportProfileTestTests`).
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: no batch-file parallelization; no aggregated export zip download; no persistence/history of batch results (still fully in-memory, no HTTP round trip); no per-file progress bar (the existing global spinner suffices at this file-count/size scale); the X11 custom-upload-button request stays explicitly deferred (this lot only added `multiple` to the existing V10-styled `<InputFile>`); no batch-level dedup/same-name detection (the orchestrator never sees the source filename, only content — a duplicate name in one batch is not an error).
- **Lot 034 (archivage des fichiers source/cible générés via `POST /api/oxo/process`, done — 2026-07-25, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-034-archivage-fichiers-generes-api.md`)**: closes the client's own reported gap — nothing kept a trace of a file processed via the API channel, unlike the Blazor test pages. **Périmètre API uniquement** (`ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor` untouched, explicit ticket exclusion).
  - **34.0 investigation found `ProcessOxoFileService` was already archiving the target file** via `IFileStorageService` (Lot K), but only on success (never on rejection), flat (no `yyyy/MM`), with no metadata, and — critically — **not best-effort**: a `SaveAsync` failure there propagates and fails the whole request. **Deliberately left completely untouched, not folded into the new mechanism**: an existing `OxoProcessEndpointTests` assertion (`Directory.GetFiles(_archiveDirectory, "*.xlsx").Should().ContainSingle()`) already pins its exact single-flat-file behavior, and the ticket's own non-regression requirement forbids modifying it. The two archiving mechanisms coexist side by side, documented inline in `ProcessOxoFileService` as a deliberate decision, not an oversight.
  - **34.1 (`src/ExcelETL.Domain/Archiving/GeneratedFileRecord.cs`, `src/ExcelETL.Application/Archiving/`)**: `GeneratedFileRecord` (plain `sealed class`, not owned by any aggregate) + `GeneratedFileArchiveStatus` enum (`Success`/`NonBlockingWarning`/`Rejected`, same semantics as Lot 033's batch badges: `Equipement is null` ⇒ `Rejected`; non-null + `HasErrors` ⇒ `NonBlockingWarning`; else `Success`). **Deliberately permissive** — `EquipementRepere`/`TargetFileName`/`TargetFilePath` are all legitimately `null` on rejection (the client's own "proof the source data was corrupt" use case) — only `SourceFileName`/`SourceFilePath` are guarded, via plain `ArgumentException.ThrowIfNullOrWhiteSpace` (a developer invariant, not a `DomainValidationException`/`DomainErrorCode` — this record is only ever built internally by `ProcessOxoFileService`, never from a user-facing form, so it stays out of the i18n scope described earlier in this file, same reasoning as `ImportResult`'s own constructor). `IGeneratedFileArchiveStore` (`SaveAsync`/`SearchAsync`/`GetByIdAsync`, append-only — no Update/Delete, no purge policy this lot) and `IGeneratedFileWriter` (`WriteSourceAsync`/`WriteTargetAsync`, both in `Application/Archiving/`) round out the interfaces 34.2/34.3 implement.
  - **34.2 (`EfGeneratedFileArchiveStore`, `GeneratedFileRecordConfiguration`, migration `20260725010636_AddGeneratedFileRecord`)**: same `IDbContextFactory`-per-method pattern as `EfImportProfileStore`, simpler (no upsert, just `Add`+`SaveChangesAsync`). `SearchAsync`'s `EquipementRepere` `Contains`/`OrdinalIgnoreCase` filter runs **in-memory** after `ToListAsync()`, not translated to SQL — same "InMemory provider's string-comparison LINQ translation is unreliable" reasoning already applied to `EfImportProfileStore.SaveAsync`'s own name-uniqueness check, and the record volume here is small by design. **Real EF Core gotcha hit and fixed**: `ImportProfileId`/`ExportProfileId` (plain denormalized `Guid`/`Guid?`, no relationship intended) initially broke `GeneratedFileRecord`'s constructor-binding materialization (`"Cannot bind 'importProfileId', 'exportProfileId' ... Navigations to related entities ... cannot be bound"`) — EF Core's foreign-key-by-convention discovery matches ANY property named `<PrincipalEntityTypeName>Id` against an entity type already registered on the same `DbContext` (here, `ImportProfile`/`ExportProfile`, both already `DbSet`s on `ExcelEtlDbContext`) and silently turns it into a shadow relationship, even with zero navigation properties on either side. Fixed by explicitly configuring both as plain scalar properties (`builder.Property(r => r.ImportProfileId)`/`.Property(r => r.ExportProfileId)`) in `GeneratedFileRecordConfiguration` — worth remembering for any future entity that stores another aggregate's `Id` by value without wanting a real EF relationship.
  - **34.3 (`FileSystemGeneratedFileWriter`, Infrastructure)**: `{RootPath}\{yyyy}\{MM}\{yyyyMMdd-HHmmss-fff}_{source|target}_{sanitized original file name}` — `timestampUtc` is a caller-supplied parameter (not read from a clock inside the writer), so `ProcessOxoFileService` captures **one** timestamp per request and reuses it for both the source and target write plus `GeneratedFileRecord.GeneratedAtUtc`, keeping a request's pair of archived files correlated by name. `GeneratedFileNameSanitizer` (new, Infrastructure/Archiving — deliberately **not** a reuse of `ExcelSheetNameSanitizer`, different forbidden-character set: Windows filename chars `\ / : * ? " < > |`, no length cap unlike Excel's 31-char sheet-name limit) replaces forbidden characters with `_`. No collision handling beyond millisecond precision (`FileMode.Create` on an exact-timestamp-and-name collision simply overwrites) — an accepted, tested (not just assumed) characterization of real behavior, per the ticket's own explicit instruction. Tested against a real temp directory (never a filesystem mock), same convention as `LocalFileStorageServiceTests`.
  - **34.4/34.6 (`ProcessOxoFileService`/`OxoController` wiring + DI)**: `ProcessOxoFileCommand` gained `byte[] SourceFileContent` (the raw uploaded bytes) alongside the already-constructed `IWorkbookReader` — the reader only ever exposes cell values, never the original bytes, so `OxoController.Process` now buffers `IFormFile.OpenReadStream()` into a byte array **once**, builds `ClosedXmlWorkbookReader` from a fresh `MemoryStream` over those same bytes, and passes the array through unchanged for archiving. `ProcessOxoFileService` archives the source file **systematically, including on rejection** (before the early `return` in the `Equipement is null` branch) and the target file only when generation actually happens, via a new private `TryArchiveAsync` helper wrapped in **its own isolated `try`/`catch`** (not the pre-existing outer one, which rethrows) — a disk-full or DB-down failure there is logged at `Error` (`"Failed to archive generated files for source file {SourceFileName} -- best-effort, HTTP response unaffected"`) and never propagates, so the HTTP response the client already has stays exactly what Lot K would have returned. `GeneratedFileRecord.TargetFileName` stores the same `generatedFileName` already computed for the HTTP response (`TargetWorkbookFileNameBuilder.Build(...)`) — `SourceFileName`/`TargetFileName` are both "the logical/display name", `SourceFilePath`/`TargetFilePath` are the actual on-disk relative paths with the archival timestamp prefix embedded (for the future Blazor page's benefit, see 34.5). `ImportProfileId`/`ExportProfileId` on the record are always populated in practice for this service (both profiles are resolved, successfully, before any archiving call is ever reached) even though the Domain model keeps `ExportProfileId` nullable per the ticket's own literal spec (a future caller reaching this store before export-profile resolution is a real, if currently unexercised, possibility). DI: `IGeneratedFileWriter`/`FileSystemGeneratedFileWriter` (`AddSingleton`, stateless) + `IGeneratedFileArchiveStore`/`EfGeneratedFileArchiveStore` (`AddScoped`, `IDbContextFactory`-backed) registered in **both** hosts (BlazorAdmin needs read access for 34.5) — new config section `GeneratedFilesArchive:RootPath` in all 4 `appsettings*.json` (prod: `D:\AM-OXO-ETL\generated-files`, shared by both hosts since it's the same on-prem filesystem; dev: `C:\ExcelEtlGeneratedFilesArchive`, also shared between the two hosts' dev settings so a locally-run BlazorAdmin can see what a locally-run WebAPI archived). `Moq` added to `ExcelETL.WebAPI.Tests` (previously had zero mocking — every prior WebAPI test used real fixtures/`WebApplicationFactory`) specifically to simulate the "archiving fails" scenario without a hand-rolled fake, since Moq is already an OSS-approved solution-wide dependency.
  - **Fixture-state discovery while writing the integration tests**: the ticket's own worked example assumed the real C7401 fixture would land `Status = Success` — it doesn't, since Lot 032's TYPE-incoherence detector already flags a real non-blocking anomaly in that fixture's PROCEDURE sheet (documented in the Lot 032/033 bullets above) — the C7401 integration test asserts `NonBlockingWarning` instead, with a comment explaining why, and the genuine `Success`-status mapping is covered at the `ProcessOxoFileServiceTests` unit level (a hand-built zero-error `ImportResult`) rather than assumed against a real fixture that could grow a new warning at any time.
  - **34.5 (`GeneratedFiles.razor`, `/generated-files`, admin-only)**: `@attribute [Authorize(Roles = IdentitySeeder.AdminRoleName)]`, nav entry added to `NavMenu.razor`'s existing admin block (after Users) with a new `bi-archive-nav-menu` icon (real Bootstrap Icons `bi-archive` SVG path, inline — no icon font, per this project's established convention). List via `IGeneratedFileArchiveStore.SearchAsync(...)` (sort delegated to the store, never client-side); a text search box (`#generated-files-search-input` + `#generated-files-search-button`/`#generated-files-clear-search-button`) re-queries `SearchAsync` rather than filtering an already-loaded list, consistent with the ticket's own "list can grow over time" reasoning. Status badges reuse Lot 033's exact class mapping (`bg-success`/`bg-warning text-dark`/`bg-danger`). **Download is lazy per row, not eager for the whole list**: unlike `ExportProfileTest.razor`'s single already-in-memory generated file, this page's rows can number in the dozens/hundreds over time with no purge policy yet, so eagerly base64-encoding every archived source (~1-6 MB) and target file on every page load would not scale — each row instead shows a "prepare download" button that reads the file **synchronously** (`File.ReadAllBytes`, not `ReadAllBytesAsync`) on click and swaps itself for a real `<a href="data:...;base64,...">` anchor, matching `ExportProfileTest.razor`'s no-JS-interop data-URI mechanism otherwise. **Synchronous read is a deliberate fix, not an oversight**: the async version raced past bUnit's click-then-render wait in this component's own tests (`ElementNotFoundException` immediately after `.Click()`, despite the file genuinely existing on disk) — a real cross-thread I/O continuation apparently isn't as reliably awaited by bUnit's synchronous `Click()` helper as an EF Core InMemory continuation is elsewhere in this codebase; a synchronous read for these small, occasional, one-shot admin actions sidesteps the question entirely rather than chasing the exact bUnit/Blazor dispatch mechanics. Table (desktop) and card (mobile) views duplicate the download button markup with a `-card-` id suffix (same pattern as `ImportProfiles.razor`'s row actions) since both are unconditionally present in the DOM (CSS-hidden, not conditionally rendered) and would otherwise collide on id.
  - Tests: `GeneratedFilesTests.cs` (bUnit, real temp-directory-backed files for the download tests — never a filesystem mock, matching 34.3's own convention) covers empty-list/loading, 3-varied-status rendering with correct badges, a `Rejected` row's missing target button + `"—"` repère placeholder, search-triggers-`SearchAsync`-with-typed-term-and-updates-list, clear-search-reloads-with-null-term, source/target download producing a correctly-base64-encoded `data:` link, and the table/card responsive split (V2 convention). **`BunitContext.Dispose()` is not virtual** (only its protected `Dispose(bool disposing)` is) — `GeneratedFilesTests`' own temp-directory cleanup needed `protected override void Dispose(bool disposing)`, not a naive `public void Dispose()` (which would silently hide, not override, the base method — xUnit calls through the `IDisposable` interface reference, so a hidden method would simply never run). `NavMenuTests.cs`'s `AdminLinkIds` array and the link-order test both extended with `nav-generated-files-link` (no dedicated "true DOM absence when unauthenticated" test on `GeneratedFiles.razor` itself — confirmed by reading `UsersTests.cs` first that `[Authorize]` route attributes aren't enforced by bUnit's direct `Render<T>()` at all, only `<AuthorizeView>` inside the render tree is; the actual "true absence" guarantee for this page is the pre-existing `NavMenuTests` mechanism, same as every other admin page). - **Lot 036 (validation explicite du contrat `POST /api/oxo/process`, done — 2026-07-25, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-036-validation-payload-oxo-process.md`)**: closes the "hors périmètre" item Lot 035 itself flagged (untested request validation on this route) — the only lot in this run that changes an *observable* HTTP behavior of the API, not just internal polish. Implemented 36.3 → 36.1 → 36.2 in the ticket's own recommended order (simplest/no-production-change first), one commit per sub-ticket.
  - **36.0 investigation (mandatory, done before any code)**: confirmed `ProcessOxoFileRequest.ImportProfileId`/`ExportProfileId` were plain non-nullable `Guid` with no validation attribute, and `OxoController` passed them straight through with no missing-ness check at all. Reproduced the real ClosedXML behavior on a non-Excel byte stream directly (a throwaway xUnit test, removed after use, not guessed from the audit): `XLWorkbook`'s constructor throws `System.IO.FileFormatException` ("File contains corrupted data.") — a stable, specific **BCL** type, not a ClosedXML-proprietary one. Confirmed `GlobalExceptionHandler.StatusCodeFor`'s switch has no explicit `default` arm of its own (falls through its `_ =>` case to `500`), but more importantly confirmed `BusinessExceptionLocalizer.TryLocalize` returns `null` for *any* exception not implementing `IHasDomainErrorCode`/`IHasApplicationErrorCode` — so `GlobalExceptionHandler.TryHandleAsync` returns `false` and never even reaches `StatusCodeFor` for `FileFormatException`, ruling out option (a) (adding it to the `switch`) as ineffective on its own; option (b) (catch explicitly in `OxoController`, at the same level as the pre-existing empty-file check) was the only one that could actually work, confirmed by reading `BusinessExceptionLocalizer` before choosing. Confirmed `ProcessOxoFileRequest`/`OxoController` have no consumer in the .NET 10 solution beyond `OxoProcessEndpointTests` — `legacy/ExcelProcessingClientService` always sends both profile-id fields as real, non-null `Guid` values (never omits them), so the `Guid?` contract change has zero impact there, nothing to signal to Simon.
  - **36.3 (test-only, no production change)**: added the two missing HTTP tests for the pre-existing `request.File is null || request.File.Length == 0 → 400` path. **Real, non-obvious finding**: a multipart request with the `File` field entirely absent never reaches that check at all — `[ApiController]`'s automatic model-state validation treats the non-nullable `IFormFile` property as implicitly required (nullable-reference-types-aware validation) and short-circuits with ASP.NET Core's own generic `"The File field is required."` `ProblemDetails` body before the action method ever runs; only a **present-but-zero-length** file reaches `OxoController`'s own `EmptyFileUploadRequired`-localized check. Both are `400`, but via genuinely different code paths — documented via two separate tests asserting their own distinct message, not assumed to be identical.
  - **36.1 (`ImportProfileId`/`ExportProfileId` absent from the multipart → 400 explicit)**: `ProcessOxoFileRequest.ImportProfileId`/`ExportProfileId` changed from `Guid` to `Guid?` (the default approach the ticket proposed, confirmed unblocked by 36.0 — no `[FromForm]` binding constraint stood in the way). `OxoController` now checks both for `null` at the very top of `Process`, before the file check and before any store lookup — each with its own localized message (`ImportProfileIdRequired`/`ExportProfileIdRequired`, new `ApplicationMessages.resx`/`.fr.resx` keys), sequential `if`s so when both are missing only the first (`ImportProfileId`) is reported (documented by a dedicated test, not left to guesswork). A syntactically-valid `Guid` matching no profile is completely unaffected — still `404` via the pre-existing `ImportProfileNotFoundException`/`ExportProfileNotFoundException` path, verified by the pre-existing non-regression tests passing unmodified.
  - **36.2 (file content not a valid Excel workbook → 400, not an unqualified 500)**: `OxoController` now wraps the `new ClosedXmlWorkbookReader(...)` construction in a `try`/`catch (FileFormatException)`, translating straight to a `400` with a new localized `InvalidExcelFileFormat` message — at the controller level (option (b) from 36.0), not `GlobalExceptionHandler.StatusCodeFor`, per the investigation's finding that the latter would never be reached for this exception type. Deliberately narrow (`catch (FileFormatException)`, never `catch (Exception)`) per the ticket's own explicit warning against masking a genuine future server error behind a false `400`. Non-regression: all 3 real fixtures (C7401/D8570/G6306B) and the existing rejected/valid-request tests pass unmodified.

- **Lot 038 (`ApiTest.razor` — real HTTP test page for the M2M contract, done — 2026-07-26, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-038-blazor-page-test-api-m2m.md`)**: a **conscious, documented reopening** of the "BlazorAdmin never talks to WebAPI over HTTP" rule Lot K4 established — not a regression of it. Motivated by 3 needs the in-process test pages (`ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor`) and the existing `WebApplicationFactory` integration tests don't cover: manual post-deployment verification against the real server, a client-facing demo of the contract, and ad hoc production debugging without the legacy app.
  - **38.0 investigation** confirmed the exact contract to reproduce: `ExcelProcessingClientService` (legacy) sends `X-Api-Key` header + multipart fields `ImportProfileId`/`ExportProfileId`/`File` to `api/oxo/process`; `ApiKeyAuthenticationHandler` fails with a bare 401 (no body) on a missing/invalid key; `OxoController` returns 400 (missing profile id / empty file / invalid Excel format), 404 (`ImportProfileNotFoundException`/`ExportProfileNotFoundException`, `Detail` already names which of the two ids), 422 (`ProblemDetails.Extensions["errors"]`, one entry per `Sheet`/`BlockIdentifier`/`Code`/`Message`), or 200 (`FileStreamResult` + `Content-Disposition` filename). No `WebApplicationFactory`-based test project exists for `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin`, so 38.1's fail-fast validation is exercised via a standalone extracted static method, not by booting `Program.cs`.
  - **38.1 (`src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Configuration/`)**: `OxoApiTestClientOptions` (`BaseUrl`/`ApiKey`) + `OxoApiTestClientOptionsValidator.ValidateOrThrow(string?, string?)` (extracted specifically so it's unit-testable without a host). **Deliberately deviates from the ticket's literal "Development.json/Production.json" wording**: no `appsettings.Production.json` exists anywhere in this solution today (confirmed by search) — the established convention here is `appsettings.json` = production defaults (no section at all for a secret, same as `ApiKeyAuthentication:ApiKey` in `ExcelETL.WebAPI`), `appsettings.Development.json` = dev override. Followed that existing convention instead: `appsettings.Development.json` gained `OxoApiTestClient:BaseUrl`/`ApiKey` (`https://localhost:7088/`, the WebAPI dev HTTPS port from its own `launchSettings.json`, and the exact same `dev-local-api-key-CHANGE-ME` value as `ExcelETL.WebAPI/appsettings.Development.json`'s `ApiKeyAuthentication:ApiKey`, so two locally-run hosts actually authenticate against each other out of the box); `appsettings.json` gained no section at all, so a real deployment missing it fails fast at startup (`Program.cs`) rather than silently degrading — the real production `BaseUrl`/`ApiKey` are expected via user secrets/env var, never committed here.
  - **38.2 (`src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Services/`)**: `IOxoApiTestClient`/`OxoApiTestClient` (registered via `AddHttpClient<IOxoApiTestClient, OxoApiTestClient>`, `BaseAddress`/6-minute `Timeout` from options — matches `ExcelProcessingClientService.DefaultTimeout`'s own rationale) + `OxoApiTestResult` (5 variants: `Success`/`BusinessRejection`/`ProfileNotFound`/`Unauthorized`/`TechnicalError`, one static factory each — no business logic in the client itself, it only maps `HttpStatusCode` → variant and reads `ProblemDetails.Detail`/`Extensions["errors"]` verbatim). `TechnicalError` deliberately carries only the raw status code, never a message — the page shows one generic localized string for every non-categorized failure, consistent with the project's "no raw technical detail in the UI" principle. Tested against a hand-rolled `FakeHttpMessageHandler` (`tests/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests/Services/`, mirrors `legacy/ExcelProcessingClientService.Tests`' own class verbatim rather than adding a MockHttp-style NuGet dependency for equivalent capability) — one case per real HTTP status `OxoController` can return, plus header/multipart-shape assertions.
  - **38.3 (`ApiTest.razor`, route `/api-test`)**: two read-only dropdowns (`#import-profile-select`/`#export-profile-select`, populated in process via `IImportProfileStore`/`IExportProfileStore` — never over HTTP, only the final `Process` call is) + a mono-file `<InputFile id="source-file-input">` (no `multiple` — the HTTP contract is single-file, unlike Lot 033's in-process batch pages) + `#process-button` (disabled until both profiles and the file are selected). Result rendering switches on `OxoApiTestResult.Status`: success is a `bg-success-subtle` card with a `data:` URI download link (same mechanism as `ExportProfileTest.razor`); business rejection is an `alert-danger` list (same shape as the two in-process test pages' own rejected-file block); profile-not-found renders the API's own `Detail` text verbatim (already names which id, from `ImportProfileNotFound`/`ExportProfileNotFound`'s existing localized messages — no extra logic needed on the Blazor side); unauthorized and technical-error are each one fixed localized message (the former explicitly pointing at the `OxoApiTestClient:ApiKey` server configuration, since the admin can't fix a 401 from the browser).

Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 462/462 green (was 437 before this lot; +25). Full solution build green.

- **Lot 039 (accessibilité clavier du toggler mobile `NavMenu.razor`, done — 2026-07-27, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-039-accessibilite-clavier-navmenu.md`)**: follows `audit-design-blazoradmin-2026-07-27.md` §3.2 / its priority synthesis — the one real total keyboard-access blocker found across the whole audited BlazorAdmin surface (everything else in that audit is comfort/announcement drift, not a hard block).
  - **39.0 investigation**: the mobile toggler `<input type="checkbox" class="navbar-toggler">` had no `id` at all and carried only a `title` — no `aria-label`/`aria-controls`/`aria-expanded`, purely CSS-driven open/close (`.navbar-toggler:checked ~ .nav-scrollable`, `NavMenu.razor.css:164`, confirmed the only such mechanism, no hidden JS). For the second constat (`.nav-scrollable`'s native, non-Blazor `onclick="document.querySelector('.navbar-toggler').click()"`, meant to close the menu when an internal link is activated): confirmed bUnit/AngleSharp has no JS engine, so this attribute is never actually executed in a bUnit test — a live-browser check was attempted (mobile viewport, real dev server, `/Account/Login`, no auth needed) but the Browser pane's own click/event-delivery was unreliable this session (a JS `.click()` fired a real native `change` event that never round-tripped to the Blazor circuit; a `computer`-tool click didn't register at all) — consistent with this project's already-documented Browser-pane flakiness, not a sign of an actual defect in this markup. **Concluded Branch A** (already works) on the strength of standard, spec'd WHATWG platform behavior: activating a focused `<a>` via Enter dispatches a synthetic "click" `MouseEvent` that bubbles exactly like a real mouse click, reaching the same ancestor `onclick` handler either way — documented in `NavMenuTests.cs` and in the ticket doc itself (39.0's checklist), not silently assumed. No code change for this second point, per the ticket's own explicit branch structure.
  - **39.1**: the checkbox gained `id="nav-menu-toggler"`, `aria-label`/`title` both reusing the pre-existing `NavMenu_ToggleTitle` resx key (its value, "Navigation menu"/"Menu de navigation", was already exactly the right `aria-label` text — no duplicate key added), `aria-controls="nav-scrollable"` (new `id` added to the `.nav-scrollable` div, none existed before, confirmed unique in the project), and `aria-expanded="@(_isNavExpanded ? "true" : "false")"` driven by a new private `_isNavExpanded` field updated via `@onchange` (`OnTogglerChanged`, reading `ChangeEventArgs.Value is true`) — purely additive to the pre-existing CSS-only mechanism, the checkbox itself and its `:checked` behavior are untouched. No JS interop introduced.
  - **39.2**: pure non-regression per 39.0's Branch A conclusion — no markup change on `.nav-scrollable` itself (still the native `onclick`, no `role`/`tabindex` added, since it's a delegated event container, not an interactive control of its own).
  - Tests (`NavMenuTests.cs`, +6): `aria-label` non-empty, `aria-controls` matches the real rendered `.nav-scrollable` id, `aria-expanded="false"` by default, `aria-expanded="true"` after `cut.Find("#nav-menu-toggler").Change(true)`, non-regression on the `navbar-toggler` class/`type="checkbox"`, and a `.nav-scrollable` structural non-regression test (`onclick` attribute unchanged, still no `role`/`tabindex`). All pre-existing `NavMenuTests` (20) pass unmodified. Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 472/472 green (was 466 before this lot's 6 additions). Full solution build green.
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: `aria-live` announcements for error/batch-summary messages and icon-convention consistency (separate audit items, other lots); color-contrast/M3 theme (needs real browser verification, not this static lot); any new `tabindex` beyond what's strictly necessary (the audit confirms none exist today and DOM focus order is left untouched).
- **Lot 040 (annonce accessible des états asynchrones, done — 2026-07-27, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-040-annonces-etat-asynchrone.md`)**: closes the `aria-live`/error-announcement gap Lot 039 itself flagged as out of scope — follows `audit-design-blazoradmin-2026-07-27.md` §3.2/§3.3 and its priority synthesis (recommandation prioritaire 1).
  - **40.0 investigation**: a grep on `class="alert alert-danger"` found **20 occurrences across 10 files**, not the audit's originally-estimated 25 across 8 — the extra 2 files (`ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor`, +6 occurrences: the top-level `#test-status`/`#export-test-status` error alert, the per-file "rejected" block, and `ExportProfileTest.razor`'s per-file/top-level generation-error blocks) also match the same `alert alert-danger` pattern and got the identical treatment, per the ticket's own explicit instruction to treat every occurrence found, not just the ones listed. `<p id="batch-summary">` (both test pages) sits inside a `card-body` that itself only exists once one of 3 mutually-exclusive `@if/else if` branches (Processing/Error/batch-results) is active — none of the three is a container present across all of them, so 40.2 could not simply add `aria-live` to an existing element; a new wrapper was required (see 40.2 below). The delete-confirmation banner (`ImportProfiles.razor`/`ExportProfiles.razor`) is a `<div class="right-aligned-actions">`, not `alert-danger` — confirmed as the correct, already-unique target for 40.3's `role="alert"`.
  - **40.1**: all 20 occurrences gained `role="alert"` (no `aria-live` added alongside — `role="alert"` already implies `aria-live="assertive"` natively, redundant to double up) — `ImportProfileEditor.razor`, `ExportProfileEditor.razor`, `SheetRuleForm.razor` (×3: top `_errorMessage`, `_unconditionalColonneEditError`, `_pointRuleEditError`), `BlockFieldForm.razor`, `SheetGenerationRuleForm.razor`, `ColumnDefinitionForm.razor`, `PointColumnDefinitionForm.razor`, `ApplicationColumnDefinitionForm.razor`, `ImportProfileTest.razor` (×2), `ExportProfileTest.razor` (×4). No `try`/`catch`/`_errorMessage` logic touched anywhere — presentation-only.
  - **40.2**: `ImportProfileTest.razor`/`ExportProfileTest.razor` each gained a new persistent wrapper div (`#test-status-region`/`#export-test-status-region`, `aria-live="polite"`) around the entire 3-way `@if (_state == State.Processing) / else if (_state == State.Error) / else if (_batchResults is not null)` block — present from the component's very first render (before any file is ever selected), regardless of which branch later mounts inside it. This is deliberate: inserting a brand-new subtree that already contains both `aria-live` and its content in the same render is not reliably announced by assistive technology — the live-region container has to already exist in the DOM before the content change it's meant to announce. `polite` (not `assertive`) per the ticket's own rationale: a batch-completion summary can wait for the user's current keyboard/screen-reader action to finish, unlike a blocking error. The pre-existing `#test-status`/`#export-test-status`/`#batch-summary` ids and the Processing spinner's own `role="status"` are untouched.
  - **40.3**: the 4 delete-confirmation banners (`#delete-profile-confirm-{id}`/`#delete-profile-confirm-card-{id}` in `ImportProfiles.razor`, `#delete-export-profile-confirm-{id}`/`#delete-export-profile-confirm-card-{id}` in `ExportProfiles.razor`) gained `role="alert"` — `assertive`, not `polite`, since it's a destructive action awaiting confirmation, consistent with 40.1's treatment rather than 40.2's.
  - Tests: rather than duplicate existing error-triggering tests, extended them in place with a `role="alert"` assertion where a natural one already existed (`Save_WithEmptyName_...`, `EditRoute_WithUnknownId_...`, `AddSheetRule_WithNonPositiveStep_...`, `AddBlockField_WithInvalidAbsoluteRange_...`, `AddSheetRule_WithDuplicateHeader_...` in both `ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`/`ExportProfileEditorTests.cs`, plus the equivalent `SelectingFile_WithoutProfileSelected_...`/`SelectingFile_ThatFailsProcedureValidation_...`/`SelectingTwentyOneFiles_...` tests in `ImportProfileTestTests.cs`/`ExportProfileTestTests.cs`, and the 2 `DeleteButton_OpensConfirmation_...` tests in `ImportProfilesTests.cs`/`ExportProfilesTests.cs`) — no assertion on the underlying error text/behavior was changed. Added new tests only where no natural error path existed yet: `ColumnDefinitionForm`/`PointColumnDefinitionForm`/`ApplicationColumnDefinitionForm`'s own empty-required-field submit paths (3, `ExportProfileEditorTests.cs`), `ExportProfileTest.razor`'s top-level "select an export profile" generation-error prompt (1), and the 40.2 aria-live wrapper itself (2 per test page — present-from-initial-render, and still-present-with-unchanged-summary-text after a real batch run against the C7401 fixture). Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 480/480 green (was 472 before this lot; +8 new tests, ~11 existing tests extended in place with no assertion changes to their original checks). Full solution build green.
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: factoring the 25/20 error blocks into a shared `ErrorAlert` component or reusing `StatusMessage.razor` (separate architecture decisions, not bundled into an ARIA fix); icon-convention consistency, `aria-describedby` between field and validation message on Login/Register/Profile, heading-level skips, missing `aria-label` on `log-copy-btn` (other audit findings, other lots); actual screen-reader verification (NVDA/JAWS/VoiceOver — out of reach for a bUnit test, a manual check if wanted separately).
- **Lot 041 (mise en cohérence de la convention icônes, done — 2026-07-27, ticket doc `docs/tickets-tdd-lot-041-convention-icones-coherence.md`)**: closes `log-copy-btn`'s icon-convention gap Lot 040 itself flagged as out of scope, plus the audit's other 3 icon-related findings — follows `audit-design-blazoradmin-2026-07-27.md` §2 and `audit-priorisation-design-blazoradmin-2026-07-27.md`'s recommandation prioritaire 3. **Decisions actées with Simon before coding**: the CTA/`Enregistrer` icon gap is an implementation lag, not a convention error (the decision matrix already required icons there); `convention-ui-blazor-icones-boutons.md`'s `bi bi-*` text was the actual error (the real mechanism has always been inline SVG via `AdminIconMarkup`) and gets corrected in place, with **no migration to a `bi bi-*` font ever planned** — not to be reopened.

  - **41.2**: `create-profile-button`/`create-export-profile-button` get `Plus`; `save-profile-button`/`save-export-profile-button` get `Check`; `process-button` (`ApiTest.razor`) gets `Send`; `generate-workbook-button` (`ExportProfileTest.razor`) gets `FileEarmarkSpreadsheet`. The 6 nested sub-forms' shared `SubmitButtonId` button (`SheetRuleForm`, `BlockFieldForm`, `SheetGenerationRuleForm`, `ColumnDefinitionForm`, `PointColumnDefinitionForm`, `ApplicationColumnDefinitionForm`) doubles as "Add" (top-level, always-present card, `ShowCancel=false`) and "Save changes" (edit mode, `ShowCancel=true`) — the ticket's own 41.0 checklist only lists the "Enregistrer" usage in scope, never "Ajouter", so the `Check` icon is rendered conditionally on the pre-existing `ShowCancel` parameter (already the reliable, existing signal for which mode is active) rather than unconditionally — an Add-mode instance of any of these 6 forms stays icon-less. No `@onclick` handler touched anywhere in this sub-ticket.
