- **Lot 055 (sémantique et volume des avertissements d'extraction, done — 2026-07-29, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-055-avertissements-extraction-semantique-et-bruit.md`)**: closes a client-reported gap — 8 near-identical `ISOLEMENT`/`PROLOCK` warnings drowned the one genuinely singular anomaly on a real import. Root cause was 3 layered defects sharing one cause (no spec for what the warning means): the code name asserted a property of the OXO referential the engine can't judge; emission granularity was already correct (see below) but under-specified; and there was no deduplication at all.
  - **55.0 investigation, the one finding that reshaped the plan**: `ConditionalPointGroupEvaluator.Evaluate` (added at Lot C6, well before this lot) already aggregates *per element* across every conditional Colonne-group of a sheet — a warning fires only when **none** of the groups matched, confirmed live by the pre-existing `DiversExtractionServiceTests.Extract_WithSoupapeType_CreatesBothSoupapePointsAndNoWarning` test (SOUPAPE → 2 Points, 0 warnings, already green before this lot). So the ticket's own "DIVERS/SOUPAPE produces 5 warnings today" narrative did not hold against the actual code — 55.4's "vert" step required **no new emission logic**, only the refacto below. What genuinely was duplicated 3× (`IsolementExtractionService`/`DiversExtractionService`/`AutresJointsTouchesExtractionService`): the block that builds the `ExtractionError`, logs it, and appends it to `errors` — `ConditionalPointGroupEvaluator`/`ConditionalPointRuleEvaluator` themselves were left untouched, per the ticket's own explicit "don't enlarge their responsibility" instruction.
  - **55.1/55.2 (renames, one dedicated commit)**: `ExtractionErrorCode.UnrecognizedTypeElement` → `NoConditionalPointCreated` (states only what the engine can honestly know — an element produced no conditional Point — never a judgment on the OXO referential); `TacheMultipleTypeIncoherence` → `TacheMultipleTypeMismatch` (the sole francophone enum member anywhere in Domain, flagged by `audit-qualite-domain-2026-07-25.md` §4.2). Mechanical propagation across `ExtractionErrorLogging`'s Warning/Error switch, `ProcedureExtractionService`, all 3 sheet services, and every referencing test.
  - **55.3 (`ExtractionError`, Domain)**: gained `string? ExtractedValue` (optional constructor parameter, default `null`) — the raw value the warning relates to, as structured data, not just interpolated into `Message`. Not renseigné by `RequiredFieldMissing`/`UnparsableValue`/`TacheMultipleTypeMismatch` (none have a single "extracted value" to report). No pre-existing `IHasDomainErrorCode`-style Args mechanism exists on this type (confirmed by 55.0 — that pattern is exception-only, `ExtractionError` is a plain accumulated-Result record), so a dedicated nullable property was the only option, not a reuse.
  - **55.4/55.5/55.6 (one cycle, `NoConditionalPointCreatedWarningTracker`, new, `src/ExcelETL.Application/Extraction/Oxo/`)**: a small stateful class instantiated fresh inside each service's own `Extract()` call (never static/injected/shared — the scoping guard-rail 55.5 itself demands), owning everything downstream of the (already-correct) per-element emission decision: the French, referential-agnostic message (*"Aucun Point conditionnel n'a été créé pour la valeur « {valeur} » : aucune condition du profil d'import ne correspond à cette valeur pour cette feuille."*, a distinct wording for a blank/null value that avoids empty `« »` quotes), the structured `ExtractedValue`, and deduplication by `Trim`+`OrdinalIgnoreCase`-normalized value (first raw form kept for display) — the same normalization `ConditionalPointRuleEvaluator`'s own comparison already uses, so two forms that would match the same `ComparisonValue` never produce two warnings either. All 3 sheet services replaced their identical `if (warning is not null) { new ExtractionError(...); Log(...); errors.Add(...); }` block with one `warningTracker.RecordIfNew(repere, typeElement, logger, errors);` call. `ExtractionErrorLogging`'s `TacheMultipleTypeMismatch`/`NoConditionalPointCreated` → `Warning` mapping is otherwise unchanged.
  - **Pre-existing fixture-count assertions updated, not weakened**: `IsolementExtractionServiceIntegrationTests`/`AutresJointsTouchesExtractionServiceIntegrationTests` previously asserted one warning per non-matching element (e.g. C7401 ISOLEMENT: 8 warnings) — now assert the deduplicated count (1 entry, `ExtractedValue == "PROLOCK"`), a deliberate behavior change this lot exists to make, not a test adjusted to hide a regression.
  - **55.7 (`ImportProfileTest.razor`)**: the warnings table's context column (`ImportProfileTest_BlockIdentifier` header, kept as-is — already generic enough, confirmed by 55.0, no rename needed) now shows `warning.ExtractedValue` instead of `warning.BlockIdentifier` specifically for `NoConditionalPointCreated` entries (via a new `GetWarningContext` helper) — every other code (`TacheMultipleTypeMismatch`, etc.) keeps rendering its `BlockIdentifier` unchanged. Each `<td>` gained a stable `warning-context{suffix}-{index}` id (none existed before on this table's rows) so the two new tests select by ID, never text/position, per this repo's own convention.
  - **55.8 (integration, `DefaultProfileSeederPipelineIntegrationTests`, not a new file — the existing seeded-profile/real-fixture home per its own established convention)**: confirmed against `IImportProfileStore`'s real seeded profile (never rebuilt inline) the exact volumes the ticket's own openpyxl-based factual survey predicted: C7401 → 1 (`ISOLEMENT`/`PROLOCK`), D8570 → 2 (`ISOLEMENT`/`PROLOCK`, `ISOLEMENT`/`VANNE`), G6306B → 3 (`ISOLEMENT`/`PROLOCK`, `AUTRES JOINTS TOUCHES`/`TUBING`, `DIVERS`/`POINT DE FEU`, and explicitly never `PLATINES`/`ORIFICES CAPACITES`) — isolement counts (23/67/18) unchanged from the pre-existing regression assertions, confirming extraction itself is untouched, only the warnings are.
  - Full solution after Lot 055: Domain 301 (was 300, +1)/Application 196 (was 186, +10 `NoConditionalPointCreatedWarningTrackerTests`)/Infrastructure 217 (was 214, +3 `DefaultProfileSeederPipelineIntegrationTests`, net after 4 pre-existing counting assertions were updated in place)/WebAPI 25/Hosting 6/legacy 9+15 (unaffected)/BlazorAdmin 692 (was 690, +2) — all green.
  - **Not verified against a real Blazor session this time either** (no running dev server/admin credentials available, same recurring limitation) — confidence rests on the full green suite, including the fixture-based integration tests that reproduce the exact client-reported scenario (C7401 → exactly 2 non-blocking warnings: the PROCEDURE TYPE mismatch plus the single deduplicated `ISOLEMENT`/`PROLOCK` entry).
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: querying the OXO `TypeElement` referential (would require a legacy AM-AR/`AvancementRecette` outbound call — the exact coupling this microservice exists to avoid); any presentation-layer aggregation/accordion/counter beyond the emission-time dedup; exposing warnings in `POST /api/oxo/process`'s HTTP response (confirmed at 55.0: the endpoint's success path returns only the generated `FileStreamResult`, never `ImportResult.Errors` — a legitimate separate lot, not touched here); `SystemLogs` label back-compat (pre-production, no real data); `DefaultProfileSeeder`'s `ZERO ENERGIE` `ConditionalPointRule` on ISOLEMENT (kept as-is despite no fixture ever triggering it); special-casing the `NotEquals` operator in the emission rule (the `TUBING`-on-AUTRES-JOINTS-TOUCHES warning is accepted as technically-true-but-low-value noise, not fixed).
- **Lot 056 (modèle d'enregistrement des éditeurs de profil, done — 2026-07-30, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-056-modele-enregistrement-editeurs-profil.md`)**: closes the 29/07 incident — Simon added a `BlockFieldDefinition` to an already-open sheet-rule edit form, clicked "Enregistrer le profil" without first clicking the level-2 "Enregistrer les modifications" button, and the field silently never reached the store. Root cause: 3 independent commit levels (sub-form → `SheetRuleForm` in-memory state → `SaveAsync`), a mid-level button visually identical to "Annuler" (both `btn-outline-secondary` in edit mode), and an unsaved-changes guard (Lot 043) that never covered in-progress form state at all.
  - **56.1**: new `*_ApplySheetRuleButton` resx keys (EN/FR, both editors) replace `*_SaveChangesButton` at the one level-2 sheet-rule submit-button call site only — the 11 other `SaveChangesButton` usages (nested sub-forms, icon `aria-label`s) are untouched, since they really do only save into their own parent form's in-memory state.
  - **56.2 (the core of the lot)**: `SheetRuleForm.Submit()`/`SheetGenerationRuleForm.Submit()` become `public async Task<bool> TryCommitAsync()` — the **single validation path**, `Submit()` reduced to `await TryCommitAsync();`. A form still in its just-reset/just-mounted state is treated as "nothing to commit" (`true`, no side effect) so the root editor can safely flush an always-rendered, never-touched add-sheet-rule form on every save without a spurious validation error. Both editors keep `@ref` on the (at most two, per Lot 057's own note that this predates its own single-open-form state) sheet-rule-form render locations — the edit-mode instance and the always-rendered add-mode instance — and a new `TryFlushOpenSheetRuleFormsAsync()` commits the edit form first, then the add form, before `SaveProfileAsync` ever constructs the `ImportProfile`/`ExportProfile` to persist. A validation failure on either aborts the whole save: `SaveAsync` is never called, no navigation, the failing form keeps its own alert. **`TryCommitAsync()` is the explicit inter-lot contract Lot 057 reuses as-is** to close a form implicitly when another one opens — designed once here, not reimplemented there.
  - **56.3**: new `OnDirty` `EventCallback` on both sub-forms, invoked from every mutation entry point (the 4 locator fields via `@bind:after`, and every add/save/delete on the 4 nested sub-lists — block fields, unconditional colonnes, point rules, header fields/composites on the import side; columns/point columns/application columns on the export side) and wired to `MarkAsChanged` at both render sites — closes the blind spot where an open-but-uncommitted sheet-rule form raised neither `#unsaved-changes-indicator` nor the Lot 043 `NavigationLock` warning. Simply opening a form (Modify, then Cancel with no edits) still raises nothing — only an actual mutation does. **One pre-existing test's own premise was exactly this blind spot** (`ImportProfileEditorTests.AddingSheetRuleWithHeaderField_ThenSubmitting_ShowsUnsavedChangesIndicator`, which asserted the indicator was absent until the whole sheet rule was submitted) — fixed in place (not duplicated) to expect the indicator present as soon as the header field is added to the still-open add-mode form.
  - **56.4**: `BlockFieldForm` (name + absolute-range), `HeaderCompositeRuleForm` (name + template), and the inline unconditional-colonne row in `SheetRuleForm` (1 field) now auto-submit on `@onfocusout` of the row's last field, or `Enter` in any of the row's fields — but only when every field of the row is already non-empty (a partially-filled row produces neither an add nor an alert on blur, a normal "click elsewhere" gesture). The row's own Add button stays in place as a now-redundant confirmation path. On success, focus returns to the row's first field (`ElementReference.FocusAsync()`, no JS interop) for rapid mouse-free entry — a blank-row blur deliberately does **not** steal focus back (would fight a click on an unrelated button). Point rules / `HeaderFieldRuleForm` / `ColumnDefinitionForm` / `Point`-/`ApplicationColumnDefinitionForm` and the root Tableaux/Applications rows are untouched (2+ inputs/selects/checkbox, or already 1-click) — **export has zero eligible sub-form** (constat 7 of the ticket, confirmed by field count), a factual asymmetry, not a parity gap.
  - **56.5**: `Ctrl+Enter` anywhere in `.profile-editor-container` triggers the same `SaveProfileAsync` (including the 56.2 flush); plain `Enter` never saves (reserved for 56.4). No `preventDefault` — there's no `<form>` element here to accidentally submit, and blocking `keydown` unconditionally would have broken typing in every text field. Save button gained a `title` shortcut hint (new `*_SaveButtonShortcutHint` resx key).
  - **56.6**: the unsaved-changes indicator and the CTA are now grouped in a `.profile-editor-save-bar` (`position: sticky; bottom: 0`, its own `max-width` mirroring `.profile-editor-container`'s so it never bleeds edge-to-edge on wide screens), one CSS declaration in `app.css`, never duplicated in a `.razor.css`. `.profile-editor-container` gained a `padding-bottom` so the bar never overlaps the last field. No inline `style` anywhere (`ProfileEditorContainer_HasNoInlineStyle_OnlyTheMaxWidthCssClass` stays green unmodified) and the CTA's own classes (`btn-primary btn-lg`) are untouched.
  - **56.7**: the submission-button class conditional (`btn-outline-secondary` in edit mode / `btn-secondary` in add mode) is gone from **all 8** sub-forms (`SheetRuleForm`, `BlockFieldForm`, `HeaderFieldRuleForm`, `HeaderCompositeRuleForm`, `SheetGenerationRuleForm`, `ColumnDefinitionForm`, `PointColumnDefinitionForm`, `ApplicationColumnDefinitionForm` — confirmed exhaustive via `grep -rl 'ShowCancel ? "btn btn-outline-secondary'`, matching the ticket's own constat 6 of 8, not the 6 a shallower read would have found): submission is now always solid `btn-secondary` in both modes, only its icon still differs (Plus/Check, Lot 041, unchanged); "Cancel" stays `btn-outline-secondary`, previously strictly identical to the edit-mode submit button — the exact defect Simon reported. The stale Lot 053-era code comments documenting the old choice are updated in all 8 files, not left contradicting the code. **6 pre-existing tests fixed in place** (not duplicated) that asserted the old outline class on the edit-mode submit button, across `ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`/`ExportProfileEditorTests.cs`.
  - **56.8 (closing)**: `ProfileEditorParityTests.cs` gains 2 strict class-string comparisons — the sticky save bar and the sub-form submit/cancel classes in edit mode — the two comparables this lot actually modifies (the final CTA was already covered by the pre-existing `FinalSaveButton_CssClass_...` test).
  - New dedicated test files, `ImportProfileEditorLot056Tests.cs`/`ExportProfileEditorLot056Tests.cs` (mirroring the project's established one-file-per-concern convention, e.g. `ProfileEditorParityTests.cs`), rather than inserted into the already-144 KB/86 KB `ImportProfileEditorTests.cs`/`ExportProfileEditorTests.cs`. Covers the incident scenario reproduced literally (existing profile, open edit, add a block field via the nested form, save the profile without ever clicking the level-2 button, reread from the store), the add-mode flush, an invalid-form abort with no persistence/navigation, the "exactly one `SaveAsync` call" atomicity property, non-regression when no form is open, blur/Enter add/reject/non-generalization-guard-rail cases (including an explicit proof that the excluded point-rule row has no `@onfocusout` handler wired at all — bUnit's own `MissingEventHandlerException` on that field *is* the guard-rail), the `Ctrl+Enter`/plain-`Enter` distinction, and the sticky-bar structure.
  - Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite after Lot 056: **742** (was 690 before this lot) — all green throughout, no regression left unresolved at any sub-ticket boundary.
  - **Not verified in a real browser this session** (no running dev server/admin credentials available, same recurring limitation as most prior lots) — confidence rests on the full green bUnit suite; the sticky bar's actual non-overlap with the last field, absence of a second scrollbar, and interplay with the Lot 043 navigation-confirmation banner all remain a manual check, exactly as the ticket's own "Vérification manuelle attendue" note anticipated.
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: autosave/draft-publish/profile versioning (all explicitly discussed and dismissed with Simon on 29/07); a mutable edit-time ViewModel or master-detail-by-route editing model (both costed and rejected); removing the sheet-rule card's own "Modify" click (kept — would converge card and form, contradicting Lot 057's mutual-exclusion decision); the repliement of the add-sheet-rule form and its mutual exclusion with the edit form (Lot 057); button tint/icon-label gabarit (Lot 058 — 56.7 only fixes the class, the taupe tint is deliberately unchanged until 058); unifying the 3 different exception-catch scopes across `SheetRuleForm.Submit`/`BlockFieldForm.Submit`/`SaveProfileAsync` (documented, not touched); the `[Authorize]` attribute/routes of the 4 editor pages (unchanged — Lot 052's `BusinessPageAuthorizationHttpTests` stayed green with no modification).
- **Lot 057 (un seul formulaire de règle de feuille ouvert à la fois, done — 2026-07-30, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-057-exclusion-mutuelle-formulaires-feuille.md`)**: closes remarque 4 of the same 29/07 review — on `/{id}/edit`, the always-rendered "Add a sheet rule" form made an already-long edit-mode page even longer, and there was no way to have only one sub-form's worth of inputs on screen at a time. Depends technically on Lot 056's 56.2 (`TryCommitAsync()`), per the ticket's own explicit dependency note.
  - **57.1**: the add-sheet-rule card's `card-body` (the `SheetRuleForm`/`SheetGenerationRuleForm`) is now conditional behind a standalone toggle button (`#toggle-add-sheet-rule-form-button` / `#toggle-add-sheet-generation-rule-form-button`), not the sub-form's own `ShowCancel` (which would also flip its submit button's icon/color, per the ticket's constat 3). Closed by default in edit mode (`/{id}/edit`); open by default in creation mode (`/new` — an empty screen with no indication of what to do next would cost more than the accepted asymmetry). Closed-state label reuses `*_AddSheetButton` verbatim + `AdminIconMarkup.Plus`; open-state label is a new `*_AddSheetToggleCloseButton` key ("Cancel the addition"), no icon (secondary action). A genuine `@if` (not CSS `display:none`), so reopening always remounts a fresh, empty sub-form instance — the pre-existing `OnInitialized`-runs-once contract (Lot 057's own constat 7) depended on this.
    - **Real bug caught before shipping, found via a scratch debug test dumping rendered markup**: an HTML comment containing the literal text `@if` made Razor's parser misinterpret it as a real directive, silently dropping the entire following card from the render tree — confirmed by the otherwise-unexplained `CS0649` "field never assigned" warning on the `@ref`-bound component field. Fixed by rewording the comment to avoid the literal `@if` text. **Lesson recorded for future `.razor` edits**: an `@` transition is parsed even inside `<!-- -->` content — never write literal `@if`/`@foreach`/etc. inside an HTML comment in a `.razor` file.
    - Fixed in place (not duplicated, same instruction as Lots 51/53/56.7): a handful of pre-existing tests needed a new `OpenAddSheetRuleFormIfClosed(cut)` helper call before touching the (now possibly-closed) add form; and `AddSheetRule_WithValidInput_DisplaysSheetSummaryAndResetsForm`'s own premise ("the form stays open, merely reset, after a successful add") was exactly inverted — renamed to `..._DisplaysSheetSummaryAndClosesForm` on both editors, asserting absence of the field rather than an empty value.
  - **57.2 (the refactor of the lot)**: both editors' pre-057 pair of independent states (56.2's `_editingIndex`, 57.1's own add-open bool) collapses into one `SheetRuleFormOpenState` `record struct` (`None` / `Add` / `Edit(index)`) — only these three states are constructible, so the two concerns can never silently contradict each other. `TryCloseOpenFormAsync()` is the single place that closes whichever form is open, trying to commit it first via 56.2's `TryCommitAsync()` (reused as-is, no second validation implementation, honoring the inter-lot contract 56.2 was explicitly designed for). Opening any form (Modify, or the add toggle) first closes/commits whatever else was open — a validation failure keeps the original form open with its own alert and refuses to switch; opening any form also clears a pending delete confirmation on another card, and requesting a delete first closes/commits whatever form was open (symmetric, per the ticket's own "l'inverse est vrai aussi"). "Cancel" (explicit abandon, both an edit form's Cancel and the toggle's "close" state) never attempts a commit — the sole deliberate no-commit closure path, unchanged from 56.2/57.1.
  - **57.3 (closing)**: `ProfileEditorParityTests.cs` gains a strict class-string comparison of the add-sheet-rule toggle button — the one new comparable element this lot introduces on both editors; the mutual-exclusion behavior itself is already exercised on both sides by the two dedicated Lot057 test files.
  - New dedicated test files, `ImportProfileEditorLot057Tests.cs`/`ExportProfileEditorLot057Tests.cs` (same one-file-per-concern convention as Lot 056's own test files). Cover: closed-by-default in edit mode / open-by-default in creation mode, toggle open/close, remount-with-empty-fields on reopen, icon/no-icon states, an end-to-end add-then-save-profile journey, switching between two open edits (the first is committed, visible in its own card summary, before the second opens), switching away from an invalid open form (refused, alert stays, no switch), opening the add form while an edit is dirty (edit commits first), Cancel discarding with no commit and no other form opening, a pending delete confirmation silently closing (not executing) when a different form opens, an invariant check that at most one sheet-rule form is ever rendered across a sequence of opens, and the editing card's own CSS class.

This makes the icon/label spacing independent of source whitespace, the exact root cause confirmed by investigation: two markup forms coexisted (a literal space in the Razor source vs. an icon inside an `@if`/`else` block followed by the label on the next line, which renders **no** space at all) — `add-block-field-button` (collé) vs `add-unconditional-colonne-button` (espacé) was the pair Simon pointed at. Values are **stored trimmed** — required so a validated-as-unique pair doesn't get persisted as two visibly-different strings. 6 new `DomainErrorCode` members (`ImportProfile_EmptyTableauName`/`TableauNameTooLong`/`DuplicateTableauName` + the 3 `..._ApplicationName` equivalents) with EN/FR `DomainErrorMessages.resx` entries, verified actually localized (not falling back to the raw key) via a new `DomainErrorMessagesImportProfileListItemLocalizationTests.cs`, same real-`.resx`-backed idiom as `DomainErrorMessagesHeaderRuleLocalizationTests`. **The core design point of the sub-ticket**: `ImportProfile.ValidateDefaultTableauName(candidate, existing)`/`ValidateDefaultApplicationName(candidate, existing)` are `public static` — a single validation path, called both by the constructor (in a loop, building the trimmed list) and by the Blazor UI (59.2) — never duplicated logic between the two. `ImportProfile` stays fully immutable (no `Add...` method introduced). - **Lot 062 (numéro de version et date de publication dans la sidebar, done — 2026-07-30, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-062-version-application-sidebar.md`)**: client asked to see, without asking Simon, which build is actually deployed — with an explicit constraint of zero manual version-bump gesture.
  - **62.1 (`ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.csproj`, `version.txt`)**: a committed `version.txt` (plain integer, starts at `0`) is incremented by a custom MSBuild target on `Publish` only, never on a plain `dotnet build`/F5. **Empirically confirmed, not assumed, that `BeforeTargets="Publish"` (the ticket's own literal suggestion) fires too late**: built a throwaway probe MSBuild project proving a target's own `DependsOnTargets` always execute before any target hooked via `BeforeTargets` on it — since `Publish`'s `DependsOnTargets` chain already runs `Build` (and therefore `GenerateAssemblyInfo`/`CoreCompile`, which bakes `Version`/`AssemblyMetadata` into the compiled DLL) before a `BeforeTargets="Publish"` hook would ever run, that hook point cannot affect the compiled assembly's own embedded version. Hooked on `BeforeTargets="BeforeBuild"` instead (the standard early extension point `Build`'s own dependency chain runs before `CoreCompile`), gated `Condition="'$(_IsPublishing)' == 'true'"` — a global MSBuild property confirmed (via a `--verbosity diagnostic` publish run) to be passed automatically by `dotnet publish` itself on the command line, never by `dotnet build`. Sets `$(Version)`/`$(AssemblyInformationalVersion)` to `1.0.<counter>` and adds an `AssemblyMetadata(BuildDate, <UTC ISO 8601>)` item, both only under that condition. **Verified end-to-end against a real local `dotnet publish`, twice in a row**: `version.txt` 0→1→2 (exactly one increment per publish, confirmed via the generated `obj/Release/net10.0/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.AssemblyInfo.cs`, which shows `AssemblyVersionAttribute("1.0.1.0")`/`AssemblyInformationalVersionAttribute("1.0.1+<commit sha>")`/`AssemblyMetadata("BuildDate", "2026-07-30T16:49:02...")` on the first run), and a subsequent plain `dotnet build` left `version.txt` at `2`, confirming zero impact outside Publish. `version.txt` reset to `0` after this verification, since the two test publishes weren't real deployments.
  - **62.2 (`Services/ApplicationBuildInfo.cs`, plain class, no `Application`-layer abstraction — a presentation detail, not business logic, per the ticket's own explicit decision)**: reads `Assembly.GetExecutingAssembly()` once, `AddSingleton` in `Program.cs`. `Version` prefers `AssemblyInformationalVersionAttribute`, trimmed at the first `+` (the SourceLink/deterministic-build commit-hash suffix observed during 62.1's own verification run — not meaningful to a non-technical client), falling back to `AssemblyName.Version.ToString()` when the attribute is absent (a plain dev build). `BuildDateUtc` reads the `AssemblyMetadata("BuildDate", ...)` item and returns `null` (no exception) when absent — the normal case for any build that didn't go through the 62.1 target. Internal constructor taking an `Assembly` exists purely for tests (`InternalsVisibleTo`), which build fixture assemblies dynamically via `AssemblyBuilder`/`CustomAttributeBuilder` rather than depending on how the test project itself happens to be built.
  - **62.3 (`NavMenu.razor`)**: `<div id="sidebar-version-info" class="sidebar-version-info">v@(BuildInfo.Version)</div>` (plus the formatted date when present) becomes the new last child of `nav.nav.flex-column`, right after `.sidebar-logo-container` (Lot 061) — outside every `<AuthorizeView>`, same always-visible rule as the logo. - **Lot 064 (heures de logs affichées en heure locale du navigateur, done — 2026-09-01, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-064-heure-locale-logs.md`)**: Simon reported a real production/client-timezone mismatch on `/logs` (`SystemLogs.TimeStamp`, written by the Serilog `MSSqlServer` sink, silently held the production server's own local wall-clock time despite the column already being read into a property misleadingly named `TimestampUtc`). **Client decision, non-negotiable**: no server/appsettings time-zone configuration of any kind — conversion happens entirely client-side, using the browser's own already-known time zone.
  - **64.0 confirmed** (README of the installed `Serilog.Sinks.MSSqlServer` 10.0.0 package, not a live production read — none was available this session): the sink's `ColumnOptions.TimeStamp.ConvertToUtc` defaults to `false`, so it writes the log-issuing host's own local time with no offset, indistinguishable from UTC in the column. **Scope-widening question put to Simon** (grep of `src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/` for every other raw-timestamp display, per this project's established "grep first, then scope" convention) — confirmed to extend beyond `Logs.razor` to `GeneratedFiles.razor` (`GeneratedAtUtc`) and `Home.razor` (`lastGenerationAtUtc`); `ApplicationBuildInfo.BuildDateUtc` (`NavMenu.razor`/`Home.razor`'s build/publish date) was named but **not** included — a different kind of fact (deployment metadata, not an incident timestamp) and Simon's answer didn't select it.
  - **64.1 (`SerilogHostLoggingExtensions.cs`)**: `WriteTo.MSSqlServer(...)` now passes an explicit `columnOptions` built by a new public `BuildSystemLogsColumnOptions()` (`TimeStamp.ConvertToUtc = true`) — extracted into its own method specifically so it's assertable by a test without opening a real SQL Server connection (the property only takes effect inside the sink's own SQL writer at write time, never on the shared `LogEvent` visible to other sinks, so the existing `CapturingSink`-based test pattern in this file can't observe it directly — same "assert the declared config" precedent as `ApplicationIdentityDbContextModelTests`, Lot 050). `tests/ExcelETL.Hosting.Tests/SerilogHostLoggingExtensionsTests.cs`: 7/7 green (was 6, +1), the 6 pre-existing tests unmodified.
  - **64.2 (BlazorAdmin)**: new `wwwroot/js/localTime.js` (plain global script, loaded in `App.razor`'s `<head>` alongside `theme.js` — no ordering requirement, unlike `theme.js`'s own pre-paint constraint) exposes `window.amOxoLocalTime.format(isoUtc, pattern)`/`.formatMany(isoUtcValues, pattern)`, built on `Intl.DateTimeFormat(undefined, ...).formatToParts(...)` (browser's own detected time zone, never sent from Blazor); `pattern` supports only the literal tokens `yyyy`/`MM`/`dd`/`HH`/`mm`/`ss` this app's own date-formatting call sites already use. New `Services/ILocalTimeFormatter.cs`/`LocalTimeFormatter.cs` (`AddScoped`, wraps `IJSRuntime`) is the one call site that actually invokes it — `FormatAsync` for a single value (Home), `FormatManyAsync` for a whole table in one round trip (Logs/GeneratedFiles). **Forces `DateTimeKind.Utc` before `.ToString("o")`**: an ISO 8601 string with no `Z`/offset suffix is parsed by the browser's own `new Date(...)` as *local* time, not UTC — silently defeating the entire conversion — so this is done in the formatter itself, never trusted from the caller (EF Core/plain `DateTime` values routinely arrive `DateTimeKind.Unspecified`).
  - Each of the 3 pages (`Logs.razor`, `GeneratedFiles.razor`, `Home.razor`) calls `ILocalTimeFormatter` from its own `OnAfterRenderAsync(firstRender)`, guarded by `RendererInfo.IsInteractive` (JS interop throws during static prerendering — same guard already established by `PasswordChangeGuard.razor`, Lot 045) and a per-visible-ids/per-value cache to avoid a JS-call → `StateHasChanged` → render → `OnAfterRenderAsync` loop. Until the conversion resolves (during prerendering, or before the first interactive `OnAfterRenderAsync`), each cell falls back to the pre-Lot-064 raw-UTC-formatted string — a brief, documented flash, never a blank cell. Stable ids added for test/future reference: `#log-timestamp-{entry.Id}` (Logs), `#generated-file-date-{record.Id}`/`-card-{record.Id}` (GeneratedFiles, table + mobile card), `#home-kpi-last-generation-value` (Home). `Logs.razor`'s column header resx key renamed `Logs_TimeUtc` → `Logs_TimeLocal` ("Time (UTC)" → "Time"/"Heure (UTC)" → "Heure") — the old label would have been actively misleading once the cell shows local time.
  - **bUnit gotcha hit while testing**: `ComponentBase.RendererInfo` throws `Bunit.Rendering.MissingRendererInfoException` the instant any code path accesses it (not just when checking `IsInteractive`) unless `SetRendererInfo(...)` has been called on the test context first — every one of `LogsTests`/`GeneratedFilesTests`/`HomeTests`' constructors now calls `SetRendererInfo(new RendererInfo("Static", isInteractive: false))` as its last statement (mirroring `MainLayoutTests`/`PasswordChangeGuardTests`' own documented ordering: register every default DI service *before* `SetRendererInfo`, which itself resolves a service internally and locks the bUnit container against any further `Services.Add*` call). `HomeTests.cs`'s one test that swaps in a custom `ApplicationBuildInfo` built from a dynamic fixture assembly (`MobileBrand_RendersVersionInfo_WithATooltipWhenBuildDateKnown`, predates this lot) could no longer re-register that service post-construction once the constructor-level lock was added — fixed by registering `ApplicationBuildInfo` via a mutable `Func<ApplicationBuildInfo> _buildInfoFactory` field read lazily inside the `AddSingleton` factory delegate, so the test can swap the *field* (not the DI registration) right before rendering.
  - New `tests/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests/Services/LocalTimeFormatterTests.cs` (`Mock<IJSRuntime>`, 2 facts) is the one test proving the JS interop call actually receives a genuine ISO 8601 UTC string (ending in `Z`), not a pre-formatted or ambiguous one — the ticket's own explicit requirement. Each of `LogsTests`/`GeneratedFilesTests`/`HomeTests` gained 2 facts: stable-id + UTC-fallback under bUnit's non-interactive default, and update-to-the-`ILocalTimeFormatter`-stub-result once `SetRendererInfo(..., isInteractive: true)` is set. Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 960/960 green (was 952, +8); full solution build green.
  - **Guide de déploiement** (`docs/conventions/guide-deploiement-am-oxo-etl-windows-server.md`, §6): gained one manual-verification checklist line ("l'heure affichée correspond à l'heure locale du poste client, pas à celle du serveur") — the ticket's own explicitly-optional discretionary addition, since bUnit cannot verify the actual browser-side time-zone conversion (no real browser in that test harness).
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: any page beyond the 3 confirmed with Simon (`ApplicationBuildInfo.BuildDateUtc` explicitly excluded); retroactively correcting already-written `SystemLogs` rows (no data migration — decision acted, future writes only); any server/appsettings time-zone configuration (explicitly ruled out); the Serilog `Console` sink's own display format (untouched, only the `MSSqlServer` sink's `ColumnOptions` changed).
  - **64.3 (correctif, incident production, done — 2026-09-01)**: Simon reported timestamps displaying in the future on `/logs` on the real production server (dedicated to a New Caledonia client, Windows time zone manually configured to UTC+11, NTP-synced) — evidence: last log entry showed `2026-09-02 01:13` while the server's own system clock read `2026-09-01 23:23` local. **This server time zone is not an anomaly** — a dedicated per-client production server in a non-French time zone is a normal, recurring deployment shape for this app (Alpha Maintenance hosts from France, but each deployment can target a different client in a different zone); no Windows configuration change was recommended or made. **Investigation, by decompiling the real installed packages (`ilspycmd` against `Serilog.Sinks.MSSqlServer` 10.0.0 and `Serilog.Extensions.Logging` 10.0.0 in this repo's own NuGet cache), not assumption**: `SerilogLogger.PrepareWrite` sources every `LogEvent.Timestamp` from `DateTimeOffset.Now` (offset-aware, correct for any server zone); `StandardColumnDataGenerator.GetTimeStampStandardColumnNameAndValue`, when `ConvertToUtc == true` (64.1's own fix), computes `logEvent.Timestamp.ToUniversalTime().UtcDateTime` — and `TimeStamp.DataType` is never overridden by `BuildSystemLogsColumnOptions()`, so it stays the vendor's own default `SqlDbType.DateTime` (confirmed by decompiling `ColumnOptions.TimeStampColumnOptions..ctor`), which is exactly the branch that produces a plain UTC `DateTime`, not a `DateTimeOffset`. **Conclusion: the current source code (commit `df5929f`) is already mathematically correct for any server offset — no residual bug found in 64.1 or 64.2.** The observed incident's arithmetic (23:13 NC-local + 2h French-browser-offset = 01:13 next day) matches *exactly* what the pre-`df5929f` behavior (no `ConvertToUtc` at all, stacked with 64.2's browser conversion) would produce — strongly suggesting the observed row/binary on that specific server predated the fix's actual republication, not a defect in the code as it exists today. No production code change was made as a result. Hardening added instead, `tests/ExcelETL.Hosting.Tests/SerilogHostLoggingExtensionsTests.cs` (12/12 green, was 7): `TimeStampConversion_GivenAnyServerTimeZoneOffset_NeverProducesAnInstantLaterThanUtcNow` (`[Theory]`, 4 offsets including the real +11:00 incident, a negative offset, UTC itself, and a non-integer-hour offset like India's — the explicit timezone-independence invariant the ticket asked for, deliberately never assuming the offset a production deployment might use) and `BuildSystemLogsColumnOptions_TimeStampColumnStaysPlainDateTime_NotDateTimeOffset` (pins the vendor default this fix's correctness implicitly depends on). **Testability limit documented, not closed with an unrequested mechanism, per explicit instruction**: no injectable clock abstraction exists anywhere in this pipeline (`LogEvent.Timestamp` comes straight from the static BCL call `DateTimeOffset.Now`), and this repo's own established convention keeps every test in this file off a real SQL Server connection — so a true end-to-end (real sink write → real read-back) test was not added. **Discovered but explicitly out of scope, not fixed**: `Logs.razor`'s "Aujourd'hui"/"Hier" filter compares `TimestampUtc.Date` against `DateTime.UtcNow.Date` — a UTC-boundary day comparison, not the viewer's own local day boundary; a distinct filtering defect from the timestamp-display incident investigated here, not proven to share 64.2's cause, so left untouched per the ticket's own scope-discipline instruction (flagged in the ticket doc for a future lot).
  - **Follow-up, same day (client feedback on the real deployed sidebar, after 64.3's fix confirmed logs now show the correct local time): "Publié le mardi 1 septembre 2026, 12:57" for a publish that happened at 14:57 (French local time)** — the exact same UTC-vs-local class of bug 64.1/64.2 fixed for logs, but on a display Lot 064's own 64.0 scope arbitration had explicitly excluded (`ApplicationBuildInfo.BuildDateUtc`, "a different kind of fact — deployment metadata, not an incident timestamp"). Reopened on Simon's explicit request (AskUserQuestion) rather than silently extended. **Root cause**: `NavMenu.razor`'s/`Home.razor`'s `BuildDateTooltip` computed property formatted `BuildInfo.BuildDateUtc` directly with `CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture` (only localizing the day/month *names*) but never converted the clock value itself — 12:57 UTC displayed as if it were already local. **Fix**: both components now also inject `ILocalTimeFormatter` and, from `OnAfterRenderAsync` (guarded by `RendererInfo.IsInteractive`, same convention as the rest of Lot 064), call `FormatAsync(buildDateUtc, "yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm")` — the only numeric pattern the existing `localTime.js` supports — then `DateTime.TryParseExact` the browser-local numeric result back into a plain `DateTime` so `CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture` can still render the day/month names correctly (`ILocalTimeFormatter` has no day/month-name-aware API, so this round-trip-through-a-parseable-string is the mechanism, not a new interface method). Both the compact label (`dd/MM/yyyy`) and the detailed tooltip fall back to the pre-fix raw-UTC values until the conversion resolves (or forever, under non-interactive rendering), same "brief documented flash, never blank" convention as the rest of Lot 064. `Home.razor`'s `OnAfterRenderAsync` was restructured so its two independent conversions (last-generation KPI, build-date footer) each fire on their own condition rather than the first one's early-return silently starving the second (a page with no generated file yet must still get its build date converted). No new resx keys — reuses `NavMenu_BuildDateTooltip` verbatim on both pages, unchanged. **Test-infrastructure fallout, not behavior changes**: `NavMenuTests.cs` — NavMenu's own internal `<AuthorizeView>` blocks mean every one of its 49 pre-existing tests calls `this.AddAuthorization()` *inside* the test body (registering services), which conflicts with `SetRendererInfo` (now needed for the new `RendererInfo.IsInteractive` access) locking the bUnit container the instant it runs — the established "`SetRendererInfo` in the constructor" pattern used elsewhere in this codebase does not work for this component. Fixed via a new `RenderNavMenu(bool isInteractive = false)` test helper that calls `SetRendererInfo` immediately before `Render<NavMenu>()`, always after that test's own `AddAuthorization()` call — mechanically replaces all 47 pre-existing `Render<NavMenu>()` call sites (plus one `Render<NavMenuWithBackLink>()` site needing its own inline `SetRendererInfo` call, since it renders a different wrapper type). `MainLayoutTests.cs` (renders `NavMenu` transitively) needed `Services.AddSingleton(Mock.Of<ILocalTimeFormatter>())` added before its own pre-existing `SetRendererInfo` call. 2 new tests (`NavMenuTests.NavMenu_VersionInfo_UpdatesLabelAndTooltip_ToLocalTimeFormatterResult_OnceInteractive`, `HomeTests.MobileBrand_VersionInfo_UpdatesLabelAndTooltip_ToLocalTimeFormatterResult_OnceInteractive`) both stub a day-crossing conversion (23:49 UTC → 01:49 local, next day) specifically so the label change (not just the tooltip) proves genuine conversion happened, not a re-render of the same UTC value under a new name. Every one of the 3 pre-existing build-date tests in each file (known/absent/tooltip-EN/tooltip-FR) needed zero assertion changes — none of them turn on interactivity, so they keep exercising the unchanged raw-UTC fallback path. Full `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests` suite: 965/965 green (net +2 vs. immediately before this follow-up — the `RenderNavMenu()` rename touched existing call sites only, no test count change from that; +2 is the two new interactive-conversion facts). Full solution build green.
- **Lot 065 (détail d'erreur enrichi sur `/api-test`, done — 2026-09-01, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-065-detail-erreur-api-test.md`)**: closes a real client-reported gap — a technical failure on `/api-test` (the case that triggered this ticket: `UnknownFieldReferenceException`) only ever showed the fixed generic `ApiTest_TechnicalErrorMessage` string, forcing Simon to go dig through server logs separately. **Client decisions (non-negotiable)**: no correlation mechanism (no correlation ID, no a posteriori `SystemLogs` query from BlazorAdmin — KISS); the detail comes directly from the WebAPI's own HTTP response, never a secondary source; content is the exception's short type name + its message, **never** the stack trace; the contract is the same for every caller (M2M or `/api-test`) — no conditional behavior by origin.
  - **65.0 investigation**: `GlobalExceptionHandler` (`src/ExcelETL.WebAPI/ExceptionHandling/`, an `IExceptionHandler` registered via `AddExceptionHandler<T>()` + `AddProblemDetails()`) already existed and already handles every business exception carrying a resource key (`BusinessExceptionLocalizer.TryLocalize`, `IHasDomainErrorCode`/`IHasApplicationErrorCode`) — Lot 036's own 400/422 mappings live entirely inside `OxoController` itself (built and returned directly, never reaching this handler) and are untouched by this lot. For anything without a resource key, the handler used to `return false`, falling through to ASP.NET Core's own default `AddProblemDetails()` fallback — confirmed by reading the code (not a live prod read) that this produces a generic `ProblemDetails` with no `Detail` at all, exactly matching what Simon observed. `OxoApiTestClient.ProcessAsync` (`src/ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin/Services/`) already reads the response body for `NotFound`/`UnprocessableEntity` via a pre-existing `TryReadProblemDetailsAsync`/private `ProblemDetailsBody` pattern (Lot 038) — the `default:` switch arm (covering 500) discarded the body entirely, going straight to `OxoApiTestResult.TechnicalError(statusCode)` with no message.
  - **65.1 (WebAPI)**: `GlobalExceptionHandler.TryHandleAsync` no longer returns `false` for an unmapped exception — it always handles it now, writing a `ProblemDetails` with `Status = 500` and `Extensions["exceptionType"]`/`Extensions["exceptionMessage"]` set to `exception.GetType().Name`/`exception.Message` (never `Detail`, which stays reserved for a genuine localized business message) and logging at `Error` (was previously never logged at all by this handler for this case, since it never ran). Mapped business exceptions are completely unaffected — same `Detail`, same `StatusCodeFor` switch, same `Warning`-level log. New `OxoProcessEndpointTests.cs` tests (`WebApplicationFactory`, a substituted `Mock<IProcessOxoFileService>` throwing a plain `InvalidOperationException` rather than trying to reproduce the real `UnknownFieldReferenceException` case byte-for-byte — simpler and sufficient per the ticket's own "arbitrary exception" option): response body contains the exception type name and message; a dedicated anti-stack-trace guard-rail asserts the body never contains the literal `"   at "` substring nor any `StackTrace`-named property, even for an exception thrown from a real call stack (not just a bare `new`); a non-regression test on Lot 036's `ExportProfileId`-missing 400 confirms its body is unchanged and carries neither `exceptionType` nor `exceptionMessage`. `ExcelETL.WebAPI.Tests`: 28/28 green (was 25, +3).
  - **65.2 (BlazorAdmin)**: `OxoApiTestClient.ProcessAsync` gained a dedicated `case HttpStatusCode.InternalServerError` (previously falling into `default:`) that reads the body via the same `TryReadProblemDetailsAsync` helper (now with `ExceptionType`/`ExceptionMessage` added to `ProblemDetailsBody`, matched case-insensitively against the JSON `exceptionType`/`exceptionMessage` extension keys the way `Detail`/`Errors` already were) and passes both through to `OxoApiTestResult.TechnicalError(statusCode, exceptionType, exceptionMessage)` (both now-optional parameters, default `null`). `ApiTest.razor`'s `TechnicalError` case renders `#api-test-technical-error-detail` (new stable id, `ApiTest_TechnicalErrorDetail` resx key, `"Technical error: {0} -- {1}"`/`"Erreur technique : {0} -- {1}"`) when `ExceptionType is not null`, replacing the generic message entirely for that case; the **repli explicite** (empty/non-parsable body, e.g. a genuine connection-adjacent 500 with no body) leaves `ExceptionType` null and the pre-existing `#api-test-result`/`ApiTest_TechnicalErrorMessage` fallback renders unchanged — no exception thrown client-side either way, `TryReadProblemDetailsAsync`'s existing `catch (JsonException)` already covered this. New tests: 2 in `OxoApiTestClientTests.cs` (enriched 500 body → both fields populated; empty-body 500 → both null, non-regression of the pre-existing `[Theory]` covering `BadRequest`/`InternalServerError` with no body), 1 in `ApiTestTests.cs` (`ProcessButton_Click_WithTechnicalErrorCarryingExceptionDetail_RendersTypeAndMessage`, selecting by the new stable id, not text) — the pre-existing `ProcessButton_Click_WithTechnicalError_RendersGenericMessage_NoRawDetail` test (asserting the old generic-message behavior for a bare `TechnicalError(500)` with no detail) needed **zero changes**, since a `null` `ExceptionType` still takes the `else` branch. `ExcelETL.BlazorAdmin.Tests`: 963/963 green (was 960, +3).
  - **Commits kept isolated per the ticket's own explicit instruction** (65.1 WebAPI, 65.2 BlazorAdmin, separate commits) — matches the established Lot 036 precedent.
  - **Not verified in a real browser this session** (no running dev server/admin credentials available, same recurring limitation as most prior lots) — confidence rests on the full green suites on both sides (real HTTP `WebApplicationFactory` test on the WebAPI side, bUnit + a fake-HTTP-handler test on the BlazorAdmin side).
  - **Hors périmètre, per the ticket**: any correlation mechanism between an `/api-test` call and its `SystemLogs` entries (explicitly ruled out); including the stack trace in any form (explicitly ruled out); changing the HTTP status code for an unmapped exception (stays 500, not reopened); fixing the real `UnknownFieldReferenceException`/`HasZeroEnergie` defect that triggered this report (a separate, likely WebAPI/BlazorAdmin deployment-skew issue, tracked apart from this lot); any change to the already-explicitly-mapped Lot 036 400/422 response shapes.

Root cause: `UnknownFieldReferenceException` (the real exception behind this report) **is** a mapped business exception (`IHasApplicationErrorCode`) — `BusinessExceptionLocalizer.TryLocalize` returns non-null for it (a raw, unlocalized resource-key string, since `ApplicationMessages.resx` has zero entries for this exception's `ApplicationErrorCode` member — a distinct, pre-existing localization gap, not fixed here), so `GlobalExceptionHandler` took the "mapped" branch and never populated the `exceptionType`/`exceptionMessage` extensions — those were only ever added in the fully-unmapped branch. `StatusCodeFor` has no explicit case for this exception type either, so it still resolved to 500 — indistinguishable, from `ApiTest.razor`'s perspective, from the pre-Lot-065 behavior. **Fixed**: the extensions are now attached whenever the *final* status is 500, regardless of which branch produced it (mapped-with-no-explicit-case, or fully unmapped) — `Detail` (the localized/raw-key business message) is untouched, extensions are additive. New `Process_WhenAMappedExceptionHasNoExplicitStatusCodeCase_StillReturnsExceptionTypeAndMessage` test reproduces the exact real scenario (`UnknownFieldReferenceException("HasZeroEnergie")` thrown by a substituted `IProcessOxoFileService`) rather than an arbitrary exception. `ExcelETL.WebAPI.Tests`: 29/29 green (was 28, +1). No BlazorAdmin change needed — `OxoApiTestClient`/`ApiTest.razor` already read the extensions correctly, they just were never populated for this case.
- **Lot 066 (complétion des colonnes cibles `Parents`/`Enfants` du profil d'export par défaut, done — 2026-09-03, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-066-completion-colonnes-parents-enfants-export.md`)**: closes a real column-parity gap found by comparing a real export against the client's own (non-authoritative, manually-edited) `OXO_TRAME_IMPORT_MAD.xlsx`. 66.0→66.5 implemented in the ticket's own recommended order.
  - **66.0 investigation found decision 4 factually wrong, corrected live with the user before implementing**: the ticket assumed `Enfants`' bare `"ZÉRO ENERGIE EN PRESENCE EE"` (no `(PS941)` suffix) PointColumnDefinition was an accidental export-side duplicate matching no real extraction — but DIVERS' own `ConditionalPointRule` (`TypeElement == "ZERO ENERGIE"`) genuinely produces that exact Colonne, independently of ISOLEMENT's `HasZeroEnergie`-driven `"...(PS941)"` variant (Lot 063) — confirmed against D8570 (13 real occurrences, `DiversExtractionServiceIntegrationTests`). Flagged via `AskUserQuestion`; decided: **merge the two columns** — DIVERS' `ConditionalPointRule` target retargeted onto ISOLEMENT's own `"(PS941)"` Colonne name (`DefaultProfileSeeder.BuildDefaultImportProfile`'s DIVERS rule), so both sheets converge onto the one real Colonne and a single `PointColumnDefinition` covers both — documented inline in the ticket doc and in the seeder's own code comments.
  - **66.1**: `"TRAVAUX COMPLET"`/`"TRAVAUX DETAIL"` `PointColumnDefinition`s dropped from `Parents` (redundant with `"Tableaux"`, decision 2) — `TravauxCompletColonneName`/`TravauxDetailColonneName` consts stay, still used for `ImportProfile.DefaultTableaux`. `Enfants`' bare `"ZÉRO ENERGIE EN PRESENCE EE"` entry is gone too (superseded by the DIVERS retarget above, 17→16 `PointColumnDefinition`s).
  - **66.2**: 7 (`Parents`)/11 (`Enfants`) new unmapped identity `ColumnDefinition(header, Source: null)`s (decision 6 — a legitimately empty cell, same `I1` precedent `GenerationPipelineIntegrationTests`' own "Fluide"/"Commentaires" approximation already established). Positioned per the ticket's own (explicitly non-blocking) `OXO_TRAME_IMPORT_MAD.xlsx`-derived ordering, with one **documented deviation**: `"SUPPRESSION"`/`"ADR Email"`/`"COMMENTAIRES"` (Parents) and `"SUPPRESSION"` (Enfants) were asked to sit "after PROGRESS" — impossible to express as-is, since `SheetGenerationEngine.GenerateSheet` always renders every `ColumnDefinitions` header before every `ApplicationColumnDefinitions` header regardless of list order — placed at the end of the descriptive-columns block instead, per the ticket's own fallback instruction.
  - **66.3 (`SheetGenerationEngine`, new engine capability)**: an Equipement-sourced sheet's Point column is now marked either by the historical direct mechanism (`PointPivot.ParentRepere == EquipementPivot.Repere`, unchanged) **or** by aggregation — at least one `IsolementPivot` of the run with `RepereParent == EquipementPivot.Repere` carries a matching Point (`HasPointForEquipement`, new private method). ColonneNom compared trimmed + case-insensitive for the aggregated path only (spec §7 convention), the direct path stays exact per the ticket's own non-regression requirement. `ImportResult` already carried both `Isolements` and `Points` at the call site the engine needed — no interface/signature change to `ISheetGenerationEngine`/`ExportProfile`/`SheetGenerationRule` was required, confirmed at investigation before writing any code. 8 new `SheetGenerationEngineTests` facts (at-least-one aggregation, "at least one" not "all", no match, defensive `RepereParent` mismatch, trim/case-insensitive ColonneNom, direct-and-aggregated-coexist non-regression, isolement-row non-regression).
  - **66.4**: the same 16 `PointColumnDefinition`s now live on both `Parents` and `Enfants`, in the same order, marked on `Parents` via 66.3's aggregation and on `Enfants` exactly as before. **Real EF Core owned-collection bug found and fixed while implementing this**: a single `static readonly` list shared by reference between both `SheetGenerationRule`s (an initial, more DRY-looking attempt) silently orphaned `Parents`' entire `PointColumnDefinitions` collection on `SaveChangesAsync` — EF Core's change tracker cannot have the exact same owned-entity object instances be owned by two different owning rows at once. Fixed by making it a factory *method* (`BuildIsolementStylePointColumnDefinitions()`, returning fresh instances on each call) invoked separately for each rule — same "factory method, not a shared instance" precedent `BuildTacheMultipleSheetRule` already established for the identical reason.
  - **66.5**: 2 new closing integration tests (D8570/G6306B; C7401's own equivalent, predating this lot, was rewritten in place — see below) in `DefaultProfileSeederPipelineIntegrationTests`, each verifying in one place: `Parents`/`Enfants`' full header (resolved **dynamically from the profile itself**, per the ticket's own explicit instruction to avoid a manual-counting divergence risk once each sheet carries ~30 columns — never a hardcoded numeric column-count), at least one data row with unmapped identity columns genuinely empty, and at least one Point column correctly aggregated (66.3) onto `Parents` from a real child isolement (D8570: `"(PS941)"` via DIVERS' 13 `ZERO ENERGIE` rows; G6306B: `"SYNCHRONISATION INSTRUMENTATION"` via DIVERS' 1 `INSTRUMENTATION` row, alongside a non-match guard for the `"POINT DE FEU"`/`"POINT FEU"` spelling mismatch already known from Lot 055). C7401's own pre-existing `Generate_C7401Fixture_WithSeededProfiles_ProducesExpectedHeaderOrderAndNewColumnValues` (Lot U) was rewritten the same way (dynamic header/column-index resolution by name) rather than hand-patched with new hardcoded indices, since every column index shifted.

- **Lot 067 (`Repère TM`/`TYPE ELEMENT CODE`/`Colonne Travaux` on the TM_PROC_* export sheets, done — 2026-09-03, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-067-tache-multiple-repere-type-colonne-travaux.md`)**: closes a real legacy-app need discussed with Simon — a task-multiple row exported today carries no repère/type link back to its base element, and no target column to write into on the legacy side. 67.1→67.5 implemented in the ticket's own recommended order.
  - **Decisions taken with Simon before implementation, all recorded in the ticket doc**: `TacheMultiplePivot` gains `Repere`/`TypeElementNom` (diffused from the run's single Equipement, same broadcast mechanism as `IsolementPivot`'s own broadcast properties) and `ColonneTravaux`. `ColonneTravaux` is **not** a hardcoded engine switch — client explicitly asked for it to stay configurable (a future client may use other legacy column names) — so it's resolved per-tache from a new profile-level configuration, `ImportProfile.TacheMultipleTypeLabels` (a list of `(Code, Label)` pairs, e.g. `("TM_PROC_MAD", "Procédure MAD")`), by looking up the tache's own `TypeTacheMultipleCode` (trim + `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, spec §7 convention) — blank, never an error, when no entry matches.
  - **67.1 (Domain, `TacheMultipleTypeLabel` + `ImportProfile.TacheMultipleTypeLabels`)**: new `sealed record TacheMultipleTypeLabel(Code, Label)` (`Extraction/Profile/`, own constructor validation, reuses `ImportProfile.MaxListItemNameLength` — no new length constant). `ImportProfile` gained `TacheMultipleTypeLabels` as the **last, optional** parameter (`= null`, normalized to `[]`) on all 3 constructors — a deliberate deviation from `DefaultTableaux`/`DefaultApplicationNames`'s own "no magic default" rule, documented inline: those two are required specifically because an accidental empty value would silently break a load-bearing extraction behavior (PROCEDURE's own unconditional Points), while an empty `TacheMultipleTypeLabels` list is a legitimate, client-confirmed default (every "Colonne Travaux" cell stays blank) — making it required would have forced touching ~72 pre-existing `new ImportProfile(...)` call sites for no safety benefit. Duplicate-`Code` detection (trim + case-insensitive) extracted into a new reusable `ImportProfile.ValidateTacheMultipleTypeLabelCode(candidate, existing)` static method (mirrors `ValidateDefaultTableauName`'s own "one validation path, shared by the constructor and the UI" rationale) — refactored out of an inline constructor loop once 67.5's UI needed the same check. EF Core: `OwnsMany` directly on `ImportProfile` (own table `ImportProfileTacheMultipleTypeLabels`, shadow `int Id`, `Code`/`Label` both `HasMaxLength(MaxListItemNameLength)`) — a genuine owned-entity mapping, unlike `DefaultTableaux`/`DefaultApplicationNames`'s primitive-collection JSON columns, since a `Code`+`Label` pair isn't a primitive scalar. Migration `20260903203705_AddTacheMultipleTypeLabelToImportProfile`, generated via `dotnet ef migrations add` (WebAPI as startup project, same convention as Lots 047/063) — not yet applied to any real SQL Server database. 5 new `DomainErrorCode` members + `.resx` entries (EN/FR), verified via the existing `DomainErrorMessagesImportProfileListItemLocalizationTests` (extended, not duplicated).
  - **67.2 (Domain, `TacheMultiplePivot`/`PivotFieldRef`/`PivotFieldResolver`)**: `TacheMultiplePivot` gains 3 `init` properties (`Repere`/`TypeElementNom`/`ColonneTravaux`, all default `""`, set in the constructor body — not property initializers, matching `IsolementPivot`'s own broadcast-property convention exactly) — no validation, an empty string is a legitimate state throughout. `PivotFieldRef` gains `TacheMultipleRepere`/`TacheMultipleTypeElementNom`/`TacheMultipleColonneTravaux`; `PivotFieldResolver` reads them straight off the pivot, no logic — the actual "Colonne Travaux" resolution lives entirely in the orchestrator (67.3), never here.
  - **67.3 (`ImportPipelineOrchestrator`)**: new `BroadcastTachesMultiplesContext` (symmetric to the pre-existing `BroadcastEquipementContext`) replaces `procedureResult.TachesMultiples` with a diffused list before it reaches `ImportResult` — `Repere`/`TypeElementNom` from the run's Equipement, `ColonneTravaux` via a new `ResolveColonneTravaux(code, tacheMultipleTypeLabels)` lookup helper.
  - **67.4 (`DefaultProfileSeeder`)**: seeded import profile gains `TacheMultipleTypeLabels = [("TM_PROC_MAD", "Procédure MAD"), ("TM_PROC_REL", "Procédure REL")]` — the exact values discussed with Simon, now configuration data, no longer a hardcoded switch anywhere in the generation engine. `BuildTacheMultipleSheetRule` gains 3 `ColumnDefinition`s: `"Repère TM"`/`"TYPE ELEMENT CODE"` lead (same position convention as Repère/Type Elément on `Parents`/`Enfants`), `"Colonne Travaux"` trails — since this one `SheetGenerationRule` drives both dynamically-generated `TM_PROC_MAD`/`TM_PROC_REL` physical sheets, adding the columns here applies to both automatically, no engine change needed. Closing integration test (`DefaultProfileSeederPipelineIntegrationTests`, against the C7401 fixture and the profile as seeded and fetched back) confirms every row of both sheets carries the correct `Repère TM`/`TYPE ELEMENT CODE`/`Colonne Travaux` value.
  - **67.5 (`ImportProfileEditor.razor`)**: new root-level add/edit/delete section for `TacheMultipleTypeLabels`, right after Tableaux/Applications and before the Sheet Rules heading — **kept inline in the page**, not a new dedicated `XxxForm.razor` component (a deviation from the ticket doc's own original plan, made once the actual codebase convention was checked: root-level lists on this page — Tableaux/Applications — are already handled fully inline, the dedicated sub-form-component pattern is reserved for nested `SheetRuleForm` lists, a different context). Each row is a Code/Label pair (2 `form-floating` fields) instead of Tableaux/Applications' single string; validation goes through `TacheMultipleTypeLabel`'s own constructor first (catches empty/too-long) then 67.1's new `ImportProfile.ValidateTacheMultipleTypeLabelCode` (catches duplicates) — same two-stage split the Domain constructor itself uses. The list `<ul>` needed its own `id="tache-multiple-type-labels-list"` (a real bUnit gotcha hit while writing the tests: the generic `.block-field-list .block-field-item` selector also matches a sheet-rule-card's own block-field summary rows, which are **not** gated behind the R3 collapsible sublist — only `UnconditionalColonneNames`/`PointRules` are — so an unscoped selector silently over-matched in a profile that has both a sheet rule with block fields and a `TacheMultipleTypeLabels` entry).

- **PLATINES "RECEPTION DEBUT MAD"/"RECEPTION DEBUT REL" become field-presence-driven, not unconditional (done — 2026-09-04, not yet a formal ticket doc)**: closes a client screenshot report (`Dossier de MaD IDL -  G4010A.xlsx`, annotated H19/H20 on the generated `Enfants` sheet) — these 2 of PLATINES' 7 unconditional Colonnes are now created only when the source block's own `POSÉE LE`/`DÉPOSÉE LE` cell actually has a value, instead of always. **Confirmed by dumping all real fixtures before implementing (not assumed)**: 51 of 53 real PLATINES blocks across all 4 client files on disk (incl. G4010A's own 4) have both cells blank — client explicitly confirmed ("Implémenter tel quel") accepting that the checkmark disappears from nearly every existing block as a result, since that reflects the real source data rather than a display artifact. `POSÉE LE`/`DÉPOSÉE LE` labels sit in column F at block offsets +2/+3 (fixed form labels, not data); the actual value cell is column H, merged `H:N` (confirmed via `ws.MergedRanges`, not assumed from `Designation`'s own `H:V` merge) — `H` at offset+2 → "RECEPTION DEBUT MAD", `H` at offset+3 → "RECEPTION DEBUT REL". The known DEBUT/FIN block-split anomaly (spec §3, C7401's PT15A/PT15B, "jugé non fiable, sans retour attendu dessus") is read exactly as any other block — its labels happen to be scrambled between offset+2/+3 for that one case, no special handling, per the spec's own established position.
  - **New Domain primitive, `FieldPresencePointRule`** (`Extraction/Primitives/`, sibling to `ConditionalPointRule`) — deliberately distinct from `ConditionalPointRule`, which always requires a non-blank `ComparisonValue` and can't express "just has a value at all". `Cell` reuses `BlockFieldDefinition` purely as "where to read this optional cell" — deliberately never added to a `RepeatingBlockLocator.Fields` list, so it never goes through `IRepeatingBlockReader`'s required-field policy (which would otherwise report `RequiredFieldMissing` and drop the whole block on the overwhelmingly-common blank case). Needed the same private-parameterless-constructor-plus-reflection EF materialization trick as `HeaderFieldRule.Cell` (Lot 047) — a reference to an owned type, not a collection.
  - `SheetExtractionRule` gained `FieldPresencePointRules` as a **last, optional** constructor parameter (`= null`, normalized to `[]`) — same "no forced update of ~30 unrelated call sites" rationale as `ZeroEnergieExpectedValue`/`TacheMultipleTypeLabels` before it; every sheet other than PLATINES, and any PLATINES profile predating this change, gets an empty list (true no-op).
  - **`RepeatingBlockReadResult.Blocks` changed shape**: each entry is now a `RepeatingBlock(int StartRow, IReadOnlyDictionary<string, string> Fields)` record instead of a bare field dictionary — needed so `UnconditionalIsolementSheetExtractionService` can compute the optional cell's absolute range per block via the pre-existing `BlockFieldRangeCalculator`. `StartRow` can't be recomputed from a block's position within `Blocks` alone: a block dropped by a `RequiredFieldMissing` error still consumes a row, so a later surviving block's list index no longer lines up with its real row — confirmed by a dedicated `RepeatingBlockReaderTests` case (a dropped first block, second block's `StartRow` still 26, not 0-indexed). The 3 consumers of this type (`UnconditionalIsolementSheetExtractionService` — PLATINES/ORIFICES CAPACITES, `AutresJointsTouchesExtractionService`, `DiversExtractionService`) updated mechanically (`block[...]` → `block.Fields[...]`); their own tests needed zero changes (they exercise the real `RepeatingBlockReader` against `Mock<IWorkbookReader>` and only assert final Points/Isolements, never the intermediate shape).
  - **`UnconditionalIsolementSheetExtractionService`** evaluates `sheetRule.FieldPresencePointRules` per block (reading `rule.Cell` at `block.StartRow` via `workbookReader.ReadCellValue`, creating a `PointPivot` only when non-blank) — stays fully config-driven, no new dependency on `IConditionalPointRuleEvaluator`/warning-aggregation machinery (a blank cell here is the normal case, not a reportable anomaly, unlike `ConditionalPointRule`'s own `NoConditionalPointCreated` warning path).
  - **`DefaultProfileSeeder`**: PLATINES' `UnconditionalColonneNames` shrinks 7→5 (removes "RECEPTION DEBUT MAD"/"RECEPTION DEBUT REL"); gains `fieldPresencePointRules: [FieldPresencePointRule(BlockFieldDefinition("PoseeLe", "H:N", 2, 2), "RECEPTION DEBUT MAD"), FieldPresencePointRule(BlockFieldDefinition("DeposeeLe", "H:N", 3, 3), "RECEPTION DEBUT REL")]`. `DefaultProfileSeederPipelineIntegrationTests`/`GenerationPipelineIntegrationTests`/`ImportPipelineOrchestrator*IntegrationTests` needed **zero changes** — none of their assertions touch these 2 Colonnes' cell values, only `DefaultProfileSeederTests`' own root-fields assertion (`UnconditionalColonneNames.Should().HaveCount(7)` → `5`, plus new `FieldPresencePointRules` content assertions) and one cross-profile consistency test (`SeedAsync_ExportEnfantsPointColumns_AllReferenceColonneNamesTheImportProfileActuallyProduces`, whose "produced colonne names" computation now also includes `FieldPresencePointRules.Select(f => f.ColonneName)`) needed updating.
  - **EF Core**: new `OwnsMany` on `SheetExtractionRule.FieldPresencePointRules` (table `ImportProfileSheetRuleFieldPresencePointRules`, shadow `int Id`), `Cell` table-split (`OwnsOne`) with the same 4 columns (`CellName`/`CellColumnRange`/`CellRowOffsetStart`/`CellRowOffsetEnd`) as `ImportProfileSheetRuleBlockFields` uses for a plain `BlockFieldDefinition`. Migration `AddFieldPresencePointRuleToImportProfileSheetRule` — not yet applied to any real SQL Server database.
  - **Non-regression, confirmed against all 4 real fixtures, not assumed**: `PlatinesExtractionServiceIntegrationTests` gained a dedicated `CreateSheetRuleWithFieldPresenceRules()` config (kept separate from the file's own pre-existing `CreateSheetRule()`, which every pre-existing unconditional-Points test still uses unmodified) — C7401's PT15A/PT15B are the only 2 of its 15 platines to get both Points (via the scrambled-label anomaly, both cells non-blank either way); D8570 (21)/G6306B (5)/**G4010A (4, the file behind the client's own screenshot)** never create either Point, confirming the fix actually closes the reported gap.
  - **No formal ticket doc for this fix** (an in-session client remark, not yet written up as `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-*`) — flagged here so a future session doesn't assume one exists.
  - **No BlazorAdmin editing UI added for `FieldPresencePointRules`** — same deliberate deferral as Lot 047→048 (`HeaderFieldRule`/`HeaderCompositeRule`); today it's only settable by editing `DefaultProfileSeeder.cs` directly. Revisit if the client needs to reconfigure this from the admin UI.
  - Full solution: Domain 355 (was 347, +8: `FieldPresencePointRuleTests` ×6, `SheetExtractionRuleTests` +2)/Application 243 (was 236, +7: `UnconditionalIsolementSheetExtractionServiceTests` +5, `DomainErrorMessagesHeaderRuleLocalizationTests` +2 — `RepeatingBlockReaderTests`' own count unchanged, existing tests extended in place)/Infrastructure 233 (was 227, +6: `EfImportProfileStoreTests` +2, `PlatinesExtractionServiceIntegrationTests` +4 — `DefaultProfileSeederTests`' own count unchanged, 2 existing tests extended in place)/WebAPI 29 (unaffected)/Hosting 12 (unaffected)/legacy 9+15 (unaffected)/BlazorAdmin 975 (unaffected) — all green, **all 233 Infrastructure tests confirmed green including the G4010A case** (`Extract_FixturesWithNoDataInPoseeLeOrDeposeeLe_NeverCreateEitherPoint`) once Simon closed the file (the initial run in this same session hit a transient `IOException` — the file was open in LibreOffice Calc on his machine, not a code issue).
- **Lot 068 (couleur d'étiquette de la feuille PLATINES exposée en export, done — 2026-09-04, ticket doc `docs/tickets/tickets-tdd-lot-068-couleur-etiquette-platines.md`)**: new client remark (no written spec) — PLATINES carries, per block, a "couleur d'étiquette" cell (`ROUGE`/`BLEUE`/`JAUNE` observed, free text, no closed value set) that no extraction service read. Investigated first via a throwaway `ClosedXML` dump of all 4 real fixtures on disk (not assumed): the value sits at `H:N`, **block offset +1** (same row as the form's own `"ÉTIQUETTE"` label in column F, which is just the paper form's field name and is ignored) — confirmed `ORIFICES CAPACITES` (shares the same extraction service) has **no equivalent row in any fixture**, so the field stays PLATINES-only.

### Test conventions in practice
- bUnit (v2.7.2): `class FooTests : BunitContext`, `Render<Foo>()`. Examples across `Components/Pages/Admin/*`, `Account/*`, `Layout/*`.
- **A bUnit test never proves a page is reachable** (Lot 049): it renders the component directly, bypassing routing, the render mode and the HTTP pipeline. For anything involving routing/navigation/redirection, add a `WebApplicationFactory<Program>` test — see `Account/ForcePasswordChangeHttpTests.cs` / `Components/AppRenderModeHttpTests.cs` for the BlazorAdmin harness, and `docs/conventions/recommandations-tickets-tdd.md` §6 for why the HTTP request alone still isn't enough.
- HTTP-calling code is tested with a hand-rolled `FakeHttpMessageHandler`, not RichardSzalay.MockHttp — avoids a new dependency for equivalent capability. Colocated per test project (e.g. `legacy/ExcelProcessingClientService.Tests/FakeHttpMessageHandler.cs`); BlazorAdmin no longer has any HTTP-calling code of its own since Lot K4 removed `ExcelProcessingClient`.
- WebAPI endpoints are tested via `WebApplicationFactory<Program>` integration tests (e.g. `OxoProcessEndpointTests.cs`), not mocked HTTP.
- bUnit + `InputFile`: `cut.FindComponent<InputFile>()` + `InputFileContent.CreateFromText(...)` + `.UploadFiles(...)`. Note: triggering `InputFile.OnChange` in bUnit blocks synchronously until the whole async handler chain completes — a `TaskCompletionSource`-gated fake HTTP response that you only complete *after* calling `UploadFiles` will deadlock the test. Use immediately-resolved fake responses instead; the transient "Uploading" state isn't observable this way.
- bUnit auth with a specific user id (not just username/roles): `this.AddAuthorization().SetAuthorized("user@example.com")` then `.SetClaims(new Claim(ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier, "user-1"))` on the same `BunitAuthorizationContext` — `AddAuthorization()` already wires `CascadingAuthenticationState` into the render tree, so components consuming `[CascadingParameter] Task<AuthenticationState>` work without extra setup. See `ProfileTests.cs`.
- `UserRepository` is now a hybrid repository (plain EF reads via `IDbContextFactory` + `UserManager`-backed writes): its test class mixes both conventions from the top-level rule above — `GetByIdAsync`/`GetAllAsync` tests seed the real EF InMemory provider directly, `UpdateProfileAsync`/`ChangePasswordAsync` tests mock `UserManager<ApplicationUser>` via `IdentityManagerMocks.CreateUserManagerMock()`. Both fixtures live side by side in the same `UserRepositoryTests` class.

### Maintenance rule
Whenever a milestone adds new DI registrations, HTTP endpoints, config keys, JS modules, or resource files, update the relevant bullet above in the same commit. Treat this section as a cache of the exploration an agent would otherwise have to redo — keep it accurate rather than exhaustive.
